Recovery after compaction: `.claude/rules/post-compaction.md`.

## Task

Record and resolve one RFC 4271 Section 9.2 defect in the route-server relay
(`bgp-rs`). When two sources announce the same prefix and one of them
withdraws it, a route-server client that did not negotiate ADD-PATH must be
sent the remaining source's route as its new best path. Today the relay
forwards the withdrawal and nothing else, so the client loses a prefix that is
still reachable.

The route reflector (`bgp-rr`) has the same gap and is in this spec's scope
(owner reading of Q-1, recorded under Q-2; design review round 1, I4): its
per-prefix owner withdrawal is forwarded bare on the same cached rail, and its
source departure is worse, a plain withdrawal sent to every peer whatever
the destination's current owner (Design-time re-check, bgp-rr row).

The scope also covers ANNOUNCEMENTS (owner, Q-3, 2026-10-13). RFC 7947
Section 2.1 says the route server "SHOULD perform one or more BGP Decision
Processes to select routes for subsequent advertisement to its clients", and
the owner reads it as: the decision may be computed in advance or cached, but
each client must hold the route the decision process elects, otherwise the
routing is wrong. So a client that did not negotiate ADD-PATH is sent, on
every announcement, the RIB-elected best route for the prefix, never merely
the newest arrival; an ADD-PATH client keeps receiving every path (Section
2.3.2.2). This holds for bgp-rs and bgp-rr alike, and the spec claims
RFC7947-x-5 once it is delivered. The file keeps its name
(`spec-bgp-rs-replacement-on-withdrawal.md`): `plan/README.md` and
`ai/rules/planning.md` require no rename, and the drain spec, the GR spec, the
verdict spec and journal rows cite this path, so only the title changed.

Found by spec-rfc-verdict-test-fix-pass (child spec-rfc-verdict-fix-bgp,
continuation 19, 2026-10-01). Main-thread ruling R57(b) in
`plan/handover/rfc-verdict-test-fix-pass/RULINGS.md` homes it here because the
fix needs a per-source path store in the relay, which is larger than a verdict
fix.

### Design-time re-check (2026-10-13)

| Item | Finding at HEAD `0ed8cfb2c6` |
|------|------------------------------|
| Other session on this spec | None. `ls -t tmp/session/*/state/` holds no state file for this stem; `git log` since 2026-10-01 has no commit naming it |
| Red probe | ABSENT. `rfc4271_replacement_red_test.go` exists nowhere in the tree and never reached history (`git log --all` over the path is empty). Ruling R57(b) said it "moves to that spec (never committed here)"; it was lost with the untracked tree. Phase 1 recreates it from the Evidence Shape row |
| Defect still open | Yes, for one ordering. Commit `b880c5a9a7` (2026-10-07) added final-writer ownership: `adjOutWrite.allow` (`internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_ownership.go`) drops a forwarded withdrawal whose source is not the destination's current owner. So A announce, B announce, A withdraw now leaves the client with B (B owns it; read at the producer, not run, Phase 1 runs it). B announce, A announce, A withdraw still sends the bare withdrawal: A is the owner, the writer admits it, and no rail sends B. The route-server guide says so itself: "This is not best-path selection" (`docs/guide/route-reflection.md`, Forward-All Model) |
| Replacement machinery that exists | Only for source DEPARTURE. `handleStateDown` (`rs/server_handlers.go`) sends each departed source's NLRIs through `recoverRouteSel` with `recovery-source` meta; the engine's `recoverNLRIBatch` / `relayRecovery` (`reactor/relay_recovery.go`) drains publication, drains RIB application, asks `RIBManager.recoveryRoutes` (`plugins/rib/rib_recovery.go`) for a survivor, and writes the replacement or the withdrawal under the destination's session/sequence fence. A per-prefix withdrawal from a source that stays up never enters this rail |
| Where that rail runs (round-1 B1) | `relayRecovery` stages a `recoveryAdmission`, hands it to the DESTINATION's forward worker with `fwdPool.dispatchOverflow(fwdKey{destination})`, and blocks on `admission.done`, which `fwdPool.releaseItem` signals once that worker has processed the item. So recovery cannot run on the destination's forward worker (`fwdBatchHandler`): it would wait on itself. Nor on the RS fast path: `tryDirectWriteNoFlush` runs inside `reactorForwardRS`, which `notifyMessageReceiver` (`reactor_notify.go`) calls on the SOURCE's read goroutine after `acceptedReceive` and before the `deliverChan` enqueue; `drainRecoverySources` waits for the source's accepted cut to publish, which needs that same enqueue. The pooled `adjOutWrite.owners` / `withdrawn` maps are cleared by `adjOutWrite.release`, so anything the writer owes must be copied out before release |
| Fence liveness (round-1 B2) | The departure fence is destination-wide and source-wide. `recoverySnapshot` / `relayRecovery` / `recoveryAdmission.current` retry when `Peer.sentUpdateSequence` moved, and it moves on EVERY UPDATE sent to the destination (`notifyMessageReceiver`, and `adjOutWrite.noteFilteredOperation`). `queueRecovery` returns `errRelayNoSource` (retry) when `receivePublicationCut.current` fails, which happens on ANY new UPDATE accepted from the survivor (`accepted.Load() != cut.messageID`). `recoverNLRIBatch` loops on retry with no progress bound. A busy client or a busy survivor can therefore postpone a replacement forever. This is departure's fence too, so AC-12 (which includes session loss) cannot be met by reusing it as is |
| Message-ID order (round-1 I1) | Message IDs come from one process-wide counter (`msgIDCounter` in `received_update.go`), assigned on each source's read goroutine. Cross-source ID order is not the order in which a destination's writer sees the UPDATEs, so a `SourceMessageID <= Cut` comparison cannot decide "newer owner" across sources |
| Election blind spots (round-1 I2) | `recoveryRouteLocked` runs `SelectBest` over every candidate except the source and down peers. The DESTINATION's own path is not excluded before election: when it wins, the code answers a withdrawal (`best.PeerIP == request.Destination`) although another survivor may exist. An egress-denied winner is turned into a withdrawal by `queueRecovery` (`errAllDestinationsSuppressed`) without trying the next candidate |
| Synthesized owner withdrawals (round-1 I5) | Both rails turn a policy-denied announcement into a source-bound synthesized withdrawal (`reactor_api_forward.go`, "Policy-denied announcements become source-bound synthesized withdrawals"; `forward_rs.go`, same for the fast path), and next-hop refusal uses RFC 7606 treat-as-withdraw. When the destination's owner is that source, `adjOutWrite.allow` admits it as an owner withdrawal, so the same bare-withdrawal gap applies, with the owner's route still present in the RIB |
| bgp-rr (round-1 I4, traced at the producer) | `routeReflector.forwardUpdate` (`plugins/rr/rr.go`) sends `cached <id>` to `*`, which reaches the shared cached rail with authority `adjOutForwarded`: an owner withdrawal is written bare, the same gap. `routeReflector.handleStateDown` sends `update text nlri <fam> del ...` (or the hex form) through `updateRouteSel` with no `recovery-source` meta, so `recoverNLRIBatch` is never reached; that batch is an originated item with no `SentOwnerMessage` (`announceTarget.sendBatchUpdate`), so `allow` is not source-bound and admits the withdrawal whatever the destination's owner (read, not run; Phase 1 runs it). bgp-rr declares `bgp-rib` a hard dependency (`rr/register.go` `Dependencies`). bgp-rr has no fast path (`reactorForwardRS` is gated on `rsForwardingEnabled`) |

### RFC text read (rfc/full/)

| RFC | Section | Whole sentence quoted |
|-----|---------|-----------------------|
| 4271 | 9.2 | "As part of Phase 3 of the route selection process, the BGP speaker has updated its Adj-RIBs-Out.  All newly installed routes and all newly unfeasible routes for which there is no replacement route SHALL be advertised to its peers by means of an UPDATE message." |
| 4271 | 3.1 | "Changing the attribute(s) of a route is accomplished by advertising a replacement route.  The replacement route carries new (changed) attributes and has the same address prefix as the original route." (method b of the three withdrawal methods: "a replacement route with the same NLRI can be advertised") |
| 7947 | 2.1 | "The route server SHOULD perform one or more BGP Decision Processes to select routes for subsequent advertisement to its clients, taking into account possible configuration to provide multiple Network Layer Reachability Information (NLRI) paths to a particular client as described in Section 2.3.2.2 or multiple Loc-RIBs as described in Section 2.3.2.1." |
| 7947 | 2.3 | "Neither Section 2.3 nor its subsections form part of the normative specification of this document; they are included for information purposes only." |
| 7947 | 2.3.2.2.2 | "As [RFC4271] specifies that a BGP listener must implement an implicit withdraw when it receives an UPDATE message for a prefix that already exists in its Adj-RIB-In, this approach requires explicit support for the feature both on the route server and on its clients." |
| 4456 | 6 | "When an RR receives a route from an IBGP peer, it selects the best path based on its path selection rule." |
| 4456 | 11 | "One of the key component of the route reflection approach in addressing the scaling issue is that the RR summarizes routing information and only reflects its best path." (round 11, N-6: the reading for bgp-rr below) |
| 8277 | 2.4 | "Upon transmission, the Compatibility field SHOULD be set to 0x800000." and "Upon reception, the value of the Compatibility field MUST be ignored." (round 11, I-6: the owed withdrawal of an announcement-created record is written in withdrawal framing; round 13, N-5: binds both halves of the forward rail's converted withdrawal, Data Flow 3b) |
| 8277 | 2.1 | "If a BGP speaker has not sent the Multiple Labels Capability in its BGP OPEN message on a particular BGP session, or if it has not received the Multiple Labels Capability in the BGP OPEN message from its peer on that BGP session, that BGP speaker MUST NOT send on that session any UPDATE message that binds more than one MPLS label to any given prefix." (round 13, I-2: ze sends no Multiple Labels Capability, `rfc/short/rfc8277.md` "Support remaining", so no test of this spec may need ze to send a multi-label announcement; a withdrawal binds no label) |
| 4724 | 4.2 | "When the Receiving Speaker detects termination of the TCP session for a BGP session with a peer that has advertised the Graceful Restart Capability, it MUST retain the routes received from the peer for all the address families that were previously received in the Graceful Restart Capability and MUST mark them as stale routing information." and "The router MUST NOT differentiate between stale and other routing information during forwarding." (round-3 I-2: why bgp-rr's departure skips retained families, Q-5) |
| 4724 | 4.2 | "If the session does not get re-established within the "Restart Time" that the peer advertised previously, the Receiving Speaker MUST delete all the stale routes from the peer that it is retaining." (the purge that reaches clients through `sentRemoval`, now owed) |
| 4271 | 6.7 | "In the absence of any fatal errors (that are indicated in this section), a BGP peer MAY choose, at any given time, to close its BGP connection by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Cease." (round-3 I-1: the publication-failure teardown) |
| 4486 | 4 | "If a BGP speaker runs out of resources (e.g., memory) and decides to reset a session, then the speaker MAY send a NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code Cease and the Error Subcode "Out of Resources"." (Section 3 defines subcode 8, Out of Resources; round-3 I-1) |
| 4486 | 4 | "It is RECOMMENDED that a BGP speaker behave as though the DampPeerOscillations attribute [BGP-4] were true for this peer when re-trying a BGP connection after the speaker receives a Cease NOTIFICATION message with a subcode of "Administrative Shutdown", "Peer De-configured", "Connection Rejected", or "Out of Resources"." (round-4 N-4: the cost of the publication-failure teardown, a slower reconnect from X) |
| 4724 | 4 | "It is noted that the normal BGP procedures MUST be followed when the TCP session terminates due to the sending or receiving of a BGP NOTIFICATION message." (round-4 I-2: the publication-failure teardown sends a NOTIFICATION, so GR retention MUST NOT follow it; at HEAD it does, defect D1 of `spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md`) |

Reading for bgp-rr: the RR is an IBGP speaker, so RFC 4271 Section 9.2 binds
its Adj-RIBs-Out exactly as it binds the route server's; the obligation this
spec meets for bgp-rr is RFC4271-9.2-7, not an RFC 4456 row. RFC4456-x-2
(Section 6, already enrolled) is not re-claimed or re-tagged here, and RFC scope
is frozen (no new row).

Reading for bgp-rr's election (round 11, N-6, read at the producers): the RFC
4456 reflection rules hold on every write this spec adds, because the recovery
write goes through `forwardUpdateSection` (`reactor_api_forward.go`, the "RFC
4456: Route reflection forwarding rules" block, whose `isReflected` refusal and
ORIGINATOR_ID / CLUSTER_LIST injection run for recovery sections too) with
`isIBGP`, `isRRClient` and `remoteRouterID` supplied by `resolveRelaySource`
(`reactor_api_relay.go`). The per-destination election is not Section 6's "it
selects the best path based on its path selection rule": the destination's own
paths are excluded first and a candidate the reflection rules forbid toward
that destination is stepped past (AC-25), so a client can be reflected a path
that is not the RR's single best, where Section 11 says the RR "only reflects
its best path". That sentence sits in Configuration and Deployment
Considerations and states no requirement keyword, and HEAD's forward-all
already reflects every path, non-best ones included, so this is no regression
and claims no RFC 4456 row; the spec's bgp-rr obligation stays RFC4271-9.2-7.

Reading: the skeleton's pointer to "RFC 7947 Section 2.3.2 on per-client best
path" carries no obligation, because Section 2.3 declares itself informative.
The binding text is RFC 4271 Section 9.2 (RFC4271-9.2-7), and RFC 7947 Section
2.1's SHOULD (row RFC7947-x-5, untagged, no audit verdict at HEAD). The
withdrawn route is "newly unfeasible", and a retained route from another source
IS a replacement route, so the bare withdrawal is owed only when none exists.

Reading for announcements (owner, Q-3): RFC7947-x-5 binds what a client is sent
while several sources are live. A non-ADD-PATH client holds one route per
prefix, so the route it holds is the decision process's choice for it, which
is the RIB's election with the client's own paths excluded and the client's
egress policy applied; Section 2.1's "taking into account possible
configuration to provide multiple Network Layer Reachability Information
(NLRI) paths to a particular client as described in Section 2.3.2.2" is the
ADD-PATH client, which keeps every path. "SHOULD" leaves when the decision is
computed (in advance, cached, or on demand); it does not leave which route the
client ends on.

| Requirement | Verdict | Carriers | Bearing on this spec |
|-------------|---------|----------|----------------------|
| RFC4271-9.2-7 | weak (`rfc/audit/rfc4271.json`) | `TestRIBBestChangeWithdraw` (+), `TestRIBBestChangeNoPublishSameBest` (-), both `plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go`; no discrimination record in `rfc/discrimination/rfc4271.json` | Note: "no UPDATE to a peer is asserted". Both carriers assert a RIB event, never a wire UPDATE. AC-3 adds wire-level carriers |
| RFC7947-x-1, x-2, x-3, x-4, 2.2-1, 2.2.2.2-1 | recorded in `rfc/audit/rfc7947.json` | transparency and per-client policy tests | Unaffected: a replacement carries the survivor's own received attributes (`recoveryCandidate`), so transparency holds by construction; AC-6 pins it |
| RFC7947-x-5 | no verdict, no tag | none | CLAIMED by this spec once delivered (owner, Q-3): the announcement carriers in the TDD plan (`RFC7947-x-5` positive and negative) get discrimination records, and `rfc/audit/rfc7947.json` is re-judged for the row. Forward-all, which the writer replaces at non-ADD-PATH clients, is not "one or more Decision Processes"; the RIB election at the writer is |

## Required Reading

### Architecture Docs
- [ ] `docs/guide/route-reflection.md` - Forward-All Model and source-departure sections, the operator-facing statement of what the route server sends
  → Constraint: the page states "The latest eligible advertisement still wins at a destination without ADD-PATH" and "This is not best-path selection". Both sentences become false (owner, Q-3): at a destination without ADD-PATH the RIB-elected route wins, on announcement and on withdrawal; an ADD-PATH destination still receives every eligible path. The Forward-All Model section is rewritten in the same phase as the writer code (Phase 3a), not appended to
  → Decision: a departing source already gets "an eligible surviving best route replaces the departed source with its own attributes; a route without a usable replacement is withdrawn". A per-prefix owner withdrawal reuses that semantics, so the operator learns one rule for both
- [ ] `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md` - "Source-DOWN replacement and sent ordering", the recovery rail this spec reuses
  → Constraint: "There is no timer retry and no second route inventory." The replacement MUST come from the selecting RIB (`bgp-rib`, a hard dependency of `bgp-rs` in `rs/register.go`), never from a new per-source store in the RS plugin. The skeleton's "per-source path store in the relay" is the rejected design
  → Constraint: no IPC or RIB lookup under a destination `writeMu`; publication drain then `DrainEventsApplied` then lookup; the destination worker re-checks session, causal send sequence, source generations and publication cuts under `writeMu` and retries on change
  → Constraint: a hard projection, lookup or writer failure retires the destination session through AutomaticStop/Cease, never leaves a known-invalid advertisement installed
  → Constraint (round-1 B1/B2): "re-checks session, causal send sequence ... and retries on change" is the departure fence this spec REPLACES, for departure and owner withdrawal alike, with a per-key fence (Data Flow step 7). The page's recovery section is rewritten in the same phase as the code, not appended to
- [ ] `docs/architecture/behavior/fsm.md` - "Reactor Notification": the FSM callback in `peer.go` shown calling `announceClosed` from the leave-Established arm (round 8, B-1)
  → Constraint: the page shows the down announced inside the callback. Phase 2b makes that wrong: the arm keeps the closing mark, the bookkeeping and `setState(PeerStateConnecting)` and closes the leave signal, and `runOnce`'s down defer fires a missing leave, drains, joins and announces the down. The page is edited in Phase 2b, in the same commit, with the FSM's entered-Established bit and the rule that a callback runs on whichever goroutine drains the FSM's pending queue (`ai/rules/documentation.md`)
- [ ] `docs/architecture/testing/interop.md` - BGP suite, scenario structure, `opExec` and session/route checker operations, the revert-to-red walk
  → Constraint: a new scenario is a named directory under `test/interop/scenarios/` plus a typed checker registered in `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_special.go` and listed in `bgp_test.go`; assertions never live in the scenario directory

### RFC Summaries (Scope: protocol)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc4271.md` - row RFC4271-9.2-7 (verbatim span of Section 9.2)
  → Constraint: tagged claims MUST state only what each test asserts on the wire; the positive carrier asserts a replacement UPDATE and no withdrawal, the negative asserts a withdrawal when no replacement exists
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc7947.md` - route-server summary, Forward-All and Path hiding rows
  → Constraint: RFC scope is frozen (owner, 2026-10-08): no new row is enrolled. RFC 7947 is already enrolled and RFC7947-x-5 already exists (§2.1, [SHOULD]), so claiming it needs no enrolment: Phase 5 tags its carriers and records their discrimination (owner, Q-3)
  → Decision: Phase 5 edits the summary to say what the evidence then supports: the Meta "Enrolment reason" sentence "The x-5 SHOULD (path-hiding mitigation) and x-6 MAY (add-path) are not gated" is rewritten (x-5 is the decision-process row, now carried; the checklist has no x-6 row), "Support coverage" gains the RIB-elected route at non-ADD-PATH clients on announcement and withdrawal with every path at ADD-PATH clients, and the "Implementation Notes" bullets "bgp-rs is the in-engine plugin implementing the forward-all model" and "Path hiding is not yet mitigated in ze" are corrected, each with a source anchor (Documentation row 9). The "Path hiding" Key Requirements row stays: RFC 7947 flags it as informative
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc5882.md` - "Reaction to State Changes (Section 4.2)" and "Multiple Control Protocols (Section 4.4)", the obligations `peer_bfd.go` implements (round 9, B-1: `stopBFDClient` is split)
  → Constraint: the split changes only WHERE the wait for the subscriber's exit runs. A BFD Down in Established still tears the session through `runBFDSubscriber` -> `deliverBFDEvent` (Section 4.2's "Signal path unavailable"), the suppressed AdminDown transitions stay suppressed, and the release still calls `ReleaseSession` once per opened session, so the refcounted shared session of Section 4.4 is unchanged. No RFC row, tag or claim changes

**Key insights:** (minimal context to resume after compaction)
- Defect open at HEAD only when the withdrawing source is the destination's CURRENT owner (B, A, A-withdraw). Ownership fence `b880c5a9a7` already covers A, B, A-withdraw.
- The survivor election and the fenced writer already exist for source DEPARTURE (`recoverRouteSel` -> `recoverNLRIBatch` -> `request bgp rib recovery`). The fix is to route owner withdrawals through that election, not to build a store.
- Round 2 (I-3): there is no mode field. Each owed key carries an EXCLUDED-SOURCE SET: empty for an owner withdrawal (the withdrawn path is already gone from the RIB after `DrainEventsApplied`) and for a bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal (the RIB removed the path before it dispatched the removal), the departed source(s) for a departure, unioned on merge. Round 3 (N-6) removed the per-source cut the set used to carry.
- Round 2 (B-1), round 3 (N-6): a departure owes a destination a decision only where the departed source is that destination's CURRENT owner of the key AND that owner's session is no longer current (`forwardSourceCurrent` is false for the source session generation now stored in `adjOutPath`), read under the adj-out table mutex. Elsewhere nothing is written, which keeps forward-all at destinations the departure does not concern and keeps a reconnected source's newer route. No message-ID cut is compared anywhere.
- Two forward rails reach a destination writer: plugin cached forwarding (`reactor_api_forward.go`, shared with bgp-rr) and the reactor RS fast path (`forward_rs.go`, RS only). Both set authority `adjOutForwarded`.
- The original red probe is lost and must be recreated.
- Round 1 (B1): the writer only RECORDS owed keys; a long-lived per-destination recovery worker, separate from the forward worker, does the drains, the lookup and the admission. Neither the destination's forward worker nor a source read goroutine ever waits on recovery.
- Round 1 (B2, I1): the fence is per key (an owed record with an epoch in the destination's adj-out table), not the destination-wide send sequence and not the survivor's whole-source publication cut, which livelock under churn. Round 2 (I-2, I-3): the record also notes which owners' withdrawals for the key reached the writer since the worker's snapshot, and admission retries only when one of them is the chosen candidate; the writer's record is the only authority on "owner moved", so the RIB carries no second owner-moved check.
- Round 2 (I-1): no shared reactor-wide drain. Each recovery worker's round drains for itself; the owed set already coalesces every key owed during a round into the next one.
- Round 1 (I4): bgp-rr is in scope: same per-prefix gap on the cached rail, and a departure that sends plain unowned withdrawals.
- Round 3 (B-1): the owed-key rule is per authority. Only an announcement that installs a NEW owner removes the record. A bgp-rib replay of `ribOut[Destination]` (`adjOutExpected`: ROUTE-REFRESH, `clear bgp rib out`) is refused while the key is owed, because it replays the very route being replaced.
- Round 3 (I-2): a GR-retained source leaves through bgp-rib's sent-lifecycle removals (`sentRemoval`), which are owed exactly like an owner withdrawal. bgp-rr's departure honours `retention.Family` as bgp-rs does (Q-5).
- Round 3 (I-1), round 5 (B-1): a receive-publication failure ends that source's session (Cease, Out of Resources), and the round that meets the failure or the stop EXCLUDES that source from every key it decides, so the election never sees the source's unpublished state; its departure decides the keys it owns.
- Round 3 (I-6): "is a recovery owner registered" is answered by the frozen command registry's lock-free `Lookup`, not by a second atomic.
- Round 3 (I-3): a round's lookup goes out in chunks of at most 500 keys per family; the chunk bounds a request, never the owed set.
- Round 4 (I-1), round 5 (I-1): R-12's sent-projection removal fires only for the sent event of a RECOVERY write (marked in a fresh copy of the item's meta) whose candidate's received path is now gone with no newer revision, and leaves through a long-lived bgp-rib lifecycle dispatcher, never an RPC on the event path. No watermark: the candidate came out of bgp-rib's own lookup, so it was applied by construction.
- Round 4 (I-2), round 5 (I-2): the publication-failure teardown is its own defect-fix phase (2b) and commit. It declares "no retention" through D1's reason producer, a second input beside `notificationExchanged`, because an unsent Cease leaves that flag false. Round 6 (I-2): that input reports a second declared reason, `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` ("adj-rib-in invalid", owner Q-6), and GR calls the declared predicate `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention`, so `gr.go` always changes.
- Round 4 (I-4), round 5 (I-3), round 6 (B-1, option i): drain replays are `adjOutExpected` and are refused for an owed key. The refusal is counted by ONE per-peer monotonic atomic on `Peer`, incremented under `writeMu` by whichever goroutine performs the write (the forward worker for a queued receipt write included), never reset, and read only as a delta. The update-route reply, the SDK and bgp-rib's `resent` / `refused` are unchanged, and no command waits for a queued write: round 5's reply count needed a wait that self-waits through bgp-rib's event delivery (round-6 B-1). This spec deletes `recoverySnapshot`, `sentUpdateSequence` and `noteFilteredOperation`, and the drain keeps only the per-peer `writeMu` take-and-release.
- Round 5 (N-7): a candidate stepped past on `errRelayNoSource` re-owes the key with that source added to its excluded-source set (a merge), so the next round elects over the survivors without it, which keeps AC-12 under the non-transitive MED comparator.
- Round 7 (I-1): `writeUpdateBody` filters a body twice whenever a pass removed an NLRI, so every owed-set effect of `allow` (owe, merge, removal, mover, owed-refused count, wake) is staged on the `adjOutWrite`, reset between the passes, and applied once per write.
- Round 7 (I-2, option b): the down event, and D1's reason producer with it, run in `runOnce` after `<-deliveryDone`, so a late publication failure is in the reason; every plugin now gets a session's buffered UPDATEs before its down. The "Adj-RIB-In invalid" fact is the existing `receivePublication.failed`.
- Round 7 (I-3): one wake rule (every owe, merge and re-owe wakes the worker; an admission that leaves a key owed starts the next round at once), a per-key source fence on the forward worker, and `recoveryAdmission.retry` deleted.
- Round 8 (B-1, I-3): an FSM callback runs on whichever goroutine drains the event, so on a local teardown the leave callback can still be running when `runOnce` passes `<-deliveryDone`. The drain and the down move into a `runOnce` DOWN DEFER: fire `EventTCPConnectionFails` when the FSM still reads Established (no other leave came), close `deliverChan`, `<-deliveryDone`, wait on the leave signal the leave arm closes after `setState(PeerStateConnecting)` when the FSM's sticky entered-Established bit is set, then reason, down, history row and dynamic-peer cleanup. Data Flow 4's departure depends on the down following `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`. Being a defer, it also covers a read-goroutine panic that leaves no lock held (round 9, I-3). Fixes HEAD's "session ends with no down event" here, no journal row.
- Round 9 (B-1): the BFD subscriber goroutine is a drainer too. A non-strict peer's BFD Down in Established runs the leave callback on the subscriber, and the callback's BFD release ended in `<-done`, the channel that same subscriber closes on exit: a self-wait. So the leave arm keeps only the release's non-waiting half (`close(stop)`, `Unsubscribe`, `ReleaseSession`) and hands the subscriber's `done` to the leave record, and the down defer waits on it after step 3, on the `runOnce` goroutine, which is never the subscriber. This also fixes a HEAD defect, the same self-wait, which leaks the subscriber goroutine and loses that session's down (the main thread journals it). Round 9 (I-2), corrected in round 10 (I-2): `Reactor.cleanup` holds `r.mu` for its whole run, Phase 1 (`fwdPool.Stop`, and in standalone mode the owned `r.api.Stop()`, which `cleanup`'s own comment bounds at `pluginStopGrace` 3 s plus a 500 ms group wait), Phase 2's one concurrent 2 s wait for every peer, and Phase 3, so a leave arm that runs after `cleanup` took `r.mu` waits that whole hold out in `markSessionUp`, and the downs this spec newly announces at reactor stop land after it. One drainer is not bounded by it: a forward-pool worker draining a leave blocks in `markSessionUp` while `fwdPool.Stop` joins it with no deadline under the same `r.mu`, a deadlock HEAD already has and this spec neither causes nor widens (Data Flow 5 step 3; `plan/journal/lock-held-across-a-join.md`).
- Owner answer Q-3 (2026-10-13): announcements are in scope. At a non-ADD-PATH destination with a registered recovery owner, a forwarded or initial-replay announcement for a key that has NO adj-out entry and no owed record is written as today (zero-copy; it is the only route that destination holds); one for a key that HAS an entry or a record is OWED like an owner withdrawal (empty excluded-source set, merged on a record) and the recovery worker writes the RIB-elected route (Data Flow 3a). The admission writes nothing when the elected candidate is the route the destination already holds (owner identity and received message ID equal). An announcement that lands on a record between snapshot and admission is a mover of kind ANNOUNCE: whatever its source, the admission writes its decision and leaves the key owed, so the next round sees the newer route (WRITTEN, RE-OWED, a seventh outcome); only a WITHDRAW mover that is the chosen candidate retries without a write, as before. A forwarded announcement enters the record's CURRENT-ANNOUNCER set with its session generation, and the round subtracts every still-current announcer from the key's excluded sources, so a reconnected source's new route stays electable whichever of its departure and its re-announcement reached the writer first (AC-18). ADD-PATH destinations and originated writes are unchanged.
- Round 8 (I-1, I-2): a non-opaque raw write applies its staged record removals (`record` runs for it); the admission's per-key outcomes are written, decided with nothing to write, record already gone, left owed, re-owed, error, and only none of these is `errRecoveryNotWritten`.

## Current Behavior (MANDATORY)

**Source files read:** (must read BEFORE you write this spec)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server_withdrawal.go` (190L) - `processForward`: extracts families and NLRI records, forwards the WHOLE UPDATE (announcements and withdrawals alike) through `batchForwardUpdate`, or only releases the cache when `ReactorForwarded` is set, then updates the per-source `withdrawals` map used at peer-down
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server.go` (922L) - `routeServer.withdrawals` is a key-only inventory (sourcePeer -> withdrawalKey -> withdrawalEntry) for peer-down; it holds no attributes, so it cannot supply a replacement. `recoverRouteSel` sends a command with `recovery-source` and `recovery-cut` meta through `UpdateRouteWithMeta`
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server_handlers.go` (790L) - `handleStateDown` batches the departed source's NLRIs as `update text|hex nlri <fam> del ...` and calls `recoverRouteSel(ExcludeAddr(source), ..., cut)`
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_ownership.go` (~600L) - `adjOutWrite.allow`: under `adjOutForwarded`, a withdrawal is admitted only when the destination's recorded owner equals the forwarded owner (`adjOutSameOwner`); an absent entry admits only a synthesized withdrawal. No replacement is considered
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/relay_recovery.go` (~300L) - `recoverNLRIBatch` per destination: snapshot session and send sequence, capture source receipts, drain publication, `DrainEventsApplied`, then `relayRecovery` asks `ribevents.RecoveryProvider()` in process or forwards `request bgp rib recovery`; retries when the fence moved
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_recovery.go` (301L) - `recoveryRoutes`: ADD-PATH destination -> withdraw only the source's sent identifiers at or below the cut; otherwise `recoveryRouteLocked` gathers candidates, skips the source and down peers, runs `SelectBest`, returns the survivor with its own attributes and next hop, or a withdrawal only if the sent owner is the source
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_rs.go` (853L) - `reactorForwardRS`: the RS fast path writes the UPDATE (withdrawals included) to eligible destinations before the plugin sees it, with authority `adjOutForwarded`
- [ ] `internal/core/bgp/ribevents/recovery.go` - `RecoveryRequest{Source, Destination, Family, NLRIs, AddPath, PrefixOnly, SentAddPath, Cut}`; no field says why recovery runs
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_pool.go` (1613L) - `fwdBatchHandler` is the per-destination forward worker; `fwdPool.Dispatch` blocks on a full channel but has no non-test caller; the forward rails use `TryDispatch` then `dispatchOverflow`, whose overflow buffer is unbounded; `releaseItem` signals `recoveryAdmission.done`
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go` (714L) - `notifyMessageReceiver`: `sentUpdateSequence.Add(1)` per sent UPDATE; on receive `acceptedReceive`, then `reactorForwardRS` on the read goroutine, then the `deliverChan` enqueue
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/receive_publication.go` - `receivePublicationCut.current` is false once any later UPDATE is accepted from that source
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rr/rr.go` - `forwardUpdate` (`cached <id>` to `*`); `handleStateDown` (plain `del` batches through `updateRouteSel`, no recovery meta, one goroutine per down event)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server.go` `recoverRouteSel` and `rs/server_handlers.go` `handleStateDown` - the departure cut is `routeServer.seenMsgID` (deleted from the departure path by round 3, N-6)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_sent_lifecycle.go` (272L) - `withdrawRemovedSentLocked` and `pruneSentSourceLocked` collect, under `peerMu` and AFTER the operation removed the received entries, one `sentRemoval` per destination whose `ribOut` entry the removed source owns; `dispatchSentLifecycle` sends each as a bare withdrawal with `sent-owner-message` meta, which the reactor admits under `adjOutExpected`. No election runs. Callers: `autoExpireStale`, `releaseRoutes`, `retainRoutes`, `purgeStale` (`rib_commands.go`) and the stale-community delete (`rib_commands_community.go`)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_replay.go` and `rib_commands.go` `sendRoutes` - `replayRoutesWithCursor`, `resendRoutesWithCursor` and `sendRoutes` replay `ribOut[peer]` with `sent-owner-message` meta (ROUTE-REFRESH, `clear bgp rib out`). The reactor admits them under `adjOutExpected` (`announceTarget.sendBatchUpdate`, `reactor_api_batch.go`): the entry must be present with an equal revision, and the owner is kept from the entry (`adjOutWrite.allow`; `fwdItem.writePath` returns only the revision)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/sent_owner.go` `sentSourceCurrent` - checks that a sent entry's received owner (source, path identifier, ADD-PATH flag, message ID) still exists in the receive inventory; used by peer-up replay only (`collectGroupedRibOutRoutesFiltered` under `peerUp`; refresh replays skip it). It also answers false for a non-local entry with `SourceOwner == 0` or `SourceMessageID == 0`, an unparsable `SourcePeer`, no `bgpPeers[source]`, a newer received revision (it compares the message ID for equality), and a received path not yet applied (round 4, I-1)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_structured.go` `handleSentStructured` / `storeSentEntries`, and `rib.go` `handleSent` - THREE sent-store sites: `storeSentEntries` reached from `handleSentStructured` and from `handleSent`'s raw-NLRI loop, and the inline store in `handleSent`'s `FamilyOps` `routeaction.Add` branch, which does not call `storeSentEntries`. All run under `peerMu` on bgp-rib's event dispatch (`dispatchStructured`), which does no IPC today. "Sent event callbacks enqueue delivery; they do not synchronously update the RIB" (`rib-storage-design.md`), and no ordering ties a sent event's application to the application of the received UPDATE it forwarded: the fast path writes before the plugins see the UPDATE. `updateRouteWithMeta` is a synchronous engine RPC with a 10 s timeout (round 4, I-1)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/plugins/gr/gr.go` `handleStructuredState` / `handleStateEvent` - `wasNotification` is `reason == "notification"`, but `peer_run.go` sends "session closed" or "connection lost", so GR retains a source's families after a NOTIFICATION teardown, including one Ze sends (`Session.teardownAutomatic`). Open defect D1 of `plan/immediate/spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md` (round 4, I-2)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/relay_recovery.go` `recoverySnapshot` and `peer.go` `sentUpdateSequence` - the snapshot takes and releases the destination's `writeMu` and reads the sequence; its two callers are the fence this spec deletes, and the sequence's only readers are `recoverySnapshot` and `recoveryAdmission.current`. The drain spec's A-14 cites the take-and-release as its ordering shape (round 4, I-4)
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/peer_run.go` - the receive delivery worker calls `publication.fail()` on a recovered panic (then exits) and when the reactor or its message receiver is nil (then continues). Nothing ends the session, so the read goroutine's enqueue takes `case <-publication.done` and only logs (`reactor_notify.go`); the worker's comment "the session will be torn down" is false. The FSM leave-Established callback calls `clearEncodingContexts` on whichever goroutine drains the FSM event (the read goroutine inside `Session.Run` on a remote close, a teardown's caller or a timer goroutine otherwise; round 8, B-1); the `runOnce` defer calls it again after `Run` returns. `setState(PeerStateEstablished)` runs inside the FSM Established callback and moves `forwardGeneration`, as `Peer.Stop` also does
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/peer_bfd.go` `runBFDSubscriber` / `deliverBFDEvent` / `stopBFDClient` (round 9, B-1, read at the producer) - the subscriber goroutine runs `defer close(done)` and delivers each BFD change inline through `deliverBFDEvent` -> `Session.handleBFDEvent`; in Established a BFD Down tears the session (`bfdTeardown` -> `Session.teardown` -> `logFSMEvent`), so the subscriber can drain the leave-Established callback. That callback's first statement for a non-strict peer is `stopBFDClient`, which nils `p.bfd` under `bfd.mu`, closes `stop`, calls `Unsubscribe` and `ReleaseSession`, then waits `<-done`: on the subscriber that wait never returns. At HEAD the subscriber goroutine leaks, the rest of the callback never runs (no `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`, no down), and `runOnce` still returns because `Session.Run` ends on the closed connection. `endVanishedBFDSession` returns at once when `p.bfd.handle` no longer matches, and `Peer.cleanup`'s later `stopBFDClient` is a no-op once the handle is nil
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor.go` `Reactor.cleanup` (round 9, I-2, read at the producer) - takes `r.mu.Lock` with a deferred unlock; Phase 1 calls `r.fwdPool.Stop()` (which closes every worker channel, then joins each worker on `<-kw.w.done` with no deadline), in standalone mode `r.api.Stop()` (bounded by `pluginStopGrace` 3 s plus a 500 ms group wait, per `cleanup`'s comment), the listeners and signals, and signals every peer (`peer.Stop`); Phase 2 waits for every peer CONCURRENTLY under one 2 s `waitCtx` (one `wg.Go` per peer calling `peer.Wait(waitCtx)`, then one `wg.Wait`); Phase 3 stops `recentUpdates`; all of it while holding `r.mu` (round 10, I-2, read at the producer). A leave arm that runs after that lock is taken blocks in `markSessionUp` (`r.mu.Lock`) until `cleanup` returns, whatever the number of peers. `cleanup` runs on the `monitor` goroutine after `<-r.ctx.Done()`, not inside `StopForRestart` (`r.stop(false)` ends at its cancel), and `Reactor.Wait` waits on `r.wg`, which `monitor` holds until `cleanup` returns. A forward-pool worker is the exception: if it drains a leave (its `checkTeardown` -> `congestionTeardownPeer`, Data Flow 5 step 3) after `cleanup` took `r.mu`, it blocks in `markSessionUp`, `fwdPool.Stop` never sees its `done`, and the stop wedges for good, at HEAD already (`plan/journal/lock-held-across-a-join.md`)
- [ ] `internal/component/plugin/server/command_registry.go` - `Freeze` publishes an immutable snapshot and `Lookup` reads it lock-free; `Register`, `Unregister` and `unregisterAll` (process death) call `republishFrozen`, so the snapshot tracks registration. Before `Freeze`, `Lookup` takes `r.mu.RLock`

The update-route reply carries `plugin.RouteResult{Announced, Withdrawn, Warnings}` (`plugins/cmd/update/update_text.go`, end of `DispatchNLRIGroups`), copied into `rpc.UpdateRouteOutput` by `extractUpdateRouteOutput` (`plugin/server/dispatch.go`) and returned by the SDK's `UpdateRouteWithMeta` (`pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_engine.go`); bgp-rib's `updateRouteWithMeta` discards both counts and `resendRoutesWithCursor` adds to `refused` only on a command error. None of these changes (round 6, B-1): an NLRI `adjOutWrite.allow` refuses with `false, nil` is dropped by `adjOutWrite.section` (`removed++`) and the command still succeeds, so at HEAD an `adjOutExpected` revision mismatch is already counted `resent` (journal row in `plan/journal/counter-counts-the-wrong-packets.md`)
- [ ] bgp-rib's ROUTE-REFRESH replay path (round 6, B-1) - `Process.deliveryLoop` -> `deliverBatch` -> `DirectBridge.DeliverStructured` -> bgp-rib's `OnStructuredEvent` closure -> `dispatchStructured` -> `handleRefreshStructured` (`rib_structured.go`; `handleRefresh` in `rib.go` for text) -> `sendRoutes` -> `updateRouteWithMeta` -> SDK `callEngineRaw` -> `DirectBridge.DispatchRPC` (synchronous on that goroutine) -> `sendBatchUpdate`. So a refresh replay runs on bgp-rib's own event-delivery goroutine. The forward worker writing a queued receipt item emits each earlier write's sent event synchronously (`session_write.go` -> `onMessageReceived` -> `EventDispatcher.OnMessageSent` -> `proc.Deliver`), and `Process.Deliver` blocks on a full `eventChan` (`eventDeliveryCapacity = 64`, `process/delivery.go`), which only that `deliveryLoop` drains. Any wait for a queued receipt write on this path waits on itself

- [ ] Announcement producers (owner, Q-3, read at the producers 2026-10-13): `adjOutWrite.allow` (`reactor/session_ownership.go`) admits a forwarded announcement (`adjOutForwarded`) whatever the entry holds and stores the new owner in `w.owners`, so the newest arrival owns the key; it REFUSES an `adjOutInitial` announcement whenever an entry exists (`case adjOutInitial: ... if entry != nil { return false, nil }`). `adjOutInitial` is set by `announceTarget.sendBatchUpdate` (`reactor_api_batch.go`, `if batch.InitialReplay != 0 { owner.authority = adjOutInitial ... }`) for a batch carrying `InitialReplay`, and its ONLY producer is bgp-rib's `replayRoutesWithCursor` (`rib/rib_replay.go`), the one site that sets `InitialReplayMeta` (round 11, I-1, corrected at the producers): it replays `ribOut[peer]` to a RECONNECTING peer (`collectPeerUpReplay` with `seenBefore`; for a peer's first session it returns nothing and logs a non-empty Adj-RIB-Out). The peer-up relays are NOT `adjOutInitial`: bgp-rs `handleStateUp` -> `replayForPeer` (`rs/server_handlers.go`) sends `request bgp adj-rib-in replay`, bgp-rr's `routeReflector.replayForPeer` (`rr/rr.go`) does the same and adds `replayFlowSpecs`, and bgp-adj-rib-in replays through `relayRoutes` / `relayChunk` -> `RelayStoredRoute` (`reactor_api_relay.go`, `src.info.initialUpdate = routes[i].InitialUpdate`) -> `forwardUpdateCore` -> `forwardUpdateSection`, which labels every section `adjOutForwarded`; `initialUpdate` only marks the write as part of the initial update. bgp-adj-rib-in reads `InitialReplay` only to signal "plugin session ready" (`signalSessionReady`). So at HEAD a new client's initial table holds, per prefix, the LAST source's relayed route (the relay is forward-all), and a live client holds the NEWEST forwarded one; neither is an election. The `adjOutInitial` owner `fwdItem` carries no `sourceMessageID` (the literal in `sendBatchUpdate` sets source, generation and initial-replay fields only), its `writePath` branch (`forward_provenance.go`) returns source, path and ADD-PATH flag only, and the batch's `SentOwnerMessage` is the ribOut entry's SENT ID (`SentOwnerMessageMeta: g.MinMsgID`), which `recordSection` stores as `entry.revision`; the received ID lives in `ribOutEntry.SourceMessageID` (`rib/ribout_entry.go`), and no meta key, `NLRIBatch` field or `replayGroup` field carries it (`replayGroup` holds `SourceID`, `SourceOwner`, `SourcePath`, `SourceAddPath`; `groupKey` does not split by received message). bgp-rr's `forwardUpdate` sends `cached <id>` to `*` (`rr/rr.go`), the same `adjOutForwarded` rail, with the RFC 4456 reflection rules applied in the reactor (`reactor_api_forward.go`, "RFC 4456: Route reflection forwarding rules") and on the relay path the recovery write takes (`reactor_api_relay.go`, "RFC 4456 reflection rules"). `adjOutPath` (`adj_rib_out.go`) holds `source`, `received`, `addPath`, `synthesized`, `revision`; the entry's `revision` is the SENT receipt's message ID (`adjOutWrite.record`, `entry.revision = w.session.sentReceipt.MessageID()`), not the source's received message ID, which the item carries as `fwdItem.sourceMessageID` ("Original received generation for ownership", `forward_pool.go`)

**Behavior to preserve:** (unless the user explicitly said to change it)
- ADD-PATH destinations: every eligible path is forwarded with its own identifier, on announcement as today (RFC 7947 Section 2.3.2.2); an owner withdrawal still withdraws exactly the advertised identifier; surviving paths are already present there.
- The first announcement of a key at a non-ADD-PATH destination that holds no entry and no record for it keeps the zero-copy cached write.
- A withdrawal from a non-owner is still dropped at the writer (`b880c5a9a7`), and a synthesized policy withdrawal from a non-owner keeps today's permission (written only when the destination holds no entry).
- Source-departure ELECTION semantics where the departed source owns the destination's route (every candidate of the departed source excluded, survivor with its own attributes) and the behaviours `rib_recovery_test.go`, `relay_recovery_test.go` and `forward_publication_ownership_test.go` pin; each existing unit's guard and its new carrier are in "Guards of deleted code" under Files to Modify (round-4 I-3). The departure FENCE changes (round-1 B2: it livelocks, and AC-12 covers session loss): a test whose assertion encodes the destination-wide retry (`TestRecoveryFinalWorkerRequestsCausalReresolution`, unrelated-prefix subcase) is changed to assert the per-key outcome, which is stricter (the replacement is written with no retry), never relaxed. The same test's other guard, "an old DOWN overwriting a newer target advertisement", is kept as an assertion and is now met by the departure's owner-and-generation test (Data Flow step 4, AC-18; round 3 replaced round 2's same-source cut with it), not by the deleted sequence fence. The worker-failure half of `TestRecoveryWaitsForFilteredReceivedPublication` is NOT preserved: it changes as listed under BEHAVIOUR CHANGES in the guards table (round 5, B-1). None of these units carries an `RFC requirement:` tag (checked 2026-10-13)
- Peer-down `withdrawals` inventory maintenance in `processForward`.
- RS transparency: AS_PATH, NEXT_HOP, MED of the replacement are the survivor's received values (RFC7947-x-1/x-2/x-3 carriers stay green).
- bgp-rr's RFC 4456 client/non-client rules, ORIGINATOR_ID and CLUSTER_LIST handling, on the cached forward and on the relay path the recovery write takes; at ADD-PATH clients its announcement forwarding is unchanged.
- A bgp-rib replay (ROUTE-REFRESH, `clear bgp rib out`) of a key that is NOT owed is admitted under `adjOutExpected` exactly as today.
- bgp-rs keeps skipping, at departure, every family `retention.Family` keeps.

**Behavior to change:** (only what the user asked for)
- (Owner, Q-3.) An announcement to a non-ADD-PATH destination, on either rail, from bgp-rs or bgp-rr, live or in the peer-up initial replay, no longer installs the newest arrival. When the destination already holds a route for the key (or the key is owed), the announcement is owed and the destination ends on the RIB-elected route for the prefix: a worse later route does not displace a better earlier one, a better later route does, and an owner's re-announcement with worse attributes yields another source's better route. A new client's initial table holds the elected route per prefix, not the last relayed one, and a reconnecting client's bgp-rib `ribOut` replay does not displace the election either (round 11, I-1). A destination already holding the elected route is sent nothing.
- A withdrawal from the destination's current owner, sent to a non-ADD-PATH destination, becomes the RIB-elected surviving route (an UPDATE carrying it) when one exists, and stays a withdrawal only when none exists. This covers received withdrawals and the owner's synthesized withdrawals (policy-denied re-announcement, treat-as-withdraw), on both rails, for bgp-rs and bgp-rr.
- bgp-rr source departure goes through the same recovery as bgp-rs departure (survivor or owned withdrawal), instead of a plain withdrawal sent to every peer.
- Departure, for bgp-rs and bgp-rr alike, decides a key at a destination only where the departed source is that destination's current owner of the key (round-2 B-1). Today `recoveryRouteLocked` checks the owner only on its withdrawal branch, and the `adjOutRecovery` admission in `adjOutWrite.allow` checks no owner for an announcement, so a departure re-elects and rewrites a destination whose route came from another source (sending B again, or E when `SelectBest` prefers E, which overrides forward-all on an unrelated departure). After this spec such a destination is written nothing.
- Departure and withdrawal recovery both run on a per-destination recovery worker with a per-key fence, instead of inline on the command goroutine with a destination-wide fence.
- A bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal (GR restart-time expiry in `autoExpireStale`, `release-routes`, `retain-routes`, `purge-stale`, the stale-community delete) sent to a non-ADD-PATH destination becomes the RIB-elected survivor when one exists, instead of a bare withdrawal (round-3 I-2). The owner's AC-12 constraint covers every way a source stops offering a route, and this is the way a GR-capable source that never returns stops offering one.
- bgp-rr's source departure honours GR retention as bgp-rs does: a family `retention.Family` keeps is not withdrawn at departure, and its eventual purge reaches the client through the lifecycle removal above (round-3 I-2; RFC 4724 Section 4.2; Q-5).
- A receive-publication failure (a recovered delivery-worker panic, or a nil reactor or message receiver) ends that source's session with Cease / Out of Resources, instead of leaving it Established while its UPDATEs never reach the plugins (round-3 I-1), and that session's end declares "no retention", whether or not the Cease reached the wire (round 5, I-2; Phase 2b).
- A recovery round that meets another source's failed or stopped publication excludes that source from every key it decides and continues, instead of failing the round and retiring the DESTINATION with Cease / Out of Resources (round 5, B-1).

## Data Flow (MANDATORY - see `ai/rules/architecture.md`)

### Entry Point
- Wire: an UPDATE from source peer A whose Withdrawn Routes field or MP_UNREACH_NLRI names a prefix A previously announced, received on an Established session.
- Format at entry: raw UPDATE bytes held in the reactor message cache, delivered to bgp-rs as a structured `update-received` event (`item.msg`) or text in fork mode.
- Announcement entry (owner, Q-3): an UPDATE from source N whose NLRI or MP_REACH_NLRI announces a prefix, forwarded live on either rail, or relayed to a client that just came up (bgp-rs or bgp-rr `replayForPeer` through bgp-adj-rib-in, or bgp-adj-rib-in's own peer-up replay, all on the forwarded rail), or replayed from bgp-rib's `ribOut[client]` to a reconnecting client (`replayRoutesWithCursor`, the only `adjOutInitial` producer; round 11, I-1), reaching a destination that did not negotiate ADD-PATH send for the family.
- Second entry (round-3 I-2): a bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal (`sentRemoval` via `dispatchSentLifecycle`) for a source whose GR-retained routes are purged, arriving at the reactor as a withdraw command with `sent-owner-message` meta.

### Transformation Path
1. Reactor receive: the UPDATE is accepted, cached, and either written by the RS fast path (`reactorForwardRS`, bgp-rs only) or handed to the forwarding plugin.
2. bgp-rs worker (`processForward`) or bgp-rr (`forwardUpdate`): forwards the cached UPDATE (`ForwardCached` / `cached <id>`) to selected targets.
3. Session writer (destination forward worker, or the source read goroutine on the fast path): `adjOutWrite.allow` per NLRI. NEW: a withdrawal for a key whose adj-out entry is owned by a source (`entry.owner.source` set), on a destination that did not negotiate ADD-PATH send for the family, while a recovery owner is registered (Key Design Decisions, "No RIB owner"), is not emitted, whatever the entry's contested bit says (round 12, I-1, correcting round 11's bare write of an uncontested owner withdrawal: a clear bit proves only that no other candidate has crossed THIS writer yet, not that bgp-rib holds none; a second candidate can be accepted in bgp-rib with its forward still in flight to this writer, the cached rail being slower than the fast path, or can be a GR-retained path whose forward `fwdBatchHandler` dropped on a generation move, `item.authority != adjOutExpected && !sourceCurrent` -> `continue`, and a bare withdrawal would then delete the entry the late candidate needed and leave C with no route while bgp-rib holds one; the bit governs re-announcements only, step 3a), when it is (a) a forwarded withdrawal, received or synthesized, from that owner (`adjOutForwarded`, `adjOutSameOwner`), or (b) a bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal the writer admits under `adjOutExpected` (`sentRemoval`, sent by `dispatchSentLifecycle`; round-3 I-2). Case (b) is how a GR-retained source's routes leave: bgp-rs, and after this spec bgp-rr (Q-5), skip retained families at departure, and the RIB's eventual purge used to write a bare withdrawal with no election. The writer records the key in the destination's OWED SET and leaves the adj-out entry with the old owner until recovery writes. The owed record holds: a copy of the destination-framed NLRI bytes (taken before `adjOutWrite.release` clears the pooled maps), the family, the destination session it belongs to, an EMPTY excluded-source set, an epoch, and an empty MOVERS set (step 7). The set is empty for (a) because an owner withdrawal excludes nobody (round-2 I-3), and for (b) because the RIB removed those received paths under `peerMu` before it dispatched the removal (A-9), so the purged path is no longer a candidate while the source's other live paths stay eligible. The writer then wakes the destination's recovery worker without blocking: a one-slot wake channel, send-or-skip. The wake carries no data; the owed set is the queue, so a skipped wake loses nothing (round-2 N-7). ONE WAKE RULE (round 7, I-3): every insertion into the owed set and every merge into a record wakes the worker, whoever makes it: the writer's owe or merge here, the departure's owe or merge (step 4), and the admission's re-owe (step 7). The worker also starts its next round at once, without waiting for a wake, whenever an admission completes with any key of its snapshot still owed (left owed on an epoch mismatch or on the mover retry, where a recorded mover is the candidate the admission was about to write, or re-owed; a record of another destination session is never left owed, step 7; round 9, I-1). This cannot hot-loop: every key still owed after an admission is owed because something changed (its epoch moved on a merge, its candidate withdrew, or a source was added to its excluded-source set), and each such change is bounded (step 7, R-9). The writer does no IPC, no drain and no wait. EFFECTS ARE STAGED, never applied inside `allow` (round 7, I-1, read at the producer): `Session.writeUpdateBody` runs `ownership.filter(nil, body)` and, whenever that pass removed an NLRI (`ownership.removed != 0`), clears `removed`, `suppressed`, `owners` and `withdrawn` and runs `ownership.filter(handle.Buf, body)` a second time, and both passes call `section` -> `allow` for every NLRI. Within ONE pass, every MP_UNREACH NLRI goes through `allow` twice (round 8, N-1, read at the producer): `filter`'s "Populate withdrawal admission before announcements" loop calls `section` on every MP_UNREACH attribute, and its second attribute traversal calls it again, so a non-IPv4-unicast withdrawal is evaluated twice per pass and four times in a twice-filtered write. An owed NLRI is always removed, so every write that owes a key and also carries an admitted NLRI evaluates `allow` at least twice for it. So every side effect this spec gives `allow` for an owed key (the new owe record, the merge, the record removal by a new owner, the mover note, the owed-refused count of step 7, and the wake) is staged on the `adjOutWrite` during `filter`, STAGED PER KEY (keyed by `adjOutKey`) and idempotent within a pass, never appended, reset with `owners` and `withdrawn` between the two passes, and applied ONCE, under `writeMu` with the table mutex, after the last pass that ran: on the `length == 0` return (an all-owed body, which never reaches `record`), on the PATHS-LIMIT `n == 0` return, and on the write path before `record`. Both passes therefore read the table as it stood before this write, and the second decides exactly as the first. A pass that returns an error applies nothing, because nothing of that body is written at HEAD either and the error goes back to the caller. A RAW WRITE IS NOT OBSERVE-ONLY (round 8, I-1, read at the producer, correcting round 7): `writeUpdateBody`'s `raw` branch, reached only from `SendRawMessage` under `writeMu` with no `sentForward`, runs `ownership.filter(nil, body)` and, when that returns no error (`opaque` false), `ownership.record(body)`, which installs each announced key with the originated owner and deletes each withdrawn key. Its bytes reach the wire whatever `allow` says, but the table changes. So a raw write whose filter pass returned no error applies its staged record removals, and only those: an announcement of an owed key is an originated write with no sent-owner authority, so it installs a new owner and REMOVES the record (step 7, first bullet, AC-6), and nothing else can be staged, because no NLRI of a raw write is source-bound (no owe, merge, mover or owed-refused count). Its originated withdrawal of an owed key deletes the adj-out entry and leaves the record, as step 7 says for every originated withdrawal, and step 8 reads that. An opaque raw write (filter error) applies nothing and records nothing, as today. A raw write never carries an owner withdrawal that should be owed, because it holds no sent-owner authority. SAME KEY WITHDRAWN AND ANNOUNCED IN ONE BODY (round 8, N-2): RFC 4271 Section 4.3 reads "A BGP speaker SHOULD treat an UPDATE message of this form as though the WITHDRAWN ROUTES do not contain the address prefix.", so an announcement of a key in the same body cancels that key's staged owe: the owner's new route installs, and the key is neither owed nor rewritten by a later round. A key owed before the write began needs no such rule, because the announcement removes its record (step 7, first bullet). The staged effects live in the pooled `adjOutWrite` and are copied into the table before `adjOutWrite.release` clears it. On the fast path, an UPDATE whose only content for this destination is owed keys reports `tryDirectWriteNoFlush` handled AND delivered: the item is finished and the destination's decision is owned by recovery, so the cached rail must not forward it a second time (round-2 N-6). Every forwarded write first checks whether the destination's owed set is empty, and only a non-empty set costs a per-NLRI lookup (round-2 N-9). Every write that installs an owner also records that owner's source session generation in `adjOutPath` (a new field: the forwarded or initial item's `receivedGeneration`, or the survivor's `generation` on a recovery write; an `adjOutExpected` write keeps `entry.owner`, generation included). `adjOutSameOwner` keeps ignoring it, as the `allow` comment requires so that a GR-retained path stays withdrawable by the same source on its new session (round-3 N-6). The same writes also record the owner's RECEIVED message ID in `adjOutPath` (a second new field: the item's `sourceMessageID` for a forwarded write; for an `adjOutInitial` write, the ribOut entry's received `SourceMessageID`, which reaches the item through a chain this spec adds (round 11, I-2): `replayGroup` gains the field and `groupKey` splits by it, so one replay command carries one received revision; `replayRoutesWithCursor` sets a new `initial-source-message` meta key beside `initial-source-owner`; `initialReplayFromMeta` (`plugins/cmd/update/update_text.go`) reads it into a new `NLRIBatch.InitialSourceMessage`, required with the other `initial-source-*` keys for a non-local replay; `sendBatchUpdate` sets the owner item's `sourceMessageID` from it; and `writePath`'s `adjOutInitial` branch returns it, so the "already holds it" test of step 3a is exact for an entry the ribOut replay installed; the candidate's `RecoveryRoute.MessageID` for a recovery write, which reaches the item as `sourceMessageID` through the R-12 chain; an `adjOutExpected` write keeps `entry.owner`), because the entry's `revision` is the sent receipt's ID and cannot say which received revision the destination holds (owner, Q-3; read in `adjOutWrite.record`). `adjOutSameOwner` ignores it too.
3a. NEW, announcement (owner, Q-3). Same writer, same staging and same recovery-owner check as step 3. A forwarded announcement (`adjOutForwarded`, either rail, bgp-rs or bgp-rr) or an initial-replay announcement (`adjOutInitial`) for a key, on a destination that did not negotiate ADD-PATH send for the family, while a recovery owner is registered:
   - THE CONTESTED BIT (round 11, I-3, replacing R-1's "the writer cannot know the candidate count"). `adjRIBOutRoute` (`adj_rib_out.go`, the per-key entry beside `owner`, `revision`, `signature`, `exactNLRI`) gains one sticky `contested` bool. The writer does not need a candidate count, only "has any other candidate reached this writer for this key since the entry was installed", and that is one bit, not an inventory of candidate identities. It is SET, staged like every other effect of `allow` (step 3), when a source-bound announcement (forwarded or `adjOutInitial`) meets the entry with an owner identity `adjOutSameOwner` does not match, when any source-bound announcement meets an owed record or a tombstone (below), and when a recovery write installs a candidate; it is maintained whether or not a recovery owner is registered, so the A-5 window keeps it true. It is never cleared while the entry lives, and dies with the entry. A TOMBSTONE (round 11, I-4) is the bit outliving its entry: a key-only set on `adjRIBOut` beside `routes` and the owed set, cleared at all three sites that clear `routes` (round 13, N-6, read at the producers): `adjRIBOut.reset` (teardown, `Peer.clearEncodingContexts`), `Session.beginAdjOut`'s inline clear on a session change (`s.adjOut.routes = nil` when `adjOut.session != s`), and `Session.retireWrite`'s inline clear on a write failure. Only a NON-ADD-PATH destination creates one (`w.key.addPath` false, round 13, N-6): only this step's non-ADD-PATH rule reads the set, so a tombstone at an ADD-PATH destination would be dead state. When a write at a non-ADD-PATH destination deletes a SOURCE-OWNED entry (`entry.owner.source` set), contested or not, outside a recovery decision (`recordSection`'s withdraw branch, `delete(w.table.routes, key)`, for an originated withdrawal, or for an owner withdrawal or lifecycle removal written bare in A-5's window), the key enters the tombstone set (round 12, I-1 applied to the two deletion exits: round 11 tombstoned only a contested entry, but a clear bit cannot prove that no second candidate is in flight to this writer or was dropped on a generation move, and that candidate would then meet no entry and be written with no election); a recovery decision's own withdrawal leaves none, because no candidate the destination may receive remained at the round's drain. A tombstone is removed when an entry is installed for the key and when a round decides the key WITHDRAW. A forwarded withdrawal that meets no entry and no record leaves it in place (`allow` returns `owner.synthesized` before any table effect), so a tombstone whose prefix never returns lives until one of the three clears above. BOUND AND LIFETIME (round 12, N-4): the set holds one key per prefix, insertion is idempotent, and only two deletions create one, an originated withdrawal of a source-owned key and a bare owner withdrawal or lifecycle removal in A-5's window, so it never exceeds the distinct keys deleted that way in one session; that is per-prefix state with a session lifetime, not per-event growth. The tombstone lookup gets the owed set's emptiness shortcut (round-2 N-9): a write first checks that the set is empty, and only a non-empty set costs a per-NLRI lookup. Cost: one bool per entry and one key per tombstoned prefix, no allocation on the write path beyond the staged key;
   - when the destination's table holds NO entry, NO owed record and NO tombstone for the key, it is written as today and installs its owner with the bit clear. It is the only route this destination holds for the prefix, and every other candidate the destination may receive reaches this writer through its own forward, relay or replay, where it meets the next bullets, so the destination ends on the election whatever the arrival order. This keeps the zero-copy cached write for the first route of every prefix (the full-table initial sync included);
   - when the table holds an entry whose owner identity EQUALS the announcement's (`adjOutSameOwner`), whose contested bit is CLEAR, and no owed record, it is written zero-copy as today, and the entry's generation and received message ID are updated (round 11, I-3). This is the sole source re-announcing its own prefix, the dominant IXP churn R-1 names: no other candidate ever reached this writer, so the newest revision of the only candidate is the election. Its correctness rests on exactly A-14's premise, which the first-arrival bullet already uses: a second candidate must cross this writer, where it sets the bit. A candidate still in flight is safe for this half, because it arrives later, meets the entry and contests it. The bit does NOT answer a withdrawal (round 12, I-1): an owner withdrawal (step 3 (a)) or lifecycle removal (step 3 (b)) is owed whatever the bit says, because a bare one deletes the very entry an in-flight or forward-dropped candidate would have contested (step 3). A single-source prefix's withdrawal therefore pays one round that decides WITHDRAW (R-1);
   - when the table holds an entry with the bit SET, or an entry another source owns, or an owed record, or a tombstone, it is not emitted: the key is owed with an EMPTY excluded-source set, or merged into the existing record, exactly as an owner withdrawal is, the bit is set, and the entry (if any) keeps its owner until recovery writes. So the announcing source's own path is covered too once the key is contested: an owner's re-announcement with worse attributes must yield a better route of another source. No attribute is copied. The record holds an NLRI copy that the round needs only for a WITHDRAW answer (every candidate gone by the time of the round), and for a record an announcement CREATES that copy is taken in WITHDRAWAL framing (round 11, I-6), not copied from the announcement `raw` that `allow` filters: the owed withdrawal is written into MP_UNREACH_NLRI by `queueRecoveryWithdrawal` -> `buildRelayWithdrawal`, which decodes the stored NLRI verbatim, and for a labelled family (SAFI 4 labelled unicast, SAFI 128 VPN) the announcement carries the label stack where RFC 8277 Section 2.4 says "Upon transmission, the Compatibility field SHOULD be set to 0x800000." The owe therefore writes the key's withdrawal NLRI once into the record's own copy: the path identifier as announced, the length byte recomputed, the label stack replaced by the 3-octet Compatibility 0x800000, the RD and the prefix with its host bits cleared, as the native key holds it (round 13, N-2: `keyLabeled` masks the trailing bits); every other family's announcement and withdrawal framing are the same bytes and are copied as they are. ONE DECLARATION OF THE LABELLED WITHDRAWAL FRAMING (round 12, I-2, read at the producers). HEAD already states this fact once: bgp-rib's `recoverySentNLRI` (`plugins/rib/rib_recovery.go`), which feeds both RIB answer paths (the departure ADD-PATH answer and the per-key answer), builds `[path-id?][bits+24][0x80 0x00 0x00][RD?][prefix]` from the native key behind its own `fam.SAFI != SAFIMPLSLabel && fam.SAFI != SAFIVPN` test, and nothing compares its bytes with the writer's for the same key. The labelled-versus-Compatibility knowledge for both SAFIs lives in the registered key functions (`keyLabeled`, and `keyVPN` over it, in `nlrisplit/prefix_key.go`, each taking a `withdraw` flag), not in `GetWithdraw`: only SAFI 4 registers a separate `withdrawalSplitters` entry (`splitVPN`), and SAFI 128 uses `splitVPN` for both framings. So the conversion is declared once in `nlrisplit/prefix_key.go`, beside `keyLabeled` / `keyVPN` (round 13, I-3, replacing round 12's key-only inverse as the sole entry point). ONE unexported step writes one withdrawal NLRI from a prefix length in bits and the bytes that follow the label field (RD and prefix): `[bits+24][0x80 0x00 0x00][RD?][prefix]`, the host bits of the last prefix octet cleared in the destination as `keyLabeled` clears them, RFC 8277 Section 2.4 quoted above the statement that writes the field. That step is the one declaration of the framing, and two entry points are built on it. (1) THE FIELD-LEVEL WRITER, `WithdrawalWriter`, a function type registered per family in a `withdrawalWriters` map beside `withdrawalSplitters` and read through `GetWithdrawalWriter`, which answers nil for every family with no registration: given one MP NLRI field (every NLRI of one attribute), the ADD-PATH flag of the session that framed it, whether the field is announcement or withdrawal framed (the same `withdraw` flag `keyLabeled` takes) and the caller's destination slice, it loops over the field itself, with NO callback: per entry it copies the 4-octet path identifier when ADD-PATH, frames the entry by its length octet, finds the end of the label field, by the bottom-of-stack walk for an announcement and as the one 3-octet field for a withdrawal, and calls the step with the RD and prefix still in place in the source, so each NLRI changes form once, announcement to withdrawal, with no key in between (round 13, N-2). It returns the octets written, never writes past the destination's length, and refuses every entry `keyLabeled` / `keyVPN` refuse and an entry its length octet overruns. The label walk and the entry framing are not written a second time: `keyLabeled`'s loop moves into one unexported label-field step that `keyLabeled` and the writer both call, and `splitByBitLength`'s per-entry length check moves into one unexported entry step that `splitByBitLength` and the writer both call. Registered for IPv4 and IPv6 SAFI 4 and SAFI 128. (2) THE PER-KEY INVERSE, `WithdrawalNLRIFromKey`: given a family, the native key `[bits][RD?][prefix]` and a destination slice, it calls the step with the key's first octet and the rest of the key; it answers `ErrUnsupported` for a family `GetWithdrawalWriter` answers nil for, so the set of converting families is the one map and is not declared twice, and that answer tells the caller its stored NLRI is already the withdrawal framing. The path identifier stays the caller's, because the key excludes it. The reactor calls the per-key inverse on the owe from the writer's `adjOutKey` (`w.key` -> `fwdPathKeyFor`, which holds the native key from `GetPrefixKey`), and bgp-rib's `recoverySentNLRI` calls it from `key.Native`: its hand-coded SAFI test and byte layout are DELETED and replaced by the call (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`), so the RIB-answered ADD-PATH identifier withdrawal and the record's owed withdrawal are produced by one function. It runs on the owe and on the RIB's recovery answer, both cold paths (one per key, no hot-path allocation beyond the record copy step 3 already makes). The forward rail's withdrawal conversion, `buildWithdrawalPayload` -> `writeWithdrawal` (`reactor/forward_build.go`), is a third place the same fact is owed and calls the field-level writer (step 3b), the per-UPDATE path, where the key-only inverse would cost a sizing walk, a key in scratch and a closure through the registered splitter (round 13, I-3). A record created by a withdrawal already holds withdrawal framing (step 3). An `adjOutInitial` announcement for a key with an entry, refused at HEAD, is owed by the same bullets as a forwarded one when the key is contested, owed or tombstoned, or the entry has another owner; from the entry's own owner with the bit clear it keeps HEAD's refusal and writes nothing, because the ribOut replay only replays an entry whose received revision is still current in bgp-rib's Adj-RIB-In at collection (`sentSourceCurrent` compares the message ID for equality), so the entry holds that revision or another revision of the same source (a later live forward, or the relay's earlier one when bgp-adj-rib-in lags bgp-rib), and the refusal is HEAD's either way (round 11, I-1, justification at the real producer; round 12, N-2): its producer is bgp-rib's `ribOut` replay to a reconnecting client, which races that client's bgp-adj-rib-in relay on the forwarded rail. The simpler design, keeping HEAD's refusal unchanged, converges for an entry another source owns, because that candidate also arrives by the relay (A-14), and it is not taken for two cases: with a record or a tombstone and NO entry, the refusal's `entry != nil` test admits the replay and installs an owner no election chose, a direct write the I-4 tombstone exists to stop; and with a foreign-owned or contested entry, refusing drops a candidate the writer must mark (it sets the contested bit). The uncontested own-owner case keeps the refusal, so the change is confined to the keys where the replay is one candidate among several. An `adjOutInitial` withdrawal stays refused as today;
   - a forwarded announcement adds its source X and X's session generation (`receivedGeneration`, current by `beginAdjOut`'s check) to the record's CURRENT-ANNOUNCER set, which, unlike the movers set, is not cleared at snapshot and dies with the record. At snapshot the round's request excludes, per key, the record's excluded sources MINUS every source with a current-announcer entry whose generation `forwardSourceCurrent` still accepts, and an announcement from a source that is in the excluded-source set moves the epoch. The order of the two events does not matter: X's departure merging after X's reconnected session announced the key (AC-18, "reached C first") and the announcement merging after the departure both leave X electable, and a later departure of the announcing session makes its entry non-current again, so X is excluded once more. X was excluded because an earlier session departed or because its candidate could not be resolved (step 7); its current session's route for the key, applied in bgp-rib by the round's drain, is a valid candidate, and bgp-rib already released or replaced X's earlier paths for the key unless GR retained them, in which case RFC 4724 Section 4.2 makes them candidates too ("MUST NOT differentiate between stale and other routing information during forwarding"). ORDER (round 11, N-3): the subtraction is applied first, at snapshot, to the record's excluded-source set (which holds the departures and the `errRelayNoSource` re-owes, both subject to it), and the round's own exclusions are added after it and are never subtracted: a source whose publication failed or stopped (step 5, added for this round's request after the drain) stays excluded even when a current-announcer entry names it, because a sealed but still Established X passes `forwardSourceCurrent` (step 5) and subtracting it would re-admit X's known-invalid Adj-RIB-In;
   - the round elects as for a withdrawal key (steps 5, 6, 8), and the admission writes NOTHING when the first candidate that passes egress is the route the destination already holds: the entry's owner identity (source, received path identifier, ADD-PATH flag, the `adjOutSameOwner` fields) and its stored received message ID equal the candidate's. That outcome is DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE (step 7). So a worse later route from another source writes nothing to the destination, and the RFC 4271 Section 9.2 "newly installed routes" UPDATE is sent only when the installed route changed.
