The test is exact for every owner-installing write: forwarded, fast-path, relay and recovery writes carry the reactor's received message ID, and bgp-rib stores the same `msg.MessageID`; an `adjOutInitial` write carries the ribOut entry's `SourceMessageID` (step 3, round 11, I-2). Nothing to write never drops a mover: when an ANNOUNCE mover was recorded for the key, the key is RE-OWED whatever the decision wrote (step 7, round 11, B-2).
   An originated write (no sent-owner authority) keeps today's rule (step 7, first bullet). RECOVERY WRITES CARRY THEIR OWN AUTHORITY (round 11, B-1, read at the producer). At HEAD `forwardUpdateSection` (`reactor_api_forward.go`) builds every section with `item.session, item.authority = session, adjOutForwarded` and relabels a recovery section `adjOutRecovery` only when it is a withdrawal or a synthesized withdrawal (`if srcInfo.recovery != nil { if mods.IsWithdraw() || ...synthesizedWithdrawal { item.authority = adjOutRecovery } }`), and `fwdBatchHandler`'s recovery branch writes each with `session.sentForward = item`. Under this step a survivor written as `adjOutForwarded` would meet the old owner's entry and its record, be merged and noted as an ANNOUNCE mover, and the admission would report a write that never happened, every round, with zero writes. So `forwardUpdateSection` labels EVERY section of a recovery operation `adjOutRecovery` (`srcInfo.recovery != nil`, announcements included; the withdrawal and synthesized-withdrawal cases keep their label), and `allow`'s `case adjOutRecovery`, which at HEAD handles only `withdraw`, gains its announcement branch: it installs the candidate as owner (the `writePath` source, received path identifier and ADD-PATH flag) with its generation (`receivedGeneration`) and its received message ID (`sourceMessageID`, the R-12 chain), sets the contested bit and removes any tombstone; it first REFUSES an unattributed candidate, `owner.source == nil` -> `errAdjOutProvenance`, the guard HEAD's `case adjOutForwarded` applies to the same write today (round 12, I-3: `writePath` returns `adjOutPath{source: item.receivedPeer}` when provenance is nil, so a survivor section with a nil `receivedPeer` would otherwise install an originated-looking owner silently where HEAD fails the write; the guard moves with the relabel, Guards of deleted code); it is never owed, notes no mover, touches no current-announcer entry and stages no owed-refused count. The `beginAdjOut` check `item.authority == adjOutForwarded && !forwardSourceCurrent(...)` (`session_ownership.go`) no longer sees a recovery announcement, and that drops no source check: `fwdBatchHandler`'s recovery branch checks `forwardSourceCurrent` under `writeMu` before it writes and re-owes the key on a lost source (step 7), which is the per-key outcome the admission needs, where a `beginAdjOut` refusal would not be. The readers of `authority` (round 12, N-1, grep of `authority` over the reactor's non-test files, 2026-10-13; `relay_recovery.go`'s withdrawal item SETS `adjOutRecovery` and reads nothing) and why none changes meaning for a recovery announcement: `beginAdjOut` (`== adjOutUnspecified`, `== adjOutForwarded`, `== adjOutInitial`: the relabel takes the section out of the `adjOutForwarded` test, covered by `fwdBatchHandler` above); `adjOutWrite.sourceBound` (`!= adjOutUnspecified`: still source-bound); `allow`'s switch (the new branch); `recordSection` (`!= adjOutExpected`, `== adjOutInitial`: a recovery section answers as a forwarded one does); `fwdItem.writePath` (`== adjOutExpected`, `== adjOutInitial`: neither, so the `receivedPeer` / provenance branches answer as for a forwarded section); `session_write.go` (`== adjOutInitial`: no); `fwdBatchHandler` (`== adjOutInitial`, `!= adjOutExpected`: the per-section source check still runs); the overflow supersede (`w.overflow[i].authority != item.authority`: a recovery section has `supersedeKey = 0`, so the loop never runs for it); and `forward_bucket.go`'s merge (`== adjOutUnspecified && recovery == nil`: a recovery section has `recovery != nil`, so it never merges). An ADD-PATH destination never owes an announcement. The fast path's handled-and-delivered rule of step 3 (round-2 N-6) covers an UPDATE whose only content for the destination is owed announcements, and the owed NLRI is removed from the body as an owed withdrawal is. RFC 4271 Section 4.3's same-body rule (step 3) still applies: an announcement that cancels a staged owe in the same body is itself subject to this step.
3b. NEW, the forward rail's converted withdrawal in labelled framing (round 12, I-2, defect read at the producer, journaled in `plan/journal/guard-added-to-one-half-of-a-pair.md`). At HEAD `fwdWithdrawal.of` -> `buildWithdrawalPayload` -> `writeWithdrawal` (`reactor/forward_build.go`) builds the withdrawal a withheld or policy-denied destination is sent from the source's own UPDATE, and `findWithdrawalMPHalves` hands it the MP_REACH_NLRI NLRI field, which `writeWithdrawal` copies verbatim after the MP_UNREACH_NLRI's AFI/SAFI (`w += copy(buf[w:], mp.reach)`), with no per-family conversion. For SAFI 4 and SAFI 128 that withdrawal carries the announcement's label stack where RFC 8277 Section 2.4 reads "Upon transmission, the Compatibility field SHOULD be set to 0x800000." With a multi-label stack a receiver that frames the withdrawal per Section 2.4 (one 3-octet field) reads the extra labels as prefix bits, so it withdraws a different prefix, or, where the result exceeds the family's address length (an IPv4 prefix past 32 bits), meets a malformed NLRI; that is the defect this step removes; with one label it is the SHOULD alone, since the receiver ignores the field ("Upon reception, the value of the Compatibility field MUST be ignored."). This spec's own design hands this rail every policy-denied and withheld destination it hands today, so the conversion goes through the step 3a function and no third copy is written. Behavior after this spec (round 13, I-3, N-3, N-4, N-5):
   1. SIGNATURE. `buildWithdrawalPayload` gains one parameter, the SOURCE encoding context; `fwdWithdrawal.of` passes `bgpctx.Registry.Get(base.SourceCtxID())`, and its two callers, `reactor_api_forward.go` (`withdrawals.of(update, peerWire)` in the forward fan-out) and `forward_rs.go` (the same call in `reactorForwardRS`), are unchanged. `writeWithdrawal` takes the family's writer and the ADD-PATH flag from it. `buildWithdrawalPayload` itself has four direct test callers, each over a unicast payload, so each passes a `nil` context (step 2: a family with no writer never reads it): `forward_build_withdraw_merge_test.go` (`withdrawalOf`), `session_ownership_writer_test.go`, and the two RFC-tagged units `rfc7311_processing_test.go::TestAIGPForwardedMetricsReachFinalWire` and `rfc7311_direct_link_test.go::TestRFC7311DirectLinkCostNeverWrapsOrDisappears` (round 14, I-1; their change is the signature only, see "RFC-tagged tests that change (AC-3)").
   2. FAMILY. `buildWithdrawalPayload` reads the family from `mp.afiSAFI` and asks `nlrisplit.GetWithdrawalWriter`. For a family with no writer, nothing below runs and every byte is HEAD's: the context is not read.
   3. CONTEXT. For a family with a writer, a nil context (an unregistered ID, `ContextRegistry.Get` returns nil) makes `buildWithdrawalPayload` return 0, checked before `EncodingContext.AddPath`, which answers false on a nil receiver and would frame an ADD-PATH field as plain (`ai/rules/principles.md`: code that cannot answer says so). Otherwise the ADD-PATH flag is `AddPath(fam)` of the source context, the source's receive state, as `adjOutWrite.key` reads the destination's.
   4. HEADER, NO SIZING WALK (N-4, skip-and-backfill, `ai/rules/performance.md`). The MP_UNREACH header size and the `need` check are computed from the UNCONVERTED length, `3 + len(mp.unreach) + len(mp.reach)`, exactly as at HEAD. Then the AFI/SAFI and both halves are written, and the two length fields (the path attribute total and the MP_UNREACH attribute length) are written last from the octets actually written. This is one mechanism for every family, not a labelled branch: for a family with no writer the converted length equals the bound, so the header and both lengths are HEAD's bytes. For a labelled field the converted length is never longer than the bound (one or more 3-octet labels become one 3-octet field), so the header the bound chose holds it, the `need` check against the bound stays sufficient and the `len(payload)+4` buffer contract of `of` is unchanged. Where the bound exceeds 255 and the converted value does not, the extended-length header carries a value under 256: RFC 4271 Section 4.3 defines the Extended Length bit as "whether the Attribute Length is one octet (if set to 0) or two octets (if set to 1)", and no sentence requires the shorter form. Receivers accept it: FRR 10.3.1's own writer, `bgp_packet_mpunreach_start`, always writes MP_UNREACH_NLRI with the Extended Length bit set ("Set extended bit always to encode the attribute length as 2 bytes"), and its `bgp_attr_parse` reads the two-octet length whenever the bit is set and checks only the bounds (round 14, N-3).
   5. BOTH HALVES CONVERTED (N-5, decided: normalize). The MP_UNREACH field the source carried goes through the writer with `withdraw` true, which keeps each entry's length octet and writes 0x800000 over its Compatibility field; the MP_REACH field goes through it with `withdraw` false. RFC 8277 Section 2.4's "Upon transmission, the Compatibility field SHOULD be set to 0x800000." binds ze's transmission of both, and the same writer pays for both, so no deviation is recorded.
   6. ALLOCATION. No closure is built and no callback is passed: the writer is a plain function value from the registry, its arguments are slices of the source payload and of the pool buffer `of` took, both already heap memory, and it loops internally, so the call allocates nothing (`ai/rules/performance.md`, per-UPDATE path). `TestFwdWithdrawalLabelledCompatibility` asserts `testing.AllocsPerRun` = 0 over `buildWithdrawalPayload` for both labelled families with and without ADD-PATH, `TestWithdrawalWriterField` asserts it over the writer alone, and `BenchmarkBuildWithdrawalPayloadLabelled` (`reactor/forward_build_test.go`, `b.ReportAllocs`) reports 0 allocs/op. A claim that the indirect call does not move a stack value to the heap is checked with `-gcflags=-m` on the implementing commit and the line cited in the spec's Evidence. Rule (round 14, N-4): no stack-local buffer is ever passed to the writer, because a stack array passed across the function-value call escapes ("moved to heap") and allocates once per call (round-14 probe: 0 allocs with heap slices, 1 with a stack `[64]byte`); a scratch the writer needs lives inside it, where a direct callee such as `keyLabeled` does not escape.
   7. REFUSAL. An entry the writer refuses makes `buildWithdrawalPayload` return 0, which `fwdWithdrawal.of` already answers with nil, and the caller logs "withdrawal conversion failed, suppressing route", the same refusal as any malformed payload today.
   AC-27, `TestFwdWithdrawalLabelledCompatibility`, `TestWithdrawalWriterField` and `bgp-vpn-withheld-withdraw-frr` hold it.
4. NEW, departure: the departure command (bgp-rs `recoverRouteSel`, and now bgp-rr `handleStateDown`, both with `recovery-source` meta) no longer runs `recoverNLRIBatch`'s inline per-destination loop. For each selected destination and each key, under that destination's adj-out table mutex, it creates or extends an owed record ONLY when the adj-out entry's owner is the departed source AND that owner's session is no longer current, meaning `forwardSourceCurrent(entry.owner.source, entry.owner.generation)` is false (round-3 N-6). Where another source owns the key, where the departed source's reconnected session owns it (its generation is current, so its newer route stays), or where no entry exists, nothing is owed and nothing is written (round-2 B-1, AC-18). Because `forwardGeneration` moves on every Established transition and on `Peer.Stop`, the rule also owes the keys of every EARLIER session of that source, such as a GR-retained route from before a reconnect that the new session never re-announced. No message-ID cut is compared: the generation is read under the table mutex every write takes, so the test is exact where the round-2 same-source cut was a RIB-side reading after a drain, and `recovery-cut` is deleted end to end (Files to Modify). At an ADD-PATH destination the departure command names received identities, not the destination's identifiers, so the record is owed per prefix there and the per-identifier owner test runs at admission (steps 6 and 7). Creating or extending adds the departed source to the key's excluded-source set; a key already owed (by an owner withdrawal, a lifecycle removal or another departure) keeps one record, its excluded-source set becomes the union, and its epoch moves (round-2 I-3). Each owe or merge wakes the destination's worker under step 3's one wake rule (round 7, I-3), so the departure command never waits on keys no round will start for. A departure that arrives while no recovery owner is registered keeps today's outcome: each selected destination is retired through AutomaticStop/Cease with the cause logged (the registry check at the top of `recoverNLRIBatch` today; AC-11; round-3 N-7), because the plugin asked for an election no process can run. The command then waits for completion of the keys it owed, so its synchronous contract with the plugin holds. Cancelling that wait only stops the waiter; the keys stay owed and the worker still decides them. EVERY removal of a record completes its waiters: a decision at admission (step 8), a removal by an originated announcement, the only announcement that installs a new owner over a record (step 7; a forwarded or initial one merges, AC-18), and a table reset, which completes them with "session retired" (Integration Points; round-2 I-5, round-3 N-8).
5. NEW, per-destination recovery worker (one long-lived goroutine per destination session, never per event; lifecycle in Integration Points). In ONE table-mutex section it takes a snapshot of the owed set (keys, excluded-source sets, epochs) and clears each snapshotted key's movers set. Only after that section does it capture the source receipts and publication cuts (`recoverySources`) and drain them (`drainRecoverySources`), then `DrainEventsApplied`. This order is a constraint (round-3 N-12): were the cuts captured first, a withdrawal landing between the capture and the movers clear would be neither drained nor recorded. There is no shared reactor-wide drain (round-2 I-1): the owed set already coalesces every key owed while a round runs into the next round, so a burst of withdrawals costs one drain per destination per round, not one per withdrawal. A source X whose publication stopped or failed before reaching its cut (`errReceivePublicationStopped`, `errReceivePublicationFailed` from `receivePublicationCut.wait`) does not fail the round: `drainRecoverySources` waits on every other source and returns the set of such sources instead of the first error, and the round adds each of them to the excluded-source set of EVERY key it asks about (round 5, B-1). The addition is for this round's request only: the owed record is not changed and no epoch moves. For a failure the round's drain observes, X's withdrawals that never reached bgp-rib therefore cannot make X's withdrawn path electable, whoever X is, the chosen survivor included, and correctness does not depend on how fast X's teardown runs: `resolveRelaySource` and `forwardSourceCurrent` still accept X while the FSM keeps it Established after `Session.teardownAutomatic` has sealed it, so the admission's source check alone would not stop the write. The exclusion is exact for what the drain sees: `wait` returns an error only for the source it waited on and checks `failed` before `completed`, so a failure after the cut is excluded too (conservative). A failure after `drainRecoverySources` returns is outside this claim (round-6 N-1): the round may elect X's withdrawn path, and on the cached rail the unpublished withdrawal never reaches C's writer, so no mover is recorded; C then heals through X's teardown and departure (Phase 2b, step 4), the same exposure as any route X forwarded. `failed` is sticky for the session, so every later round of that session excludes X again until X's session ends. A stopped publication belongs to a session that has ENDED: `errReceivePublicationStopped` needs `publication.done` closed, which happens only after a panic (failed) or after `runOnce`'s down defer closes `deliverChan` once `Session.Run` has returned or unwound. X's own departure then decides every key X owns at this destination (step 4). The delivery worker's three failure paths in `peer_run.go` (a recovered panic, a nil reactor, a nil message receiver) call `Session.teardownAutomatic` with Cease / Out of Resources on the session they serve (round-3 I-1, Phase 2b). Rounds after that teardown read the RIB with X down, so they depend on X's routes not being RETAINED: X's Adj-RIB-In is known-invalid (UPDATEs whose publication failed, withdrawals included, never reached bgp-rib). RFC 4724 Section 4 says "the normal BGP procedures MUST be followed when the TCP session terminates due to the sending or receiving of a BGP NOTIFICATION message", but at HEAD the reactor reports the down reason as "session closed" / "connection lost" and `gr.go` retains X's GR families unless the reason is "notification" (D1 of `spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md`). D1 alone does not close it: D1 reads `notificationExchanged`, which `recordNotificationUnsent` leaves false when the Cease is built but not written (round 5, I-2). So D1's reason producer reads two inputs (Phase 2b; round-6 I-2), and the second is the "Adj-RIB-In invalid" fact, which already exists: `receivePublication.failed`, an `atomic.Bool` (`receive_publication.go`) that every `publication.fail()` site sets (the delivery worker's three sites in `peer_run.go`, and the read goroutine's deferred `fail()` in `notifyMessageReceiver` for an accepted UPDATE that never reached `deliverChan`), sticky for the session, one per `runOnce`. No second flag is declared beside it (round 7, N-4: the fact is an atomic, as `notificationExchanged` is, because the worker writes it and another goroutine reads it). THE DOWN EVENT FOLLOWS THE SESSION'S BUFFERED DELIVERIES AND ITS LEAVE TRANSITION (round 7, I-2, option b; round 8, B-1 and I-3; read at the producers). At HEAD the FSM leave-Established callback computes the reason and calls `announceClosed` -> `notifyPeerClosed` while the delivery worker may still hold up to `deliveryChannelCapacity` (256) accepted UPDATEs; `runOnce` closes `deliverChan` and waits `<-deliveryDone` only after `Session.Run` returns. That callback does not run inside `Session.Run` as such (round 8, B-1, correcting round 7): `fsm.change` runs each callback on whichever goroutine is draining the FSM's pending queue, and a goroutine that finds a drainer active only enqueues and returns, it never waits. So the callback runs on whichever goroutine fires or drains the event: the read goroutine on a remote close (`handleConnectionClose`); a teardown's caller, because `Session.teardown` calls `closeConn` before `logFSMEvent`, so on a local Cease (`stopWithCease`, operator teardown) the teardown goroutine usually wins Established -> Idle and the read goroutine's later `EventTCPConnectionFails` finds Idle, which RFC 4271 Section 8.2.2 ignores ("Any other event (Events 9-12, 15-28) received in the Idle state does not cause change in the state of the local system."); the recovery worker for AC-11 (`teardownAutomatic`); the delivery worker for Phase 2b; the hold and send-hold timers, which send the NOTIFICATION before they fire the event; and the BFD subscriber goroutine (`runBFDSubscriber` -> `deliverBFDEvent` -> `Session.handleBFDEvent` -> `bfdTeardown` -> `Session.teardown`) on a BFD Down in Established, which wins the drain easily, because `teardown` calls `closeConn` before `logFSMEvent` while the read goroutine must still wake from a blocked read (round 9, B-1); the destination's own forward-pool worker, whose post-batch `checkTeardown` runs `congestionTeardownPeer` on grace expiry, firing `EventTCPConnectionFails` directly after `closeConn` on its GR branch and going through `peer.teardownAutomatic` on its non-GR branch; and the one-shot collision goroutine (`CloseWithNotification(Cease, ConnectionCollision)` -> `logFSMEvent(EventOpenCollisionDump)`, which `handleEstablished` maps to Idle) when `detectCollision` read OpenConfirm and the session reached Established before that goroutine ran (round 10, I-1). A failure in the delivery tail (a recovered panic on a buffered item), or an unrelated close (a TCP read error) that runs the callback before the worker reaches its `fail()`, would put the fact after the reason had already gone out, and GR would retain X with withdrawals missing. So the reason producer and the down event move out of the callback into a `runOnce` DEFER, the DOWN DEFER, registered right after the delivery worker starts. Defers run last-in first-out, so it runs before the existing `runOnce` defer that calls `clearEncodingContexts`, stops and joins the recovery worker and sets `upSession = nil`; and because it is a defer, it also runs when a panic on the read goroutine unwinds `Session.Run` and `runOnce` on its way to `safeRunOnce`'s `recover` (round 8, I-3). That claim holds only for a panic that leaves no lock held (round 9, I-3, read at the producers): `reactorForwardRSSection` (`forward_rs.go`) holds `r.mu.RLock()` over its peer scan, and `sourcePeer.mu.RLock()` before it, each released by an explicit, non-deferred unlock, and `notifyMessageReceiver` (`reactor_notify.go`) holds `r.mu.RLock()` the same way over its PeerInfo build (`updateDelayEndOfRIB`, `health.onEORReceived`, `fwdWeights.PeerEORReceived`). A panic inside such a span leaves the lock held; step 1's leave callback then blocks in `markSessionUp` on `r.mu.Lock()` for good, and the waiting writer blocks every later `r.mu.RLock` in the reactor. At HEAD the same panic wedges the reactor one step later: `runOnce`'s only defer takes `p.mu` and leaf locks (`clearEncodingContexts`, `releaseRouterIDClaim`), never `r.mu`, so `runOnce` returns, and the next `r.mu` writer (the next session's `announceEstablished` -> `markSessionUp`) blocks forever. Both outcomes are fatal; this spec does not make the defer survive a leaked lock, and claims nothing for that case. In order, the down defer:
   1. LEAVES ESTABLISHED WHEN NOTHING ELSE DID. When the session's FSM still reads Established, the defer fires `EventTCPConnectionFails` itself, the event `handleConnectionClose` fires, so the leave callback and its bookkeeping run before anything below. `Session.Run` returns with the FSM still Established on a body-read error (`read body`), on a header read error that is neither EOF nor a reset, on `waitForResume` returning on cancel, and on a read-goroutine panic; at HEAD such a session emits no down event at all. When another goroutine's teardown moves the FSM between the read and the event, the event finds Idle and is ignored, so no second leave is fired. A session whose FSM never reached Established is left as it is.
   2. DRAINS: `close(deliverChan)`, then `<-deliveryDone`, so every buffered item is published or failed before anything below reads the fact.
   3. JOINS THE LEAVE CALLBACK, for a session that entered Established: it waits on the session's LEAVE SIGNAL, a channel created per `runOnce` and closed by the leave-Established arm as its last statement, after `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`. Whether the session entered Established is read from the FSM, which gains one sticky bit written in `change` at the statement that appends the enter-Established transition to `f.pending`, after `change`'s early return for a nil callback or an unchanged state, in the same `f.mu` hold as the state write (one `FSM` per `Session`, one `Session` per `runOnce`; round 9, N-1: a bit written beside the state write, before that return, would be set on a nil-callback FSM that queues nothing, and the join would hang; production cannot reach it, because Established needs `Session.Run`, which runs after `SetCallback`). A flag set in the enter-Established callback would not be exact: that callback can still be pending on another drainer when `Session.Run` returns, so the flag could read false for a session whose leave callback is still to come. The bit cannot: bit set means the enter transition is queued, step 1 means the FSM is no longer Established, so a leave transition is queued after it, and the FIFO drainer runs its callback, which closes the signal. The join never hangs, outside a panic that leaked a lock (above, round 9, I-3), because the leave arm waits on no goroutine that can be its own drainer (round 9, B-1): the drainers are the read goroutine, a teardown's caller, the recovery worker, the delivery worker, the hold and send-hold timers, the BFD subscriber, the destination's own FORWARD-POOL WORKER (round 10, I-1: after each batch the worker loop calls `fp.congestion.checkTeardown(key.peerAddr)`, whose grace expiry runs `congestionTeardownPeer`; its GR branch does `closeConn` and then calls `session.fsm.Event(EventTCPConnectionFails)` directly, never through `Session.teardown`, and its non-GR branch reaches `Session.teardown` through `peer.teardownAutomatic`), and the one-shot COLLISION GOROUTINE (round 10, I-1: `peer_connection.go` spawns `go func(){ session.CloseWithNotification(Cease, ConnectionCollision) }()`, which runs `logFSMEvent(EventOpenCollisionDump)`, and `handleEstablished` maps that event to Idle; reachable when `detectCollision` read OpenConfirm and the session reached Established before the goroutine ran). The only wait HEAD's arm held, `stopBFDClient`'s `<-done`, waits on the BFD subscriber, so it moves out of the arm (below); the arm waits on none of the drainers. Besides `r.mu`, the arm takes only leaf locks, none held across a wait on a drainer (round 10, N-2, read at the producer): `p.mu`, `bfd.mu`, the BFD engine's `subsMu`, `l.mu` and `runtimeStateGuard` (through `Unsubscribe` and `ReleaseSession`; the engine's fan-out never blocks, `trySendStateChange`), `aigp.dispatch` and `aigp.mu` (`forgetAIGPPeer`), the update-group lock, `adjOut.mu` (`clearEncodingContexts`) and the update-delay lock. `r.mu` meets reactor stop, pre-existing at HEAD (round 9, I-2, corrected in round 10, I-2, read at the producer): `Reactor.cleanup` holds `r.mu` for its whole run, Phase 1 (`fwdPool.Stop`, and in standalone mode the owned `r.api.Stop()`, bounded by `pluginStopGrace` 3 s plus a 500 ms group wait, about 3.5 s), Phase 2's single concurrent 2 s wait for every peer's `Peer.Wait`, and Phase 3 (`recentUpdates.Stop`), so a leave arm that runs after `cleanup` took `r.mu` (a session HEAD ended silently, such as a paused session's `waitForResume` returning on cancel, now fires its leave in step 1; and at HEAD already, `StopForRestart` -> cancel -> `handleConnectionClose`) blocks in `markSessionUp` until `cleanup` returns, for all peers at once, and its down is delivered in step 4 after it. Because that peer's `Peer.Wait` in Phase 2 times out while its arm is blocked, its down lands after `cleanup` returns, which is after `Reactor.Wait` returns. THE FORWARD-WORKER EXCEPTION, not bounded and not this spec's: a forward-pool worker that drains a leave after `cleanup` took `r.mu` blocks in `announceClosed` -> `markSessionUp` -> `r.mu.Lock`, so its `done` never closes, `fwdPool.Stop` (`<-kw.w.done` per worker, no deadline) never returns, and the reactor stop wedges for good. The window is real: a closed worker channel still yields its buffered items, each batch runs `checkTeardown`, and a grace period can expire during shutdown. This is HEAD's defect, journaled in `plan/journal/lock-held-across-a-join.md`, and this spec inherits it without widening it (round 10, I-2, decided at the producer): HEAD's leave arm already calls `announceClosed` -> `markSessionUp` on whichever goroutine drains it, the forward worker included, and this spec keeps `markSessionUp(sessionUpClosing)` in the arm at the same place; the down defer runs on the `runOnce` goroutine, never on a forward worker; and nothing this spec adds to the forward worker drains a leave, because the recovery admission's write failures go through `Session.retireWrite`, which closes the connection and fires no FSM event, so the read goroutine drains that leave. The one difference is that a wedged worker now also holds its peer's down defer on the leave signal, where HEAD's `runOnce` returned; nothing observes it, because `cleanup` never leaves Phase 1 and so never reaches the Phase 2 `Peer.Wait`, and `Reactor.Wait` is held by `monitor` in both cases. Plugin delivery itself does not strand at stop: `Process.Deliver` returns false on `eventClosed` or a done context, and `deliveryLoop` answers every queued `Result`. Then, after the join, the defer waits on the BFD subscriber's `done` channel when the leave arm left one in the leave record (a non-strict peer whose BFD session was open), so the subscriber has exited before step 4 and before the outer defer's `clearEncodingContexts`; this wait runs on the `runOnce` goroutine, which is never the subscriber, and it is bounded (round 10, I-4, read at the producer). After `stop` closes, `runBFDSubscriber`'s `select` picks at random between `<-stop` and a buffered `sub` value, so the subscriber may first consume at most the `SubscribeBuffer` (8) buffered values plus one in-flight `makeNotify` snapshot send (`Unsubscribe` stops new fan-out, and that one send goes through the non-blocking `trySendStateChange`), each calling `deliverBFDEvent` on this session while `p.session` is still set; each is a no-op on the Idle FSM (`bfdTeardown` answers false in Idle, `change` cleared `bfdSub` so nothing advances, and `handleIdle` ignores every BFD event). When another goroutine drained the leave, the subscriber may also be inside its own `deliverBFDEvent`, finishing a `Session.teardown` that lost the drain race, including `closeConn`'s 100 ms read-drain and the NOTIFICATION write, bounded exactly as at HEAD, where the arm waited on the same `done`. `endVanishedBFDSession` returns at once, because `p.bfd.handle` is nil or a newer client's.
   4. PRODUCES THE REASON AND THE DOWN, for a session that entered Established: the producer starts from the reason the leave arm computed ("session closed" or "connection lost") and left in the per-`runOnce` leave record, and reads `notificationExchanged` and `receivePublication.failed`, where every worker write happened before `deliveryDone` closed. Then come, all taking its answer: the down event (`notifyPeerClosed`'s observer loop), the session-dropped report (`raiseSessionDropped`, still only when no NOTIFICATION was exchanged), the "session closed" log line, the leave-Established FSM history row, and `scheduleDynamicPeerCleanup` (round 8, N-3: its `AfterFunc(ConnectRetry)` timer now starts after the session's down was delivered, so the removal down, `rpc.ReasonPeerRemoved`, can never precede it).
   THE DEPARTURE DEPENDS ON THIS ORDER (round 8, B-1). Data Flow 4 owes a key only where `forwardSourceCurrent(entry.owner.source, entry.owner.generation)` is false, which is `!stopping && State() == PeerStateEstablished && generation ==`, and a `Session.teardown` moves no generation (only `Peer.Stop` and an Established transition do). A down that reached bgp-rs or bgp-rr before `setState(PeerStateConnecting)` would find the departed session still current, owe nothing, write nothing, and C would keep the departed source's route for good. Step 3 delivers the down only after the source stopped being current, on every path. The same join keeps `announceClosed`'s contract that the closing mark precedes the down's read of the delivery graph (`applyHotSwappableSettings`), and puts the callback's `updateGroups.Remove` before the outer defer's `clearEncodingContexts` on every path, where at HEAD a contended teardown could reverse them.
   What stays in the leave-Established arm, in HEAD's order: the NON-WAITING half of the BFD release for a non-strict peer (under `bfd.mu`, `canceled` set and `restart` cleared when a start is in flight and the `p.bfd` fields niled; then `live.Store(false)`, `close(stop)`, `Unsubscribe`, `ReleaseSession`; round 9, B-1; round 10, N-1), which puts the subscriber's `done` channel in the leave record instead of waiting on it, so the arm never waits on a goroutine that can be draining it (step 3 waits on it after the join; `stopBFDClient`'s other callers, `applyBFDOptInChange` and `Peer.cleanup`, run on goroutines that are never the subscriber and keep the waiting form; one pre-existing exception, the same at HEAD (round 10, N-3): a reload's `applyBFDOptInChange` can run `startBFDClient` between this release and the arm's `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`, its `State() != Established` re-check misses, and the new subscriber is in no leave record, so it outlives `runOnce` until the next leave or `Peer.cleanup` releases it, touching no encoding context because `deliverBFDEvent` reaches only `p.session`'s FSM, which is Idle, nil or the next session's own), `updateDelayPeerDown`, `markSessionUp(sessionUpClosing)` (its contract is that the down "is being delivered, or was" and reads the graph after the mark at a point no reader can place, so a later read keeps it; `applyHotSwappableSettings` judges a session up only while `State() == PeerStateEstablished` and the phase is `sessionUpDelivered`), the reactor bookkeeping `notifyPeerClosed` does before its observer loop (`forgetAIGPPeer`, `updateGroups.Remove`, which MUST precede `clearEncodingContexts`), `negotiated.Store(nil)`, `clearEncodingContexts` and `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`, so forwarding to the peer stops when it leaves Established, as today; then it stores its reason and its transition (from, to, timestamp) in the leave record and closes the leave signal. It no longer appends the history row: step 4 appends it with the producer's reason, and the row keeps its place, because after the leave every later event of this session finds Idle and appends nothing, and the next session's rows start only after this `runOnce` returns. Between the arm's `setState(PeerStateConnecting)` and step 4 there is a transient window, accepted (round 9, N-2): `State()` reads Connecting while the FSM history still lacks the leave row and `LastStateChange` returns the Established row's timestamp, for as long as the 256-item drain and the plugins' acknowledgements take; the final history is correct, because the leave record carries the transition's own timestamp, `p.history.append` has no other writer, and no other transition of this FSM runs after Idle. `notifyPeerClosed` is split along that line, its observer loop running in step 4. THIS SPEC FIXES A PRE-EXISTING DEFECT, and records no journal row for it: a session can end with no down event at HEAD (the `Session.Run` returns with the FSM still Established named in step 1, and a read-goroutine panic that leaves no lock held, which also leaks the delivery worker on `range deliverChan`). It is code this spec relies on, because the departure and the reason producer run only from that down. IT ALSO FIXES A SECOND HEAD DEFECT (round 9, B-1): a BFD subscriber that drains the leave-Established callback waits in `stopBFDClient` on its own `done` channel, so the subscriber goroutine leaks and the session's down is lost (Current Behavior, `peer_bfd.go`). The goal depends on it: with step 3's join, the same self-wait would hang `runOnce` for good, with no reconnect, no outer defer and no recovery-worker join, and `Reactor.cleanup`'s `Peer.Wait` would wait out its deadline. The main thread records that defect's journal row; this spec fixes it in Phase 2b. A panic inside an FSM callback stays outside this: `change` runs the callback with `f.mu` released, and `Event`'s deferred unlock of the released mutex then throws, which no `recover` catches. ORDERING CHANGE, for every peer, not only a failed one: each plugin now receives a session's buffered UPDATEs BEFORE that session's down event, where at HEAD the down could overtake up to 256 of them on the per-plugin FIFO (`process/delivery.go`). Read at the consumers of the down event: bgp-rs and bgp-rr (`handleStateDown`) start the departure, whose drain waits for the source's publication anyway, so the change removes a wait. They also now see those buffered UPDATEs while the source is still up in their own state, where at HEAD bgp-rs's `processForward` treats an UPDATE from a peer it holds down (`StateSeen && !Up`, `server_withdrawal.go`) as from a down peer; the forwards they send are refused at the reactor, because the source left Established in the callback and `forwardSourceCurrent` fails (`errForwardNoSource`), and every key they enter in bgp-rs's per-source `withdrawals` inventory is decided by the departure that follows (Data Flow 4); GR (`handleStructuredState` / `handleStateEvent`, `onSessionDownDeferred`) retains or releases an Adj-RIB-In that is now complete; bgp-rib applies UPDATEs of a session it has not yet been told is down, where at HEAD an UPDATE of an ended session could follow its down on the same FIFO; BMP (`bmp_events.go`, RFC 7854 Section 3.3 Peer Down on leaving Established) sends that session's Route Monitoring before its Peer Down. No consumer depends on the down arriving before the buffered deliveries. The down event's `State` field (`peer.State().PluginState()` in `apiStateObserver.OnPeerClosed`), read at HEAD before `setState(PeerStateConnecting)` on the uncontended path and racing it on the contended one, now always reads the post-Established state, because step 3 follows `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`. No reader depends on it (round 8, N-6, read at the producers): `apiStateObserver.OnPeerClosed` hands `rpc.SessionStateDown` to the dispatcher explicitly, `getStructuredStateEvent` (`bgp/server/events.go`) copies no `State` from the `PeerInfo`, and neither `appendStateChangeJSON` (`format/text_json.go`) nor `appendPeerJSON` (`format/text.go`) prints it; the other `OnPeerClosed` observers are `callbackAdapter` -> health-revert, which reads only the reason, and MRT, which reads nothing because its `*plugin.PeerInfo` assertion fails (an incidental find routed to the main thread's journal). The delay is bounded by what `runOnce` already waits for at HEAD before it returns and the peer can reconnect: the 256-slot drain and the plugins' delivery of it. The new waits are step 3's join, bounded by the leave callback, which waits on no goroutine (its BFD wait moved out, round 9, B-1) and takes `r.mu` and only leaf locks (round 10, N-2), and step 3's wait on the BFD subscriber, bounded because the arm already closed `stop` and the subscriber's remaining work is at most 8 buffered values plus one in-flight send, each a no-op on the Idle FSM, or its own lost-race teardown as at HEAD (round 10, I-4); at reactor stop the arm's `r.mu` lock waits out `Reactor.cleanup`'s whole `r.mu` hold, Phase 1 (`fwdPool.Stop`, and about 3.5 s for a standalone reactor's owned `r.api.Stop()`), Phase 2's single concurrent 2 s peer wait and Phase 3, which HEAD already has, except a forward-pool worker draining a leave there, which deadlocks with `fwdPool.Stop` at HEAD and after this spec alike (step 3; round 9, I-2; round 10, I-2; `plan/journal/lock-held-across-a-join.md`); a delivery that never returns wedges `runOnce` at HEAD already. The next session's up cannot overtake the down, because the next `runOnce` starts only after this one returns. With `notificationExchanged` set, the reason is D1's `rpc.ReasonNotification`, whether or not the fact is also set (a delivered Cease sets both). With only the fact set, no NOTIFICATION crossed the wire, so reporting "notification" would put a false reason into state events, `show` FSM history and the session-closed log line; the reason is instead a SECOND declared constant beside D1's, `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` (spelled "adj-rib-in invalid", owner Q-6), meaning "Adj-RIB-In invalid, no NOTIFICATION delivered". Beside the two constants (`pkg/plugin/rpc/enums.go`) one declared predicate, `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention`, answers true for exactly the down reasons after which GR MUST NOT retain: those two and `rpc.ReasonPeerRemoved` (round 7, N-1: `onPeerRemoved` releases and does not retain, so a predicate that answered false for a removal would mislead a later caller that trusts its name; `gr.go`'s `removed` branch still runs first and is unchanged, so including it changes no GR behavior), and GR's no-retention test in `handleStructuredState` and `handleStateEvent` calls it instead of comparing a string, so `gr.go` changes in every case and names no reason literal: a second literal compared in `gr.go` would be a hand list beside the declared constants. A normal session end produces neither error: `close(deliverChan)` lets the worker publish every buffered item before `deliveryDone` closes, so `wait` returns nil. The destination is never retired for another source's publication outcome, and the round never retries a failed source's drain, because `failed` is sticky and `wait` would return at once, a hot loop (AC-17). The worker then asks bgp-rib through `relayRecovery`'s existing transports (in-process provider or `request bgp rib recovery`), in requests of at most 500 keys per family (round-3 I-3; the bound bgp-rs already uses for its departure commands, `withdrawalBatchSize`), so a full-table departure costs many short `peerMu` holds in `recoveryRoutes` and many short IPC arguments, not one of each. The chunk bounds a request, never the owed set, so AC-12 is unaffected. Each request names, PER KEY, the key's identity and its excluded-source set; no cut travels (step 4), and the IPC argument list of `recoveryCommand` changes shape accordingly (Integration Points). The in-process `RecoveryOwner.Lookup` takes no context, so a round blocked inside it cannot be interrupted, and the session's join waits for it (Integration Points, round-3 N-4).
6. bgp-rib `recoveryRoutes`, per key: every candidate of each excluded source is skipped, as departure skips the source today; an owner-withdrawal or lifecycle-removal key (empty set) skips none, so the withdrawing source's other received paths stay eligible (AC-9). The DESTINATION's own candidates and down peers are skipped before election (round-1 I2). Election does not require a `ribOut[Destination]` entry for the key (round-2 I-4). The answer for each key is exactly one outcome:
   - SURVIVORS: the remaining candidates in election order, defined as repeated `SelectBest` over the remainder (the first `SelectBest` winner, then `SelectBest` over the rest, and so on), because the decision comparator is not transitive under MED and cannot sort (round-2 N-1). Each candidate carries its source, its received path identifier and its received ADD-PATH flag in new `RecoveryRoute` fields, so the admission compares a mover with the candidate it is about to write without re-deriving the received identity from NLRI framing (round-3 N-5);
   - WITHDRAW: no candidate remains.
   The round-2 KEEP outcome is gone (round-3 N-6). It answered "the destination holds the reconnected source's newer route", and that route is now never owed (step 4), so the RIB is never asked about it; A-7 and the per-source cut go with it.
   A departure key at an ADD-PATH destination keeps today's `request.SentAddPath` branch, without its cut: the answer is WITHDRAW for each of the departed source's sent identifiers at the destination for the prefix, and no election runs. The admission writes only those identifiers whose adj-out entry is still owned by a no-longer-current session of the departed source (step 7), so the reconnected session's identifiers are never withdrawn. An owner withdrawal, a lifecycle removal or an announcement never reaches an ADD-PATH destination's owed set (steps 3 and 3a).
   The RIB no longer answers "nothing" for a key whose sent owner moved: the writer's record is the single authority on ownership (an originated announcement removes it, a forwarded or initial one merges, step 7), so the round-1 I1 RIB check is dropped rather than kept as a second declaration (round-2 I-3). A requested key with no outcome in the answer is a lookup error (AC-11), never read as "no survivor".
7. Admission on the destination's forward worker (the existing `recoveryAdmission` item, dispatched with `dispatchOverflow`): under `writeMu`, per key, the owed record must still be present, belong to this destination session, and carry the epoch captured in step 5. A record already gone (removed by a new owner, a decision, or a table reset between snapshot and admission) is that key's outcome "record already gone", and nothing is left owed. A record whose epoch moved is left owed for the next round, that key alone (no destination-wide retry), and the worker starts that round as soon as the admission completes (step 3, one wake rule). A record of another destination session is unreachable (round 8, N-4, read at the producer): every table reset clears the owed set, `beginAdjOut` clears `routes` and the owed set when the table's session changes, and `runOnce` joins a session's recovery worker before the next session exists. Were one ever met, it is removed and its waiters complete with "session retired", never left owed, so it cannot start rounds forever; the admission reports it as RECORD ALREADY GONE (round 9, N-3), so it is one of the six outcomes and never retires C. THE FORWARD WORKER'S SOURCE FENCE BECOMES PER KEY (round 7, I-3, read at the producer): at HEAD the `fwdBatchHandler` recovery branch walks every section of the admission, sets `recovery.retry = true` and `break`s on the first lost source, which skips every sibling key of the admission, and `releaseItem` then sends `recovery.done <- recovery.retry` back to `relayRecovery`'s retry loop, which this spec deletes. After this spec a lost source re-owes only the key its section belongs to, and the sibling keys of the same admission are still written. `recoveryAdmission.retry`, the `bool` that `recoveryAdmission.done` carries, and the `case retry := <-admission.done` return in `relayRecovery` are deleted, not kept beside the re-owe (no-layering): the admission reports per key, and the FULL per-key outcome set is (round 8, I-2, read at the producer): WRITTEN (a survivor, or the owed withdrawal from the record's copy); DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE (the record is removed and its waiters complete, because the owed withdrawal is due, the outcome being WITHDRAW or SURVIVORS with every candidate egress-denied, and the adj-out entry is already gone, step 8 and case (i), or because no identifier of an ADD-PATH departure key's WITHDRAW answer is still owned by a no-longer-current session of the departed source; round 9, I-1); RECORD ALREADY GONE (removed by a new owner or a table reset between snapshot and admission, cases (b), (c) and (l), or a record of another destination session, removed with "session retired" above; round 9, N-3); LEFT OWED (the epoch moved, or a recorded mover is the candidate the admission was about to write, the MOVER RETRY of fence case (a): the record is untouched and its movers are cleared at the next round's snapshot; round 9, I-1); RE-OWED with a source excluded; or a write ERROR. `releaseItem`'s `errRecoveryNotWritten` check ("not retried and not written") becomes the per-key form: only a key with none of these outcomes is the error, where each DECIDED outcome (WRITTEN, and DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE) may carry the RE-OWED BY A MOVER flag (owner Q-3; round 11, B-2, defined after the mover rule below), and WRITTEN, RE-OWED is WRITTEN with that flag set; a flagged outcome is still a decision, and on it the record is NOT removed and its waiters do NOT complete, which `relayRecovery` treats as a hard failure and which retires the destination (AC-11). At HEAD the whole-admission flag is blind to the second and third outcomes only because `fwdBatchHandler` sets `recovery.written` once per batch after the flush; per key, each of them is a decision and MUST NOT retire C. The comment above HEAD's fence loop, "A generation change MUST retry the whole lookup, never relabel selected bytes", is rewritten to say what the code does after this spec: a generation change re-owes that key with its source excluded, and never relabels selected bytes (`ai/rules/stale-comments.md`). The epoch moves on exactly these events, each of which changes the question asked of the RIB or the routes it answers from (round 11, N-1, listed once): (1) a departure merge into an existing record (step 4); (2) an `errRelayNoSource` re-owe, which adds the survivor's source (below); (3) a source in the excluded-source set entering the current-announcer set (step 3a), because the round then subtracts it; (4) a decided outcome flagged RE-OWED BY A MOVER (below). An owner-withdrawal or lifecycle-removal merge, an announcement merge from a source not in the excluded set, and a WITHDRAW mover move no epoch. Between snapshot and admission the writer applies one rule per authority to an owed key (round-3 B-1; owner Q-3 for the announcement rows):
   - an ORIGINATED announcement (no sent-owner authority, `SendRawMessage` or a local `update-route`) is written, installs the originated owner and REMOVES the record, completing its waiters: an operator's own route for the prefix is not a candidate of the election and is not overridden by it (fence case (n));
   - a forwarded (`adjOutForwarded`) or initial-replay (`adjOutInitial`) announcement for an owed key is NOT written and does NOT remove the record (owner, Q-3, replacing round 2's "newer owner wins"): it merges into the record as step 3a says, and, because the round's drain may predate it, it is noted in the key's movers set as a mover of kind ANNOUNCE with its owner identity (below). This covers the withdrawing owner's own later re-announcement (round-4 N-5): its new route is a candidate of the next round, never refused as "the owner being replaced" and never installed without an election. AC-6 and AC-18 assert the elected route, not the newest one;
   - an `adjOutRecovery` announcement is the admission's own survivor write (step 3a, round 11, B-1): one with no source (`owner.source == nil`) is refused with `errAdjOutProvenance`, as HEAD's `adjOutForwarded` case refuses it (round 12, I-3); otherwise it is written, installs the candidate as owner with its generation and received message ID, sets the contested bit, is never owed, notes no mover and touches no current-announcer entry; the admission then removes the record, or leaves it owed when the outcome is flagged RE-OWED BY A MOVER. A recovery write of ANOTHER admission for the same key cannot exist, because one worker serves the destination session and admits its rounds one at a time (Integration Points);
   - an `adjOutExpected` announcement is REFUSED and leaves the record. It is a bgp-rib replay of `ribOut[Destination]`: ROUTE-REFRESH and `clear bgp rib out`, and also the drain spec's drain replay and its `drain-unjudged` one-entry follow-up replay (`plan/immediate/spec-daemon-drain-before-stop.md`), because `sendBatchUpdate` sets `adjOutExpected` whenever `SentOwnerMessage != 0` (round-4 I-4). While A's withdrawal is owed that entry still names A, because the deferred withdrawal produced no sent event, so it replays the very route being replaced (for the drain, tagged with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN). Admitted, it would remove the record and leave A's withdrawn route installed for good. The recovery write answers the refresh instead (AC-20). The refusal is COUNTED, never silent, and the count has ONE home: a per-peer monotonic atomic on the destination's `Peer` (round 6, B-1, option i, replacing round 5's update-route reply count). `allow` returns a distinct owed-refused outcome, the write stages one count per NLRI so refused, and the staged count is added to the counter ONCE per write, with the write's other staged effects (step 3, round 7 I-1): an owed-refused NLRI is removed, so a replay that also carries an admitted NLRI is filtered twice, and a count taken inside `allow` would add two for it. It is added under `writeMu`, on whichever goroutine performs the write: the command goroutine for a direct receipt write, and C's forward worker for a receipt write that `queueBehindForwards` diverted ("a nil answer means queued"), so a queued write is counted when it is written and no command waits for it. The counter lives on `Peer`, not on `adjRIBOut`, which resets per session; it is never reset, and every reader reads it as a delta between two loads. The command still succeeds, and the update-route reply, `rpc.UpdateRouteOutput`, the SDK's `UpdateRouteWithMeta` and bgp-rib's `resent` / `refused` keep their HEAD meaning: `resent` is "accepted by the engine", and at HEAD an `adjOutExpected` revision mismatch is already refused with `false, nil` and counted `resent` (journal row, `plan/journal/counter-counts-the-wrong-packets.md`). Round 5's wait for a queued receipt item is not taken: a ROUTE-REFRESH replay runs on bgp-rib's own `Process.deliveryLoop` (DirectBridge, synchronous), and the forward worker writing the queued item blocks in `Process.Deliver` once bgp-rib's 64-slot `eventChan` is full, so the wait would wait on itself (Current Behavior, refresh replay path). The counter counts every `adjOutExpected` refusal at an owed key, whatever replay carried it, so a reader that takes a delta over a window counts a ROUTE-REFRESH or `clear bgp rib out` refusal inside that window too; the drain spec reads its delta as "receipt replays refused at an owed key during the drain" (Blast Radius). Nothing is exported (Integration Checklist, Prometheus row). Under enhanced route refresh (RFC 7313), an End-of-RIB marker that reaches the destination before the recovery write makes it drop the prefix until that write lands; that converges and is accepted;
   - an `adjOutExpected` withdrawal (a lifecycle removal) merges into the record as in step 3: no excluded source is added, the epoch does not move, nothing is written;
   - an originated withdrawal (no sent-owner authority, which `allow` admits today whatever the owner) is written as today and leaves the record, so recovery still decides. Its write deletes the adj-out entry (`adjOutWrite.recordSection`, withdraw branch), which step 8 reads so that C is not sent the withdrawal twice (round-4 N-10);
   - a forwarded withdrawal from any source, admitted or dropped as non-owner, adds its owner identity (source, received path identifier and ADD-PATH flag, the fields `adjOutSameOwner` compares) to the key's movers set as a mover of kind WITHDRAW and writes nothing else. The rule applies to a key owed BEFORE the write began: the owner withdrawal that creates the record does not also note its own owner as a mover, because the owe is staged and applied after the last filter pass (step 3, round 7 I-1). The same holds for an announcement that creates the record (step 3a).
   Admission retries the key, leaving it owed with its record untouched (LEFT OWED in the outcome set above; round 9, I-1), only when a WITHDRAW mover is the candidate it is about to write, because only that candidate's own withdrawal can make the chosen route dead (round-2 I-2). When any ANNOUNCE mover is recorded (owner Q-3), a source offered a route the round's drain may not have applied: another source's route that may beat the chosen one, or the chosen candidate's own re-announcement, which makes the chosen bytes one revision old. The chosen path is still live in both cases, so the admission DECIDES as it would without the mover (the RIB's best as of the drain: written, or nothing to write when the destination already holds it, or the owed withdrawal on a WITHDRAW answer or an all-denied list), and, WHATEVER THAT DECISION WROTE, leaves the key owed with its epoch moved: the RE-OWED BY A MOVER flag (round 11, B-2). The re-owe is independent of the write because the mover recorded after the snapshot may be a better route the drain did not cover: C holds A, B's announcement owes the key, F's better announcement is accepted after the round's cut capture and merges at C's writer, the election returns A, which C already holds, and without the flag the record would be removed and F's forward, consumed by the merge, would never reach C. With the flag the next round, started at once under the one wake rule, elects with the newer route. Retrying without a write instead would let a source postpone the decision for as long as it re-announces, holding the destination on a route that may be the withdrawn owner's; writing a live path one revision old for one round is the lesser exposure, and the destination converges on the election when the churn stops. On a WITHDRAW or all-denied answer the same flag sends the owed withdrawal, so C lacks the prefix until the next round even though the mover's route exists (round 12, N-3); that is accepted because the alternative, holding the withdrawn owner's route until a round meets no mover, keeps C on a dead route that a churning set of sources could postpone without bound, and RFC 4271 Section 9.2 owes the withdrawal against the RIB as drained. With a write this is WRITTEN, RE-OWED; with nothing written it is DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE, RE-OWED; both are decided outcomes of the set above carrying the flag (owner Q-3; round 11, B-2, replacing round 10's separate seventh outcome, which a nothing-to-write decision could not reach), and `releaseItem`'s per-key `errRecoveryNotWritten` check counts each as a decision. The flag is the recorded mover, never the admission's own write: an `adjOutRecovery` write notes no mover (above), so no round re-triggers itself, and a round whose snapshot cleared the movers and that meets none decides without the flag, which ends the cycle once the announcements stop. A WITHDRAW mover of a non-chosen source needs no such step, because its withdrawal removes a candidate the decision did not pick. Any other mover is irrelevant to the decision: a candidate ranked above the chosen one was already denied by egress, one ranked below was not chosen, and a source with no candidate offered nothing. This still closes the round-1 hole where the survivor's own withdrawal is dropped as non-owner while A's withdrawal is owed, and it bounds retries: each retry needs the chosen candidate's own withdrawal, after which that candidate is gone from the RIB. When `queueRecovery` cannot resolve the chosen survivor, or the survivor's forward generation moved since the round's receipt (`errRelayNoSource`, at queue time), or the same loss is found later on the way to the write (round-6 N-3, read at the producer): `forwardUpdateValidated` refuses with `errForwardNoSource` when its `forwardSourceCurrent` check fails as `queueRecovery` hands the item to `forwardUpdateCore`, and the forward worker's recovery branch (`fwdBatchHandler`) finds `forwardSourceCurrent` false under `writeMu` at write time, where HEAD sets `recovery.retry`; in all three cases the admission writes nothing for the key and RE-OWES it with that survivor's source added to the record's excluded-source set, as a merge: the epoch moves and the next round elects again (round 5, N-7, replacing round 3's step to the next candidate), started by the one wake rule with no further external wake (step 3, round 7 I-3); the admission's other keys are still decided. That survivor's session ended or restarted, or it was not in the round's receipt capture (`queueRecovery` also returns `errRelayNoSource` when the elected source is absent from the round's `sources` map, a peer added to `r.peers` after `recoverySources` captured it; round 7, N-3), so its round-start route is no valid replacement, and a restarted survivor's new route reaches the destination through its own forward (round-3 N-1). Excluding an uncaptured source is safe for the same reason: its own forward to C merges into the record and enters the current-announcer set with its current generation, so the next round subtracts it and elects over its route (step 3a). Stepping to the next candidate of the same list would not do: under the non-transitive MED comparator AC-5 exercises, the list was built by repeated `SelectBest` with the dead source's other paths still in the remainder, so `SelectBest(S \ {D1})` can differ from `SelectBest(S \ {D1, D2})`, and C would end on a route that is not the RIB's best of the survivors (AC-12). The re-owes are bounded per source SESSION event, not per source (round 11, N-2): an excluded source becomes a candidate again only through the current-announcer subtraction (step 3a), which needs a new session of that source that re-announced the key, so each further re-owe of the same source needs a real restart plus a re-announcement. Egress-denied candidates are still stepped past in list order (step 8, AC-5), because a denial removes no path from the RIB. The survivor's receipt is its forward generation only; its whole-source publication cut is no longer compared (round-1 B2). For an ADD-PATH departure key, each identifier in the WITHDRAW answer is written only when its adj-out entry is still owned by a no-longer-current session of the departed source (step 6).
8. Destination writer: on SURVIVORS, the first candidate that passes ordinary egress policy (and bgp-rr's reflection rules, applied on the relay path, `reactor_api_relay.go`) is written with the survivor, its session generation and its received message ID as the new owner, unless it is the route the destination already holds (owner identity and received message ID equal, step 3a), in which case nothing is written and the outcome is DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE; when every candidate is denied by egress, or the outcome is WITHDRAW (a candidate whose source no longer resolves re-owes the key instead, step 7), the owed withdrawal is written from the record's copy, which is always in withdrawal framing (a withdrawal-created record copied withdrawal NLRI, an announcement-created one converted it at owe time, step 3a; round 11, I-6), with the old owner (round-1 I2; RFC 4271 Section 9.2 owes the withdrawal when no replacement exists), unless the key's adj-out entry is already gone, in which case nothing is written: an originated withdrawal admitted during the owed window already withdrew the prefix at C, and writing the record's copy again would send C a second withdrawal against AC-2's "exactly once" (round-4 N-10). A WITHDRAW answer also removes the key's tombstone (step 3a). In every case the record is removed in the same `writeMu` section and its waiters complete, unless the outcome is flagged RE-OWED BY A MOVER, in which case the record stays owed with its epoch moved and its waiters keep waiting for the next round's decision (step 7; round 11, B-2).

### Boundaries Crossed
| Boundary | How | Verified |
|----------|-----|----------|
| Reactor -> bgp-rs | structured `update-received` event (DirectBridge) or text event | Yes, read `processForward` |
| bgp-rs -> Reactor | `ForwardCached(ids, destinations)` | Yes, read `batchForwardUpdate` |
| Reactor -> bgp-rib | in-process `ribevents.RecoveryProvider().Lookup`, else hidden command `request bgp rib recovery` | Yes, read `relayRecovery` |
| bgp-rib -> Reactor | `[]ribevents.RecoveryRoute` (owned bytes) | Yes, read `recoveryCandidate` |

### Integration Points
- `adjOutWrite.allow` / `adjOutWrite.record` (`reactor/session_ownership.go`) - the single decision point every forward rail shares; gains the "owed to recovery" outcome for an owner's forwarded withdrawal and for a bgp-rib lifecycle removal, and (owner, Q-3) for a forwarded or initial-replay announcement of a key the non-ADD-PATH destination already holds or owes (Data Flow 3a), the per-authority rule for an owed key with WITHDRAW and ANNOUNCE movers (Data Flow 3 and 7), and the owner's source session generation and received message ID in `adjOutPath`.
- The destination's adj-out table (`adjRIBOut`, `reactor/adj_rib_out.go`) - holds the owed set beside `routes`, under the same table mutex every write already takes. Each record names the destination session it was owed to. EVERY site that resets the table clears the owed set with `routes` and completes each departure waiter with "session retired" (round-2 I-5): `adjRIBOut.reset` (called by `Peer.clearEncodingContexts` on teardown), `Session.retireWrite` (clears `routes` when the table is still its own), and `beginAdjOut` when the table's session changes. So an old session's record, framed for the old session's ADD-PATH negotiation, is never seen by the next session's worker, which also checks the record's session at admission. The departure command and the recovery worker now read the table under its mutex without being final writers, so the type's comment "Its only readers are final writers under writeMu" becomes false and is rewritten in Phase 3 (round-3 N-10, `ai/rules/stale-comments.md`).
- The recovery worker's lifecycle (round-2 I-5, round-3 I-4, N-2, N-3, N-4) - one per destination session. It is STARTED in the FSM Established callback BEFORE `setState(PeerStateEstablished)` publishes Established, and its first round runs on start without waiting for a wake, so a key owed before the worker existed is never stranded (round-3 N-2). STOP is an idempotent cancel plus a closed stop signal and never waits, so any teardown path may call it: `Session.retireWrite` (which runs under `writeMu`, possibly on the forward worker that owes the worker an `admission.done`), `Session.teardownAutomatic` from AC-11 (which runs on the recovery worker itself), and `Reactor.stop` / `StopForRestart`, which seals each live session through `Peer.sealSession` and notifies nobody. The ordinary close paths (a NOTIFICATION from the peer, hold-timer expiry, a TCP read error) reach none of those three, so the `Peer.runOnce` DEFER, the one that runs after `Session.Run` has returned and calls `clearEncodingContexts` (not the FSM leave-Established callback, which also calls it but runs on whichever goroutine fires or drains the FSM event, round-3 N-3, corrected in round 8 B-1), first calls stop and THEN joins. It runs after the down defer (Data Flow 5), which was registered later, so the leave callback has finished and the down has been delivered before the recovery worker is joined. Every exit path is therefore covered by construction (round-3 I-4), and the join holds neither `writeMu` nor the forward worker. A worker woken by stop releases its pending admission with "session retired". The in-process `RecoveryOwner.Lookup` takes no context, so the join is bounded by the lookup in flight; that lookup holds `peerMu` and by its contract never calls the forwarding engine, so it returns, and the IPC transport is already bounded by its command context. A provider that blocks forever (AC-16's test double) would wedge the join, so that test releases its provider before it tears the sessions down (round-3 N-4).
- `recoverNLRIBatch` / `relayRecovery` / `queueRecovery` (`reactor/relay_recovery.go`) - the inline per-destination loop is deleted (no-layering); departure enqueues into the owed set under the owner-and-generation check (Data Flow 4); the lookup transports and the admission item are reused by the recovery worker; `recoveryAdmission.current` and the `receivePublicationCut.current` check in `queueRecovery` give way to the per-key check of Data Flow 7; `errRelayNoSource` re-owes the key with that source excluded (round-3 N-1, round-5 N-7); `drainRecoverySources` returns the set of stopped or failed sources, which the round excludes from every key (round-5 B-1); `relayRecovery` chunks a round's keys at 500 per family (round-3 I-3). The registry lookup `Registry().Lookup("request bgp rib recovery")` at the top of `recoverNLRIBatch` stays the one answer to "is a recovery owner registered": the writer calls the same lookup, which reads the frozen snapshot lock-free, and treats `command == nil` or `command.Process == nil` as "no owner", the same two conditions `recoverNLRIBatch` tests (Key Design Decisions, "No RIB owner"; round-3 I-6, round-4 N-6). With the fence gone, `recoverySnapshot` has no caller and `Peer.sentUpdateSequence` has no reader, so both are deleted with the increment in `notifyMessageReceiver`; `adjOutWrite.noteFilteredOperation`, whose only effect is the other increment, is deleted with its two callers in `session_write.go` (no-layering; round 4, found while applying I-4; round 5, N-3). The recovery admission marks its write for R-12 by setting a `recovery-write` key in a FRESH copy of the item's meta, never in the shared `fwdItem.meta` map, which `session.sentMeta = item.meta` hands to the sent event (round 5, I-1). The drain spec's ordering step needs only the per-peer `writeMu` take-and-release, not the sequence (Blast Radius).
- `ribevents.RecoveryRequest` / `RecoveryRoute` (`internal/core/bgp/ribevents/recovery.go`) - the batch-wide `Source` becomes a per-key excluded-source set and `Cut` is deleted (round-3 N-6); the answer carries one outcome per key (SURVIVORS in election order, WITHDRAW), and each SURVIVORS candidate gains its received path identifier and received ADD-PATH flag (round-3 N-5) and keeps the `MessageID` `RecoveryRoute` already carries, per candidate and in the IPC form, because R-12's check reads it as the recovery write's `SourceMessageID` (round-6 N-4). No mode field (round-2 I-3). `RIBManager.recoveryRouteLocked` reads the per-key set; `recoveryCommand` (`plugins/rib/rib_recovery.go`), which today takes exactly eight positional arguments, takes the new per-key form over IPC, and `relayRecovery`'s argument builder writes it.
- `rr/rr.go` `handleStateDown` - sends its batches with `recovery-source` meta, as bgp-rs does, and first drops every family `retention.Family` keeps, as bgp-rs `handleStateDown` does (round-3 I-2, Q-5).
- The `recovery-cut` plumbing (round-3 N-6) - `routeServer.recoverRouteSel` stops sending the meta, `routeServer.handleStateDown` loses its cut parameter (the `seenMsgID` read in the state handler stays for `handleStateUp` and `ForwardFrom`), `plugins/cmd/update/recovery.go` stops parsing it, and `NLRIBatch.RecoveryCut` (`internal/component/bgp/types/types.go`, set in `update_text.go`) is deleted.
- bgp-rib's sent lifecycle (`rib_sent_lifecycle.go`) is unchanged in what it sends; the reactor's writer now owes its removals at non-ADD-PATH destinations (Data Flow 3). bgp-rib's sent projection gains one check for R-12, run only for a sent event carrying the `recovery-write` meta key, at whichever of the three store sites receives it (`storeSentEntries` from `handleSentStructured`, `storeSentEntries` from `handleSent`'s raw loop, and the inline `FamilyOps` `routeaction.Add` store in `handleSent`), with its removals dispatched off the event path (R-12, round-4 I-1, round-5 I-1).
- The receive delivery worker (`reactor/peer_run.go`) - its three `publication.fail()` sites, which already set `receivePublication.failed` (the "Adj-RIB-In invalid" fact, no new flag; round 7 N-4), call `Session.teardownAutomatic` with Cease / Out of Resources on the session the worker serves, and its stale comment is corrected (round-3 I-1). D1's reason producer moves out of the FSM leave-Established callback into `Peer.runOnce`'s down defer, which fires the leave itself when the FSM is still Established, closes `deliverChan`, waits `<-deliveryDone`, then waits on the session's leave signal when the FSM's sticky entered-Established bit is set (Data Flow 5, steps 1 to 4; round 8 B-1, I-3); it reads `notificationExchanged` and `receivePublication.failed` and reports `rpc.ReasonNotification` when the flag is set, else `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` when the fact is set (round-5 I-2, round-6 I-2), and the down event (`notifyPeerClosed`'s observer loop), `raiseSessionDropped`, the "session closed" log line, the leave-Established history row and `scheduleDynamicPeerCleanup` move with it; the callback keeps the closing mark, `notifyPeerClosed`'s other reactor bookkeeping, `clearEncodingContexts` and `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`, then records its reason and transition in the leave record and closes the leave signal (Data Flow 5, round 7 I-2, round 8 N-3). Because it is a defer, a read-goroutine panic that unwinds to `safeRunOnce` and leaves no lock held still drains, joins and announces the down; a panic inside the non-deferred `r.mu.RLock()` spans of `reactorForwardRSSection` or `notifyMessageReceiver` wedges the reactor, as it does at HEAD one step later (Data Flow 5; round 9, I-3). The leave arm keeps only the non-waiting half of the BFD release and the down defer waits on the subscriber's `done` after the join (round 9, B-1). GR's two state handlers call the declared predicate `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention` (Data Flow 5). All of it lands in Phase 2b, a defect-fix commit of its own.
- The owed-refusal counter (round 5 I-3, round 6 B-1 option i, round 7 I-1) - one monotonic atomic on `Peer` (`reactor/peer.go`); `adjOutWrite` (`reactor/session_ownership.go`) stages one count per NLRI `allow` refuses with the owed-refused outcome, and `writeUpdateBody` adds the staged count once per write under `writeMu`, after the last filter pass, on whichever goroutine writes (Data Flow 3, staged effects); never reset; read only as a delta. The update-route reply, `rpc.UpdateRouteOutput`, `extractUpdateRouteOutput`, the SDK's `UpdateRouteWithMeta`, `announceTarget.sendBatchUpdate`, `queueBehindForwards`, `fwdPool.releaseItem` and bgp-rib are not changed for it (Data Flow 7).

### Architectural Verification
| Check | Holds? | Evidence |
|-------|--------|----------|
| No bypassed layers (data flows through the intended path) | Yes (design) | Replacement selected by bgp-rib, exported through the ordinary forward rail and egress policy, as departure recovery does |
| No unintended coupling (components stay isolated) | Yes (design) | Reactor reaches the RIB through the existing `ribevents` provider or registered command, never by import |
| No duplicated functionality (extends existing, does not recreate) | Yes (design) | Reuses `relayRecovery`'s transports, `recoveryAdmission`, `recoveryRoutes`, `SelectBest`; no RS-side store. The owed set is a set of KEYS owed a decision, emptied as each is decided, not a route inventory: it holds no attributes |
| No layering (replace, not add) | Yes (design) | The inline per-destination loop of `recoverNLRIBatch` and the destination-wide sequence fence are deleted, not kept beside the worker; departure and withdrawal share one worker and one fence |
| Zero-copy preserved where applicable (refs, not copies) | Yes (design), narrowed by Q-3 | ADD-PATH destinations, and the first announcement of a key at a non-ADD-PATH destination, keep the cached zero-copy write. An announcement of a key the non-ADD-PATH destination already holds, and an owner-withdrawn NLRI, take the cold recovery path: the writer copies only the key, and the round writes the elected candidate's bytes from the RIB answer (`recoveryCandidate`). The RFC requires that destination to receive the elected route, which the cached bytes of the newest arrival are not |
| Registration over hardcoding, outbound | Yes (design) | No new command, family or plugin; the recovery provider is already published by bgp-rib via `ribevents.PublishRecovery` |
| Registration over hardcoding, inbound | Yes (design) | No switch learns a name; searched `relay_recovery.go`, `rib_recovery.go`, `session_ownership.go` for mode lists: none exists, and none is added: the per-key excluded-source set replaces a mode, so the two triggers differ only in data |

## Risks & Assumptions

<!-- LIVE: written during RESEARCH/DESIGN, statuses updated during implementation.
     Gate answers from /ze-spec (assumption challenge, Failure Mode Analysis)
     land HERE, not only in conversation. -->

### Assumptions
<!-- Every row needs a validation method. `unvalidated` is not a valid final
     status: closure re-checks each one. A broken assumption also gets a
     Mistake Log row and a Deviations entry. -->
| ID | Assumption | Basis (file/doc/user statement) | If wrong | Validated by | Status |
|----|-----------|--------------------------------|----------|--------------|--------|
| A-1 | After `DrainEventsApplied`, bgp-rib no longer holds A's withdrawn path, so an owner-withdrawal key (empty excluded-source set) need exclude nothing | `rib-storage-design.md` "this receipt proves successful application"; `recoveryRouteLocked` gathers from live peer RIBs | A stale candidate re-elects A's dead route and the client keeps a withdrawn path | Unit test: withdrawal-mode lookup after the RIB removed A's path returns B; reactor test drives the drain | unvalidated |
| A-2 | Every forward rail to an RS destination passes `adjOutWrite.allow` with authority `adjOutForwarded` | `reactorForwardRSSection` (`forward_rs.go`) and `forwardUpdateSection` (`reactor_api_forward.go`) both set it; guide: "All forwarding paths share the destination session's final writer ownership check" | A rail that bypasses the writer still emits the bare withdrawal | Wiring tests on both rails (cached and fast path) | unvalidated |
| A-3 | The survivor the RIB elects (`SelectBest`) is an acceptable replacement under forward-all, rather than "the latest remaining advertisement" | Departure recovery already uses `SelectBest` and the guide documents it | Owner may want latest-remaining semantics, which the RIB does not track | Owner answer Q-1 | unvalidated |
| A-4 | The in-tree cached forwarders are bgp-rs and bgp-rr only, both with `bgp-rib` as a hard dependency; an external SDK plugin can also forward cached UPDATEs | grep of `cached ` / `ForwardCached(` producers (2026-10-13): `rr/rr.go`, `rs/server_forward.go`, plus the SDK and dispatch plumbing; `rs/register.go` and `rr/register.go` `Dependencies` | A forwarder without a RIB would be retired on every owner withdrawal if the outcome failed closed on "no owner" | The writer outcome is gated on whether `request bgp rib recovery` is registered with a process (the frozen registry `Lookup`, absent when `command == nil` or `command.Process == nil`, as `recoverNLRIBatch` tests; round-3 I-6, round-4 N-6), not on a plugin name (Key Design Decisions, "No RIB owner"); test: no owner registered, owner withdrawal is written as today and logged once per destination | unvalidated |
| A-5 | `bgp-rib` is always loaded with `bgp-rs` and `bgp-rr`; a gap in its registration (bgp-rib restarting) is a fault window, not a configuration | `rs/register.go` and `rr/register.go` `Dependencies` name `bgp-rib` | During the window no recovery owner is registered, so an owner withdrawal is written bare (the RFC 4271 Section 9.2 defect, for that window) and announcements are written forward-all; the consequence would outlive the window, because a bare withdrawal deletes the entry and the next announcement would then be written directly with no election (round 11, I-4), so the contested bit is kept during the window and the deletion of a source-owned entry, contested or not, leaves a tombstone at a non-ADD-PATH destination (Data Flow 3a; round 12, I-1; round 13, N-6: an ADD-PATH destination keeps no tombstone, because it holds every path and no rule reads one there): the first source-bound announcement of that key after the window is owed and elected.
