What still outlives the window is a key that receives no write after it: C keeps the window's route for that key until its next announcement, withdrawal, departure or purge, the same exposure as R-12's dropped removals; keys already owed have no lookup owner, and R-12 removals still pending in bgp-rib's lifecycle dispatcher are dropped (round 5, N-4) | The once-per-destination log line names the window (round-2 I-6); a key owed while the owner was registered and looked up after it unregistered retires the destination (AC-11), tested by unregistering the command between owe and lookup | unvalidated |
| A-6 | Every forwarded write that can change a destination's route for a key passes that destination's `adjOutWrite.allow`, so the per-key record sees every announcement (removal) and every withdrawal (mover) that matters to a pending lookup | A-2; synthesized withdrawals of a policy-denied announcement reach the writer on both rails (`reactor_api_forward.go`, `forward_rs.go`) | A survivor change that bypasses the writer lets admission write a superseded survivor | Interleaving tests in `TestOwedKeyFenceInterleavings` (survivor withdraws, survivor re-announces, third source announces, each between lookup and admission) | unvalidated |
| A-7 | Withdrawn by round 3 (N-6). It was "the `ribOut[Destination]` entry describes the route last sent, so the KEEP outcome is sound"; the KEEP outcome and the cut are gone, because the departure's owner test reads the owner's session generation in the writer's own table (Data Flow 4) | - | - | - | - |
| A-8 | A source X whose receive publication stopped or failed before its cut has ended, or is ending, its session, and its routes are not retained after it, so any key X owns at a destination reaches that destination's worker through X's own departure, and no later round elects over X's known-invalid Adj-RIB-In. Rounds before X's session ends need no assumption: they exclude X (Data Flow 5, round 5 B-1) | Round 3 (I-1) found it FALSE at HEAD: the delivery worker's `publication.fail()` paths (`peer_run.go`) end nothing, and the read goroutine's enqueue only logs on `case <-publication.done` (`reactor_notify.go`). This spec makes it true by construction: each failure path calls `Session.teardownAutomatic` (Cease / Out of Resources) on its own session (Data Flow 5, Phase 2b). A normal end yields no error, because `close(deliverChan)` lets the worker publish everything before `deliveryDone` closes. Retention: at HEAD `gr.go` retains after a Ze-sent Cease because the reason is not "notification" (D1); D1 reads `notificationExchanged`, which an unsent Cease leaves false (`recordNotificationUnsent`), so the "Adj-RIB-In invalid" fact, `receivePublication.failed`, is the second input of D1's reason producer (round 5, I-2). Late failures (round 7, I-2): the producer and the down event run in `runOnce` after `<-deliveryDone`, so a failure the worker meets after the FSM left Established, or after `Session.Run` returned, and a session an unrelated close ended before the worker's `fail()`, all reach the reason before it goes out. The fourth `fail()` site, the read goroutine's deferred `publication.fail()` in `notifyMessageReceiver`, has two routes (round 8, I-3, read at the producer): (a) the `case <-publication.done` arm of the enqueue `select`, reachable only after a worker failure, because `deliveryDone` closes only after the worker's `recover` (which already called `fail()`) or after `close(deliverChan)`, which the down defer runs only once `Session.Run` has returned, so Phase 2b's teardown from the worker covers it; (b) a panic on the read goroutine between `acceptedReceive` and the hand-off (`reactorForwardRS`, the event-bus emit, the synchronous `OnMessageReceived`), reachable without a worker failure, with no `recover` on the read path. Route (b) unwinds `Session.Run` and `runOnce` to `safeRunOnce`, and, for a panic that leaves no lock held, the down defer still runs: it fires the leave, drains, joins and produces the reason with the fact set (Data Flow 5, steps 1 to 4). A panic inside `reactorForwardRSSection`'s non-deferred `r.mu.RLock()` or `sourcePeer.mu.RLock()` span leaves that lock held and wedges the reactor, here at step 1's `markSessionUp` and at HEAD at the next `r.mu` writer; this spec claims nothing for it (round 9, I-3). So (b), outside those spans, ends the session with a down, a departure and the no-retention reason, where at HEAD it ended with no down at all and leaked the delivery worker. So "no retention" holds for every publication failure of the session, not only one seen before it left Established | A source torn down too slowly would at worst be excluded for more rounds; a retained X would keep withdrawn routes electable until End-of-RIB or restart-time expiry | `TestOwedKeyPublicationFailureTearsDownSource` (AC-17): a panicking receiver and a nil receiver each tear the source down with Cease / Out of Resources, C is not retired, C converges, including when X is the chosen survivor; GR-capable X with the Cease delivered and with it unsent: no retention | unvalidated |
| A-9 | Every bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal is collected AFTER its operation removed the source's received paths, under `peerMu`, so an owed lifecycle key needs no excluded source | Read at the producer for all five callers (round-4 N-3). The `removedReceivedOwners` doc ("Callers MUST collect it before removing the received entries") speaks of the INDEX of removed owners, which is filled before the removal; the removals sent to the reactor are built after it. `autoExpireStale`, `purgeStale` (`rib_commands.go`) and the stale-community delete (`rib_commands_community.go`) fill the index in the `RemoveFamilyMatching` callback, then build the writes with `withdrawRemovedSentLocked` after the removal and before `peerMu` is released. `releaseRoutes` deletes `bgpPeers[peer]` before `pruneSentSourceLocked`. `retainRoutes` (not `onDown`) calls `RetainFamilies`, which releases the families not kept, before `pruneSentSourceLocked` | A purged stale path stays a candidate and is re-elected as its own replacement | Phase 3 reads `autoExpireStale`, `releaseRoutes`, `retainRoutes`, `purgeStale` and the stale-community delete; `TestOwedKeyGRPurgeRecoversSurvivor` asserts the purged path is never written back | unvalidated |
| A-10 | bgp-rib's `ribOut[client]` records bgp-rr's forwards with the reflected source as `SourcePeer`, so a lifecycle removal reaches an RR client as it reaches an RS client | sent events come from the reactor for every UPDATE sent (`notifyMessageReceiver` sent path), not from the forwarding plugin | GR-purge recovery would never reach RR clients (AC-19 bgp-rr half) | `TestRRGRPurgeRecoversSurvivor` | unvalidated |
| A-11 | `Session.teardownAutomatic`, called from the receive delivery worker, never waits for `deliveryDone` or for the delivery worker | `Session.teardown` seals and sends a NOTIFICATION; `runOnce` closes `deliverChan` and waits for `deliveryDone` only after `Session.Run` returns | The worker would wait on itself and the session would never end | Phase 3 reads `Session.teardown` before wiring the call; `TestOwedKeyPublicationFailureTearsDownSource` runs with `-race` and a deadline | unvalidated |
| A-12 | Withdrawn by round 5 (I-1). It was the per-source applied watermark that told "applied then removed" from "not applied yet" for every sent projection. R-12 now runs only for a recovery write, whose candidate bgp-rib's own lookup returned under `peerMu`, so its received path was applied before the write by construction and its absence can only mean removal. The watermark, its write sites in `handleReceived`, `handleReceivedPool` and `handleReceivedStructured`, and the "sent before received" case go with it. The reviewer also showed the watermark was unsound as written: an event `handleReceivedStructured` drops on an early return leaves a hole the next message ID covers | - | - | - | - |
| A-14 | (Owner, Q-3.) Every candidate route a non-ADD-PATH destination may receive reaches that destination's writer as a forwarded (live, or the peer-up relay) or initial-replay (bgp-rib `ribOut` replay on reconnect) announcement AFTER the destination's entry for the key was installed, or meets a tombstone, so writing the first route of a key directly (no entry, no record, no tombstone) and writing an uncontested owner's re-announcement zero-copy still end on the election: the next candidate to arrive finds the entry, sets the contested bit and is owed (Data Flow 3a, round 11, I-3) | bgp-rs forwards every accepted UPDATE to every selected target (`processForward` -> `batchForwardUpdate`, `selectForwardTargets` in `rs/server_forward.go`: every Up peer except the source, split live / replay by `ForwardFrom`, and the fast path `reactorForwardRSSection`), bgp-rr sends `cached <id>` to `*`, and a target that comes up is relayed every source's routes on the forwarded rail (bgp-rs and bgp-rr `replayForPeer`, bgp-adj-rib-in `relayRoutes` -> `RelayStoredRoute`), the live and replay rails split by the `ForwardFrom` cut (`rs/peer.go`); a candidate egress policy denies for the destination is not one it may receive; GR-retained routes of a DOWN source are never writable (`resolveRelaySource` requires Established, so `queueRecovery` re-owes them away) (round 11 review, checked at the producers). TWO EXITS deleted the entry while permitted live candidates remained in bgp-rib, which had already crossed the writer and would not cross it again (round 11, I-4): (1) an originated withdrawal of a key a forwarded source owns and that is not owed (`allow` admits it whatever the owner; `recordSection` deletes the entry); (2) A-5's window, where an owner withdrawal is written bare. Both now leave a TOMBSTONE for every source-owned entry, contested or not, so the next announcement is owed rather than written directly (round 12, I-1, correcting round 11's "an uncontested entry had no other candidate": a candidate can reach bgp-rib before its forward reaches this writer, the rails differing in latency, or have its forward dropped by `fwdBatchHandler` on a generation move while GR retains its path, and RFC 4724 Section 4.2 keeps that path a candidate once its source is Established again). The premise above is therefore about ARRIVAL, never about timing: a candidate may reach the writer after an owner withdrawal of the key, and the design stays correct only because no owner withdrawal or lifecycle removal is written bare while a recovery owner is registered (Data Flow 3: it is owed and elected against the drained RIB) and every other deletion leaves a tombstone. A dropped forward of a GR-retained path whose key then receives NO withdrawal and no announcement leaves C on what it holds for the key, or on nothing, until that source re-announces or the purge runs, as at HEAD | A permitted candidate that never reaches the writer leaves the destination on the first arrival, or on the sole owner's latest revision, when that candidate is better | `TestRSAnnouncementFirstArrivalConverges` (both arrival orders, both rails, a client that comes up after both sources announced, and a reconnecting client whose ribOut replay races its relay), `TestAdjOutContestedBitAndTombstone` (both exits), `TestOwnerWithdrawalOwedWhileCandidateInFlight` (round 12, I-1, both race cases) | unvalidated |
| A-13 | Withdrawn by round 6 (B-1). It was "a receipt replay reaches `sendBatchUpdate` on an engine command goroutine, and the forward worker never waits on bgp-rib", the basis of round 5's wait for a queued receipt item. Both halves were false at the producer: a ROUTE-REFRESH replay runs on bgp-rib's `Process.deliveryLoop`, and the forward worker blocks in `Process.Deliver` on bgp-rib's full `eventChan` (Current Behavior, refresh replay path). The wait, the queued-item completion and the reply count go with it; the per-peer owed-refusal counter needs no wait (Data Flow 7) | - | - | - | - |

### Risks
| ID | Risk | Early signal | Mitigation / fallback |
|----|------|--------------|----------------------|
| R-1 | Hot-path cost: every owner withdrawal to a non-ADD-PATH destination now pays a publication drain, a RIB applied-event drain and a lookup. That includes a single-source prefix (the common IXP case): the contested bit does not exempt a withdrawal (round 12, I-1, correcting round 11's bare write), because a clear bit cannot prove bgp-rib holds no second candidate (one in flight to this writer, or a GR-retained path whose forward was dropped on a generation move, Data Flow 3), so every owner withdrawal or lifecycle removal pays the round-10 cost, one round that decides WITHDRAW for a single-source prefix; the bit's saving applies to re-announcements only. PER ANNOUNCEMENT (owner, Q-3): an announcement to a non-ADD-PATH destination costs, at the writer, one owed-set emptiness check (as every write already pays, round-2 N-9), one read of the entry's contested bit under the table mutex `allow` already holds, and, when the destination holds no entry for the key or holds the announcing source's own uncontested entry, nothing more than the tombstone lookup that a first arrival also pays, behind the same emptiness shortcut as the owed set (round 12, N-4) (the zero-copy write as today); when the key is contested, one map insert or merge, one key copy and one non-blocking wake instead of the cached write, then its share of a round: the same drain, `DrainEventsApplied` barrier and 500-key lookup chunk as a withdrawal key, coalesced with every other key owed at that destination, plus one repeated-`SelectBest` election in `recoveryRouteLocked` under `peerMu` and, when the election changes the route, one recovery write built from the RIB's bytes (`recoveryCandidate` copies, no zero-copy). The dominant IXP churn, a sole source re-announcing its own prefix with new attributes, pays NO round (round 11, I-3, correcting round 10's "the writer cannot know the candidate count"): the writer needs no count, only the contested bit, which is one field on the existing per-key entry and not a second route inventory, so the re-announcement is written zero-copy as at HEAD. A contested key's re-announcement pays the round, and M non-ADD-PATH clients cost M elections per changed contested key per round. The owed-set emptiness shortcut (round-2 N-9) degrades: contested announcements keep a destination's owed set non-empty far more often than withdrawals alone did, so more forwarded writes pay the per-NLRI owed-set lookup, one map lookup per NLRI under the table mutex, no allocation; the bit read is paid on every non-ADD-PATH write either way. A client's initial sync (the peer-up relay on the forwarded rail) writes the first relayed source's route per prefix zero-copy and owes the prefix once for all other sources (one record per prefix, coalesced), so it costs one election per multi-source prefix, chunked at 500. A worse later route writes nothing to the client (DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE), so the client's UPDATE rate does not grow with sources | `./le perf` withdrawal-heavy run regresses; RS backpressure `.ci` slows; an announcement-heavy `./le perf` run (many sources per prefix, attribute churn from a sole source) regresses against HEAD's forward-all | The owed set coalesces every key owed while a round runs into the next round: one drain per destination per round and one lookup per 500-key chunk per family per round, however many withdrawals arrived (round-3 I-3: the chunk keeps each `peerMu` hold in `recoveryRoutes` and each IPC argument short, as bgp-rs's `withdrawalBatchSize` does today). M clients cost M drains per ROUND, not per withdrawal, and each round adds one `DrainEventsApplied` barrier to bgp-rib's event queue (`admitAppliedBarrier`, `process/delivery.go`), so M clients mean M barriers per round competing with events, within the accepted cost (round-3 N-11). The shared reactor-wide drain round 1 (N1) proposed is dropped (round 2, I-1): it had no owner, no lifetime and no failure mapping, and no measurement shows the per-worker drain is a problem (`ai/rules/simplicity.md`). In-process provider; owner accepted the cost (Q-1). Perf numbers are measured on the owner's Mac, not here; a measured regression from per-worker drains is the evidence that would re-open a shared drain. R-12's check (round-4 I-1, narrowed by round-5 I-1) costs a non-recovery sent event one meta-key test and nothing else; a recovery write's sent event pays one received-inventory lookup under the `peerMu` the projection already holds, and a removal only when the candidate was purged in the window, dispatched by the lifecycle dispatcher, never on the event path (R-12). The owed-refusal counter costs one atomic add per refused NLRI, under the `writeMu` the write already holds, and nothing on any other write; no replay waits (Data Flow 7, round-6 B-1). The owner's reading of RFC 7947 Section 2.1 allows a decision "computed in advance or cached"; a per-key election shared across destinations (the destination's own paths and its egress are per destination, so the shared part is the ordered candidate list) is the optimization a measured regression on the owner's Mac would justify, and is not designed here (`ai/rules/simplicity.md`, "a cache ... with no measured problem") |
| R-2 | Head-of-line blocking or self-wait: recovery on the destination's forward worker, or on a source read goroutine, waits on itself (round-1 B1) | Deadlock in the Phase 3 tests; forward queue depth grows | Recovery runs only on the per-destination recovery worker; the writer's hand-off is a non-blocking signal; the forward worker only performs the admission, which does no IPC and no drain. `TestOwedKeyHandOffNeverBlocksWriter` asserts the writer returns with the recovery worker parked |
| R-3 | Reordering: a later forward to the same destination for the same prefix (C announces) is written before the deferred recovery; or a bgp-rib replay (ROUTE-REFRESH, `clear bgp rib out`) re-sends A's withdrawn route while A's withdrawal is owed (round-3 B-1) | Client ends on a route that is not the RIB's best of B and C; or client keeps A's withdrawn route for good | (Owner, Q-3, replacing "C's announcement installs a new owner".) C's forwarded announcement merges into the owed record and is noted as an ANNOUNCE mover: when the round's choice is B and C was not in its drain, the admission writes B and re-owes the key, and the next round elects over B and C; when the choice is C itself, the mover retry re-elects with C's current bytes. Either way the client ends on the election, whatever the message-ID order. An originated announcement still installs and removes the record. A replay arrives under `adjOutExpected`, which installs no new owner: it is refused for an owed key and the record stays, so recovery's write answers the refresh (Data Flow 7, AC-20). The writer's record is the one authority; the RIB holds no second owner-moved check (round-2 I-3), and no message-ID comparison exists (round-1 I1, round-3 N-6). `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalLaterAnnouncementElected` drives C's ID below A's withdrawal ID; `TestOwedKeyRefreshDuringRoundKeepsRecord` drives the replay |
| R-9 | Livelock: a destination-wide or source-wide fence retries forever under churn on other prefixes, or from a busy survivor (round-1 B2) | A replacement never arrives while the client or the survivor is busy | Per-key fence: a key is retried only when its excluded-source set grew (a merge) or the candidate admission is about to write withdrew that key between snapshot and admission (round-2 I-2). A survivor that no longer resolves or whose forward generation moved (`errRelayNoSource`) re-owes the key with its source excluded (round-3 N-1, round-5 N-7). A source whose publication failed or stopped is excluded within the round, never retried (round-5 B-1). Withdrawals from any other source, and every write for other keys, cause no retry, so no peer can postpone the replacement by repeating withdrawals for the same prefix. Retries are bounded: each needs a merge (one per departing source session, or one per source re-owed on `errRelayNoSource`, which becomes a candidate again for that record only through a new session that re-announces the key, so the bound is per source session event; round 11, N-2) or the chosen candidate's own withdrawal, after which that candidate is gone; egress steps are bounded by the candidate list. AC-15 drives continuous churn on other prefixes, and a same-prefix withdrawal stream from a non-candidate, and requires the replacement within one round. A retried or re-owed key never waits for unrelated traffic to start its round: every owe, merge and re-owe wakes the worker, and an admission that leaves a key owed starts the next round itself (Data Flow 3, round 7 I-3). ANNOUNCEMENTS (owner, Q-3): an ANNOUNCE mover, from any source including the chosen candidate, never blocks a decision, because that admission still decides (WRITTEN, RE-OWED, or DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE, RE-OWED; round 11, B-2) and only the next round re-decides; so a source re-announcing the owed prefix in a continuous stream costs one round per batch of its announcements (coalesced), each writing the then-best only when it changed, and never postpones the replacement. The cost of not retrying is that the destination may hold the chosen path one revision old for one round (Data Flow 7); AC-22 asserts both the write under churn and the convergence when churn stops |
| R-10 | Unbounded owed set grows without limit under a withdrawal storm toward a stalled recovery worker | Memory growth of the owed set | Accepted by the owner (AC-12, unbounded). Growth is bounded by distinct keys, not by events (a key owed twice is one record), and the set empties as each round completes; a stalled worker is visible through the existing forward congestion signals and the hard-failure path (AC-11). No cap, no drop (AC-12) |
| R-11 | Recovery worker outlives its destination session, or two workers serve one destination, or the join blocks a reconnect | Goroutine leak in `-race` tests; writes to a replacement session from an old worker; a peer that never reconnects after a remote NOTIFICATION | One worker per destination session (`ai/rules/goroutine-lifecycle.md`), started before Established is published; stop is an idempotent cancel plus a closed signal callable from any teardown path; the `Peer.runOnce` defer (after `Session.Run` returns) calls stop and then joins, so the ordinary close paths that reach none of the named stop callers are covered (round-3 I-4); never joined from `retireWrite` or from the worker's own AC-11 teardown (Integration Points, round-2 I-5); every adj-out table reset (`adjRIBOut.reset`, `Session.retireWrite`, `beginAdjOut`) clears the owed set and releases waiters with "session retired"; records name their session and admission checks it. `TestOwedKeyWorkerLifecycle` covers AC-11 self-retire, `retireWrite` while an admission is pending, a `StopForRestart` reactor restart, a remote NOTIFICATION while the worker is parked, and a key owed before the worker started |
| R-4 | Replacement denied by egress policy for this destination | Client keeps A's dead route | Existing departure behaviour: "a policy-denied replacement withdraws the obsolete advertised identity"; AC-5 asserts it |
| R-5 | Recovery hard failure retires the destination session (Cease) for one withdrawal | Unexpected Cease on a client during withdrawals | Same fail-closed policy as departure; documented; logged with the cause. Owner informed (Q-1) |
| R-6 | Withdrawn by round 2 (I-3). It was "a withdrawal-mode request reaches the departure branch"; with no mode, an owner-withdrawal key simply carries an empty excluded-source set, so there is no branch to reach. AC-9 and `TestRecoveryOwnerWithdrawalKeepsSourceOtherPaths` still prove A's remaining path stays eligible | - | - |
| R-7 | Interop scenario passes vacuously because BIRD already holds B (A, B order), or because BIRD negotiated ADD-PATH and holds both paths | Red phase does not go red on revert | Checker asserts BIRD's path is FRR's before the withdrawal AND that BIRD's session did not negotiate ADD-PATH for the family (round-1 N3); revert-to-red walk recorded. The scenario exercises whichever rail its config selects; the other rail is proven by the `.ci` twins |
| R-8 | RFC-tagged claims on new carriers wider than their assertions | `./le rfc check` or the judge refuses | TDD plan fixes the claim text; discrimination records via `./le rfc discriminate-record` |
| R-12 | Round 3, found while applying I-2: a lifecycle purge of the CHOSEN survivor's path lands between the lookup and the admission. Example: B reconnected under GR, its retained stale path is elected for C's owed key, then B's End-of-RIB makes bgp-rib purge it. The purge emits a `sentRemoval` only where B owns `ribOut`, and at C the owed key is still A's, so no writer event reaches C and the movers set never hears it; the admission then writes B's purged route and nothing removes it. Departure recovery at HEAD has the same window | C holds a route its source no longer has | Round 4 (I-1) bounded the fix; round 5 (I-1) narrowed it to the only write that has the window. WHICH WRITES: only a RECOVERY write. Its candidate came out of bgp-rib's own lookup under `peerMu`, so its received path was applied before the write, and absence at projection time can only mean removal; no watermark is needed (A-12 withdrawn). A forwarded write needs no check, because the source's own later withdrawal reaches C's writer in source order (one delivery worker per session publishes in order, `peer_run.go`; one FIFO per plugin, `process/delivery.go`); the purge-before-projection race of a forwarded write of a just-retained source exists at HEAD and is not this spec's, so Phase 2 writes one journal row for it. The recovery admission sets the `recovery-write` meta key in a fresh copy of the item's meta (Integration Points); `handleSentStructured` already skips `replay` and `rib-lifecycle` events, and the check runs only when the key is present, at whichever of the three sent-store sites receives the event (`storeSentEntries` from `handleSentStructured`, `storeSentEntries` from `handleSent`'s raw loop, the inline `FamilyOps` `routeaction.Add` store in `handleSent`). A non-recovery sent event is stored as today and triggers nothing. TRIGGER: the source's receive inventory now holds neither the candidate's received path nor a newer revision of the key from that source (the case `withdrawRemovedSentLocked`'s comment names, "A fresh received revision can precede both forwarding and sent projection"); `sentSourceCurrent`'s path-presence half answers it. A recovery entry that lacks its provenance (`SourceOwner`, `SourceMessageID`, a parsable `SourcePeer`) is a defect in the recovery write: it is stored as today, triggers nothing, and is logged. The recovery write's sent event carries the candidate's provenance (read at the producer, round-6 N-4): `SourcePeer` comes from `ownership.source.addrString` and `SourceOwner` from `ownership.source.sourceOwner` (`session_write.go`, sent receipt); `SourceMessageID` chains `item.sourceMessageID` <- `ReceivedUpdate.sourceMessageID()` = `validationMsgID` <- `route.MsgID` (`buildRelayUpdate`) <- `MsgID: recovery.MessageID` (`relayRecovery`) <- `MessageID: best.entry.MsgID` (`recoveryCandidate`). So the per-key SURVIVORS answer, its IPC form included, MUST carry `MessageID` for EVERY candidate, or this check loses its "newer revision" input (Integration Points, `ribevents` row). EFFECT: the projection stores the entry and removes it in the same `peerMu` section, deleting it from `ribOut` as `withdrawRemovedSentLocked` does (`delete(routes, key)`), and queues the `sentRemoval` it would have emitted had the projection preceded the purge. DISPATCH: not on the event path. A synchronous `updateRouteWithMeta` (an engine RPC with a 10 s timeout) on `dispatchStructured` would stop bgp-rib applying events while it is in flight, so every destination's `DrainEventsApplied` would wait behind it. The removal is appended to an unbounded pending list under `peerMu` and sent by one long-lived bgp-rib lifecycle dispatcher (started with the plugin's run loop, woken through a one-slot send-or-skip channel; `ai/rules/goroutine-lifecycle.md`). The dispatcher sends through a context it owns, derived from the run loop's and cancelled at stop, not `updateRouteWithMeta`'s `context.Background()`, so a stop cancels the RPC in flight; stop is cancel, then join (round 5, N-4). Pending removals are DROPPED at stop: the stop is bgp-rib's shutdown or restart, and while bgp-rib is unregistered no recovery owner exists, so this is A-5's fault window, and a destination still holding the purged route keeps it until the next decision for the key (a later withdrawal, departure or purge), the same exposure A-5 records for an owner withdrawal in the window. Late delivery is safe because the writer admits an `adjOutExpected` withdrawal only when the entry exists with the same revision (`adjOutWrite.allow`), so a removal overtaken by a newer write is refused; at a non-ADD-PATH destination an admitted one is owed (Data Flow 3) and recovery elects again. The five existing `dispatchSentLifecycle` callers keep their synchronous dispatch on their command goroutines. Tests: `TestOwedKeyPurgeBetweenLookupAndAdmission` (reactor, real RIB) and `TestRIBSentProjectionOfPurgedOwnerRemoves` (RIB unit) |

## Blast Radius

<!-- What a wrong landing costs, and how to get out. A reviewer reads this first. -->
| Question | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| What breaks if this is wrong? | RS clients without ADD-PATH: a wrong replacement installs a route the source no longer has (blackhole), or a missed withdrawal leaves a stale route; a recovery failure resets the client session |
| How is it reverted? | Single commit revert; no config or wire format change |
| Who else touches this path? | `spec-rfc-verdict-fix-bgp` (Blocked-by row for 9.2-7), `spec-rfc7947-adj-rib-in-accepts-filtered-updates` (RS ingress), the ownership work behind `b880c5a9a7`; bgp-rr (in scope) shares `forwardUpdateSection`; departure recovery for bgp-rs changes its fence and its thread. At design time (2026-10-13) `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_special.go` and `bgp_test.go` carry another session's uncommitted hunks: Phase 5 re-checks before editing them |
| Shared with the drain spec | `plan/immediate/spec-daemon-drain-before-stop.md` edits `forward_rs.go`, `session_write.go`, `forward_pool.go`, `reactor_api_batch.go`, `peer_run.go`, `internal/component/bgp/types/types.go` and `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md`, which this spec also edits, and it reads `relay_recovery.go`, which this spec rewrites; this spec adds the per-peer owed-refusal counter to `peer.go` (round 6, B-1); the drain spec reads it at its reply beside its own two per-peer drain counters, re-advertised and withheld, which it defines and holds where its `reactor.go` Files row says ("the per-peer drain counters"), with re-advertised counted in `writeUpdateBody` next to `updateSendTotals.announcedRoutes` and withheld counted by its gate (drain spec Data Flow 5 items 1 and 2; round 7, main-thread correction); both specs then edit `writeUpdateBody`, and each counts an NLRI of a twice-filtered body once (round 7, I-1); whichever lands second rebases onto the other. Behaviour: the drain replay and its `drain-unjudged` follow-up replay are bgp-rib receipt writes of `ribOut[destination]` (`adjOutExpected`, set by `sendBatchUpdate` whenever `SentOwnerMessage != 0`), so this spec's round-3 B-1 rule (an `adjOutExpected` receipt replay is refused while its key is owed, Data Flow 7) also refuses them. That rule is what keeps a drain from re-advertising a withdrawn, owed path with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN during the owed window, so it MUST hold for drain-marked replays. The refusal is counted by the per-peer monotonic owed-refusal counter on `Peer`, incremented under `writeMu` by whichever goroutine writes, the forward worker for a queued receipt write included, and never reset (Data Flow 7, round 6 B-1, option i). The drain spec's third figure is the delta of that counter over the drain, read with its re-advertised and withheld drain counters on the same timing; it reads "receipt replays refused at an owed key during the drain", whatever replay carried them, because a ROUTE-REFRESH or `clear bgp rib out` refusal inside the drain window is counted too. `refused` and `resent` keep their HEAD meaning. The drain spec already reads it so (checked 2026-10-13: "the rs spec's per-peer owed-refusal counter on `Peer`", a before/after delta at its drain reply), so no drain-spec text is owed by this round. The replacement this spec writes goes `queueRecovery` -> `forwardUpdateCore` and gets the drain verdict from the drain step in `r.orderedEgressSteps`. Ordering shape: this spec deletes `recoverySnapshot` and `Peer.sentUpdateSequence` with the destination-wide fence, and the drain spec's A-14 cites `recoverySnapshot` as its ordering shape. What A-14 needs is only the per-peer `Session.writeMu` take-and-release, which stays valid: every write still enqueues its sent event before it releases `writeMu`, and an owed withdrawal writes nothing and sends no sent event, so it needs no ordering. The drain therefore takes and releases each Established peer's `writeMu` itself, reading no sequence (the drain-spec text change is reported to the main thread; this spec does not edit that file). No landing order is required; the spec that lands second carries `TestDrainReplayRefusedAtOwedKey` (a drain-marked receipt replay during an owed window is refused and moves the owed-refusal counter; the replacement written afterwards is tagged per the verdict), and `TestOwedKeyFenceInterleavings` gains case (k) once the drain spec has landed |
| Depends on GR D1 | `plan/immediate/spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md` D1 (Status design, open, owner question Q-6 on the reason spelling): a NOTIFICATION teardown currently still triggers GR retention. Round 5 (I-2) narrowed the dependency to one sub-case. D1 alone does not cover the publication-failure teardown: D1 derives the reason from `notificationExchanged`, and a Cease that is built but not written goes to `recordNotificationUnsent`, which leaves that flag false, so GR would still retain X's known-invalid Adj-RIB-In. Round 4's reason for not declaring non-retention locally ("a second answer to did a NOTIFICATION end this session") does not apply, because the fact is a different one: "this Adj-RIB-In is known-invalid", which no NOTIFICATION flag carries. So D1's reason producer gets a second input (Phase 2b): one producer, two inputs, two declared reason values (`rpc.ReasonNotification`, and `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` when only the fact is set, round-6 I-2), and one declared predicate, `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention`, that GR calls. The new value is operator-visible; the owner accepted its spelling "adj-rib-in invalid" (Q-6 here, 2026-10-13), as D1's is Q-6 of the GR spec. The teardown half of Phase 2b needs nothing from D1 and lands first as its own defect-fix commit (`ai/rules/git-safety.md`, one logical change per commit); its non-retention input and AC-17's GR-capable half land when D1's producer exists. Phase 3 does not wait on D1: with B-1's in-round exclusion, a round never reads X's unpublished state, and only rounds after the teardown depend on retention. Round 7 (I-2): D1 places its producer in the FSM leave-Established callback, and this spec's Phase 2b teardown commit moves the down event, and with it the producer's site, to `runOnce`'s down defer, after `<-deliveryDone` and the leave-callback join (round 8, B-1). If D1 lands first, Phase 2b moves D1's producer there; if Phase 2b lands first, D1 writes its producer at the new site. The GR spec's text is reported to the main thread; this spec does not edit that file |

## Acceptance Criteria

| AC ID | Input / Condition | Expected Behavior |
|-------|-------------------|-------------------|
| AC-1 | Sources A and B announce one prefix in the order B then A; A withdraws; client C did not negotiate ADD-PATH send for the family | C's wire receives one UPDATE whose NLRI is the prefix and whose path attributes are B's received attributes (AS_PATH, NEXT_HOP, MED unchanged); C's wire carries no Withdrawn Routes or MP_UNREACH entry for the prefix after A's withdrawal. Proven on both the cached rail and the fast path |
| AC-2 | A is the only source; A withdraws | C's wire receives the withdrawal of the prefix, exactly once |
| AC-3 | RFC4271-9.2-7, RFC4271-9.2-8, RFC7947-x-5 (owner, Q-3 and Q-4) | New wire-level carriers tagged with the claim text fixed in the TDD plan, each with a discrimination record written by `./le rfc discriminate-record`: 9.2-7 in both polarities, 9.2-8 negative, x-5 in both polarities. `TestRIBBestChangeWithdraw` and `TestRIBBestChangeNoPublishSameBest` lose EVERY `RFC requirement:` tag and stay as plain unit tests, after Phase 5 has confirmed at the producer that the 9-3 halves and the 9.1.2-2 negative they carried stay carried by `rfc4271_receive_decision_test.go` (TDD plan, "RFC-tagged tests that change"); the verdicts of 9.2-7, 9.2-8, 9-3, 9.1.2-2 and x-5 are re-judged; the 9.2-7 row is removed from the Blocked-by list of `plan/pre-release/spec-rfc-verdict-fix-bgp.md` |
| AC-4 | Interop | Scenario `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr`, BIRD the non-ADD-PATH client, GoBGP's route made worse by one prepended AS. (1) GoBGP announces the prefix; BIRD holds GoBGP's path. (2) FRR announces it (better, later); BIRD holds FRR's path. (3) GoBGP re-announces it, still worse, with a changed community (later); BIRD still holds FRR's path through a stability window. (4) FRR stops announcing; BIRD holds GoBGP's path and never loses the prefix. Two revert-to-red walks, recorded: with the announcement half reverted (Data Flow 3a) the checker fails at (3); with the withdrawal half reverted (Data Flow 3) it fails at (4), because under the announcement half FRR still owns BIRD's route when it withdraws |
| AC-5 | Three sources A, B, E announce (B, E, then A); A withdraws; B (the RIB's best of the survivors) is denied by C's egress policy, E is permitted | C receives E's route with E's received attributes, no withdrawal (round-1 I2). With E also denied, C receives the withdrawal of the prefix exactly once (no stale A route, no denied route). "Best" and the order stepped through are election order: repeated `SelectBest` over the remaining candidates (Data Flow 6, round-2 N-1), and the RIB unit asserts that order with candidates whose MEDs make the comparator non-transitive |
| AC-6 | A, B announce (B then A); source F announces the prefix after A's withdrawal is owed but before recovery admits, with F's message ID LOWER than A's withdrawal's (the IDs come from one process-wide counter and are not writer order) | (Owner, Q-3, replacing "C ends on F's route".) C's wire ends on the RIB's best of B and F, run once with F better than B and once with B better than F; never A's route, never a withdrawal. F's announcement is not written directly: it merges into the owed record (Data Flow 3a and 7), and when it reaches C's writer after the round's snapshot it is an ANNOUNCE mover, so the admission writes the round's choice and re-owes the key, and the next round's choice is C's final route. The message-ID order decides nothing; the writer's record is the only ownership authority (round-2 I-3) |
| AC-7 | Client C negotiated ADD-PATH send for the family | Unchanged: C receives the withdrawal of A's advertised path identifier only; B's path already present stays |
| AC-8 | A non-owner withdraws (A, B announce in that order; A withdraws) | Unchanged from `b880c5a9a7`: nothing is written to C for the prefix |
| AC-9 | A announced two received paths for the prefix over ADD-PATH and withdraws one; C has no ADD-PATH | C receives A's remaining path (or the RIB's elected survivor including it), never a withdrawal. Proven at the RIB unit level and on C's wire (`TestRSOwnerWithdrawalAddPathSourceKeepsOtherPath`, round-2 N-5), where the owner key the writer matches carries A's received path identifier (`adjOutPath.received`) |
| AC-10 | bgp-rr (round-1 I4; Q-2 reading: rr in scope). Sources A and B (non-clients) announce one prefix (B then A) to a client C without ADD-PATH; then (a) A withdraws the prefix, and separately (b) A's session goes down | (a) C receives B's route with B's received attributes (plus the RR's ORIGINATOR_ID / CLUSTER_LIST handling), no withdrawal. (b) Same, through departure recovery; and with the order A then B (B is C's owner), A's departure writes NOTHING to C for the prefix, where today it writes a plain withdrawal, and also nothing when a third source E that `SelectBest` prefers over B is present (forward-all is not overridden by an unrelated departure). When no survivor exists, C receives the withdrawal exactly once. The same "order A then B, A departs: nothing written to C, with and without a preferred E" holds for bgp-rs, whose departure today re-elects at every destination (round-2 B-1). (c) Q-5 (owner, yes): A negotiated Graceful Restart and its family is retained; A's session down writes nothing to C, and the retained route leaves only through AC-19. With no recovery owner registered (an external forwarder running without bgp-rib), an owner withdrawal is written as today and one log line per destination names the missing owner (round-2 I-6) |
| AC-11 | Recovery lookup fails: a lookup error, a malformed or incomplete answer (a requested key with no outcome), or the recovery owner gone after the key was owed (the command unregistered between owe and lookup); or a departure arrives while no recovery owner is registered (round-3 N-7, today's outcome kept) | Same as departure: the destination session is retired through AutomaticStop/Cease and the cause is logged; never a silent stale route. A stopped or failed publication of ANOTHER source is not a lookup failure (AC-17) |
| AC-12 | Owner constraint, 2026-10-13: "what happens if the route is known from X neighbors and two go down ! It must be unbounded". The prefix is known from N sources (N at least 4 in the test, and a property-style test over random N and orders); K of them (1 < K < N) withdraw or their sessions go down, in every order, back-to-back and concurrently | C always ends holding the RIB's best of the N-K survivors, never a withdrawal, never a stale route from a gone source; when K = N, exactly one withdrawal. No fixed limit on N, K, or the number of in-flight recoveries per prefix or per client: no bounded queue, slot or per-prefix pending table whose overflow drops, merges wrongly, or blocks other prefixes. The one-slot wake channel is not such a slot: it carries no data, the owed set is the queue, and a skipped wake loses nothing because the worker re-reads the whole set each round (round-2 N-7); the 500-key lookup chunk bounds a request, not the set (round-3 I-3). A session that goes down includes a GR-capable source whose retained routes are purged later (AC-19, round-3 I-2). Proven against the REAL bgp-rib, not a stubbed provider (round-1 I3): `TestOwedKeyRealRIBConvergence` and `TestOwedKeyRealRIBConvergenceProperty`, plus `test/plugin/bgp-rs-withdrawal-replacement-many.ci` |
| AC-13 | Round-1 I2. Destination C itself announced the prefix, and C's path is the RIB's best; A (C's owner) withdraws; B is a survivor | C receives B's route, not a withdrawal: C's own candidates are excluded before election, for owner-withdrawal and departure keys alike. Proven at the RIB unit level and on C's wire (`TestRSOwnerWithdrawalSkipsDestinationOwnPath`, round-2 N-5) |
| AC-14 | Round-1 I5. A is C's owner; A re-announces the prefix with attributes C's egress policy denies (synthesized withdrawal), and separately A's re-announcement is turned into treat-as-withdraw by next-hop refusal; B is a survivor | C receives B's route, not a withdrawal, on both rails. A's denied path, still in the RIB, is stepped past by the egress check (AC-5's ordered candidates) |
| AC-15 | Round-1 B2, liveness. While one key is owed, the survivor B sends a continuous stream of UPDATEs for OTHER prefixes and C is sent a continuous stream of other UPDATEs (at least 1000 each in the test, no pause) | C receives the replacement for the owed key within one recovery round: the admission for that key is not retried because of traffic on other keys or other sources. Same-prefix half (round-2 I-2): a non-candidate source D (one that announced the prefix earlier and is not the chosen survivor, plus one that never announced it) sends a continuous stream of withdrawals FOR THE OWED PREFIX itself; every one is dropped at C's writer as non-owner, and C still receives B's route within one round. A retry happens only when the candidate admission is about to write withdrew that key between snapshot and admission, a departure merged into it, or the chosen survivor's session ended or restarted (`errRelayNoSource`, `errForwardNoSource` or the write-time source check, each re-owing the key with that source excluded; Data Flow 7, round-5 N-7, round-6 N-2), and a retried key's next round is started by the worker itself under the one wake rule, never by waiting for unrelated traffic (Data Flow 3, round 7 I-3). AC-15 involves no session end or restart, so the test asserts the retry count is zero in both halves |
| AC-16 | Round-1 B1. A withdrawal storm on the fast path and on the cached rail while C's recovery worker is held inside its lookup (a provider that blocks until released) | Neither the source read goroutine nor C's forward worker blocks: the source keeps reading (its later UPDATEs reach other destinations), C's forward worker keeps writing other keys, and every owed key is decided once the provider is released. No goroutine is created per withdrawal. The test releases the provider before it tears the sessions down, because the in-process lookup takes no context (round-3 N-4) |
| AC-17 | Round-2 I-1, round-3 I-1, round-5 B-1. While C's worker is inside its drain for an owed key, the receive publication of a source X FAILS, injected as a panicking message receiver and, separately, as a nil message receiver; in one case X is a third source (not the survivor), in the other X is the chosen survivor whose withdrawal of the prefix is the UPDATE whose publication fails (B announces, A announces and owns C, B withdraws, B's publication fails, A withdraws or departs) | X's session is torn down with Cease / Out of Resources (RFC 4486 Section 4); C is not retired; C's round excludes X from every key it decides and C receives the RIB's best of the remaining candidates, or one withdrawal when none remains, never X's route; any key X owned at C is decided through X's departure. The drain is not retried in a loop. A normal end of X's session during the drain produces no error and C converges the same way. GR-capable half (round-4 I-2, round-5 I-2; lands with Phase 2b's non-retention input, once D1 of `spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md` has landed): X negotiated Graceful Restart for the family and owns a key at C; after the teardown no GR retention of X's routes follows, both when the Cease reached the wire (RFC 4724 Section 4, a NOTIFICATION teardown) and when it did not (`recordNotificationUnsent`); the down reason is `rpc.ReasonNotification` in the first case and `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` in the second, never "notification" for a Cease that was not delivered (round-6 I-2); the same holds for a LATE failure, one the delivery worker meets on a buffered item after the FSM left Established or after `Session.Run` returned, and for a session an unrelated close (a TCP read error) ended before the worker reached its `fail()`, because the reason is produced after `<-deliveryDone` (round 7, I-2), and for a panic on X's read goroutine between `acceptedReceive` and the hand-off that leaves no lock held (outside `reactorForwardRSSection`'s non-deferred `r.mu.RLock()` span; round 9, I-3), after which X's session still ends with a down event carrying that reason (round 8, I-3); every plugin receives the session's buffered UPDATEs before its down event, for every session (`TestSessionDownFollowsBufferedDeliveries`); X's departure decides every family, and C never holds a route X withdrew in an UPDATE whose publication failed. The nil-receiver and nil-reactor halves are reached through a test seam on the delivery worker, because production never sets `p.reactor` or `r.messageReceiver` to nil and `notifyMessageReceiver` returns before the enqueue when the receiver is nil (round-4 N-7) |
| AC-18 | Round-2 B-1, round-3 N-6, old DOWN after reconnect. A owns the prefix at C; A's session goes down and A reconnects and re-announces the prefix (a newer route, same source) before or while the departure's keys are being decided | C ends on the RIB's best of A's newer route and the survivors (owner, Q-3), never the old departure's choice made without A's newer route, never A's withdrawn old route. If A's re-announcement reached C's writer after the key was owed by the departure, it merged into the record, entered the record's current-announcer set with A's current generation and moved the epoch, so the next round does not exclude A and elects with A's newer route as a candidate. If it reached C first, it was itself owed (C held A's old route, so an entry existed), and the departure that merges afterwards adds A to the excluded-source set, which the round again subtracts because A's current-announcer entry is current (Data Flow 3a). With A's newer route the best, C ends on it; with a survivor better, C ends on the survivor. And with A reconnected but NOT re-announcing the prefix, the entry still names A's earlier session, so the departure owes it and C receives the survivor |
| AC-19 | Round-3 I-2, owner constraint AC-12. A owns the prefix at C (no ADD-PATH); A negotiated Graceful Restart; B also announces the prefix; A's session goes down and its family is retained (bgp-rs skips it at departure, as bgp-rr does under Q-5); then the retained route is purged by restart-time expiry, and separately by `release-routes` and by A reconnecting and sending End-of-RIB without the prefix (`purge-stale`) | C receives B's route with B's received attributes, never a bare withdrawal; with no survivor, C receives the withdrawal exactly once. At an ADD-PATH destination the purge is written as today. Proven for bgp-rs and bgp-rr against the real bgp-rib |
| AC-20 | Round-3 B-1. A's withdrawal is owed at C; before the admission, C sends ROUTE-REFRESH, and separately an operator runs `clear bgp rib out` for C | bgp-rib's replay of A's route is refused at C's writer and the owed record stays; C ends on B's route (or one withdrawal when no survivor exists), never on A's withdrawn route. The replay command does not fail, and C's per-peer owed-refusal counter moves by exactly the number of refused prefixes, read as a delta, both when the receipt write is direct and when it was queued behind forwards owed to C (read after C's forward worker has drained the queued item); `clear bgp rib out`'s `resent` and `refused` keep their HEAD meaning (round-4 I-4, round-5 I-3, round-6 B-1). A replay of a key that is NOT owed is written as today and does not move the counter. A MIXED replay, one UPDATE carrying an owed key and a non-owed key, writes the non-owed key and moves the counter by exactly 1 per refused prefix, although `writeUpdateBody` filters that body twice (round 7, I-1) |
| AC-21 | Round-3 I-3. A departure owes C 2000 keys of one family | bgp-rib receives every lookup for that round in requests of at most 500 keys; every key is decided |
| AC-22 | Owner, Q-3, bgp-rs, on the cached rail and on the fast path. C did not negotiate ADD-PATH send for the family. (a) A announces the prefix, then B announces it with a worse route (one more AS in AS_PATH); (b) B announces the worse route, then A announces the better one; (c) A announces, then A re-announces with a route worse than B's after B announced; (d) A and B announce, then a third source E announces a worse route and E then withdraws it; (e) under churn: while A's withdrawal is owed, B re-announces the prefix 1000 times with alternating communities and no pause; (f) a sole source A re-announces its prefix with new attributes, and A withdraws it, with no other source ever announcing it (round 11, I-3); (g) a labelled family (labelled unicast and VPN, bgp-rr reflecting VPN): C holds A's route, B's announcement owes the key, then A and B both withdraw before the round (round 11, I-6) | (a) C's wire carries A's route and, after B's announcement, NOTHING for the prefix (no re-send of A, no B). (b) C receives B's route, then A's route (one UPDATE each). (c) C ends on B's route with B's received attributes. (d) C's wire carries nothing for the prefix after E's announcement and after E's withdrawal, and bgp-rib's Loc-RIB still holds the best route C holds (RFC 4271 Section 9.2, 9.2-8 negative). (e) no admission for the key ends without a re-owe while B's announcements arrive (each is WRITTEN, RE-OWED or DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE, RE-OWED), the retry count without a decision is zero, and once B stops C holds B's last route (round 11, N-4). (f) each re-announcement reaches C as one UPDATE written zero-copy from the cached bytes and no recovery round runs for it; A's withdrawal is owed, one round decides WITHDRAW, and C receives exactly one withdrawal for the prefix (round 12, I-1: an owner withdrawal is owed whatever the contested bit says). (g) C receives exactly one MP_UNREACH_NLRI withdrawal for the prefix whose NLRI carries the Compatibility field 0x800000 in place of A's label stack (RFC 8277 Section 2.4), never the announcement framing. The attributes C receives are the elected source's received ones (AS_PATH, NEXT_HOP, MED unchanged, RFC7947-x-1/x-2/x-3) |
| AC-23 | Owner, Q-3. C negotiated ADD-PATH send for the family; A and B announce the prefix in both orders, one route worse | C receives both paths, each with its own identifier, in arrival order; no election runs and nothing is owed (RFC 7947 Section 2.3.2.2) |
| AC-24 | Owner, Q-3. A and B (the better route) announced the prefix before C's session came up; C (no ADD-PATH) comes up and is relayed both routes on the forwarded rail, through bgp-rs `replayForPeer` and, separately, through bgp-adj-rib-in's own peer-up replay; run with the relay order A first and B first. Separately, C held B's route in an earlier session and RECONNECTS, so bgp-rib's `ribOut[C]` replay (`adjOutInitial`) races the adj-rib-in relay, both orders (round 11, I-1) | C's initial table ends on B's route for the prefix in every order; when A is relayed first, A's route is written and then replaced by B's, and when B is first, A's relayed route writes nothing; on the reconnect C ends on B's route and A's relay writes nothing; in the order relay first, B's ribOut replay of the same received revision is refused and writes nothing, and in the order ribOut replay first, B's relay repeats B's route once, as forward-all does at HEAD (the forwarded rail keeps admitting its own uncontested owner). The repeat is kept on purpose (round 12, N-5 considered and not adopted): suppressing an own-owner, bit-clear announcement whose received message ID equals the entry's is not a received-ID test alone, because the relay rail re-sends a stored route under its stored ID for a live change too (bgp-rs and bgp-rr `server_validation.go` / `validation.go` relay the same `StoredRoute.MsgID` on a validation change, which bgp-rs's comment calls "A validation change is a live change.", and `RelayStoredRoute` carries it as the item's `sourceMessageID` through `validationMsgID`), so equal IDs do not prove the destination holds the bytes the relay's egress would now write; making it exact needs the written signature as well, machinery this spec does not need for convergence. End-of-RIB is still sent once per family after the replay, and it may precede the recovery write of the elected route when keys are still owed at that point, because `forwardOrderHold` releases on the sync end, not on the owed set; the client then holds the first-written route until that write lands (round 11, N-8, as the RFC 7313 sentence of Data Flow 7 accepts for refresh). Predicted at HEAD (round 11, I-1, correcting round 10's "the first replayed route stays"): the relay is forward-all, so AC-24 is red at HEAD only in the order B first, A last, where C ends on A |
| AC-25 | Owner, Q-3, bgp-rr. Sources A and B (non-clients) announce one prefix to client C without ADD-PATH, A's route better; cases (a) and (b) of AC-22; and C coming up after both announced, relayed by `routeReflector.replayForPeer` (round 11, N-8) | C ends on A's route in every order with the RR's ORIGINATOR_ID / CLUSTER_LIST handling; a candidate the RFC 4456 rules forbid reflecting to C (a non-client route at a non-client destination) is stepped past, never written, so C may be reflected a path that is not the RR's single best (Reading for bgp-rr's election, round 11, N-6) |
| AC-26 | Owner, Q-3. A owns the prefix at C; between the round's snapshot and its admission, (a) a third source F announces a route better than the round's choice, (b) the chosen candidate re-announces with changed attributes, (c) C already holds the round's choice A (B's worse announcement owed the key) and F's better announcement is accepted after the round's cut capture, so the drain does not cover it (round 11, B-2); (d) the admission's own survivor write when C holds the old owner's entry (round 11, B-1) | (a) the admission writes the round's choice and re-owes the key, and C ends on F's route after the next round; (b) the admission writes the chosen candidate's route and re-owes the key, and C ends on that candidate's newer attributes after the next round; (c) the admission writes nothing (DECIDED WITH NOTHING TO WRITE, RE-OWED), the record stays, and C ends on F's route after the next round; (d) the survivor is written, installs its owner with generation and received message ID, and is not recorded as a mover, so the round after it is not started by that write; in all four, `releaseItem` reports no `errRecoveryNotWritten` and C is not retired |
| AC-27 | Round 12, I-2, Data Flow 3b. C holds A's earlier revision of a labelled route (labelled unicast SAFI 4, and VPN SAFI 128) carrying ONE label, as ze may send it without the Multiple Labels Capability (RFC 8277 Section 2.1, round 13, I-2). A then re-announces the same key with a label stack of THREE labels, with and without ADD-PATH on A's session, and C is withheld from that revision (a next-hop gate or an egress policy denies it), so the forward rail converts A's UPDATE into C's withdrawal and ze never sends C a multi-label announcement | C's wire carries one MP_UNREACH_NLRI whose NLRI is, per route, ze's own path identifier for the path when C receives ADD-PATH for the family and none otherwise (`buildFwdBody` re-frames every forwarded NLRI to ze's identifier, round 13, N-1), the length A sent minus 48 bits, the 3 octets 0x80 0x00 0x00, then the RD (SAFI 128) and the prefix with its host bits cleared (RFC 4760 Section 5 and RFC 4271 Section 4.3: "Note that the value of trailing bits is irrelevant.", so masking changes no match, round 14, N-2), never A's label stack; an MP_UNREACH_NLRI field A's UPDATE also carried leaves with 0x800000 in each Compatibility field whatever A sent (round 13, N-5); the same NLRI bytes after the path identifier are produced for that key by the reactor's owe (Data Flow 3a) and by bgp-rib's `recoverySentNLRI`, all three through the one `nlrisplit` step; ze's UPDATEs to C never bind more than one label to a prefix; an IPv4 or IPv6 unicast withdrawal is byte-identical to HEAD's. Predicted at HEAD: red in the unit test for both families (the withdrawal carries the three labels); red in interop for SAFI 128 only (`bgp-vpn-withheld-withdraw-frr`: FRR resets the session), while FRR's labelled-unicast parser walks the copied stack to its bottom-of-stack bit and withdraws the right prefix, so SAFI 4 is green at HEAD in interop and is proven by the unit test's hex |

-> Constraint (owner, Q-3): an AC above that makes a source C's owner by announcement ORDER (AC-1, AC-5, AC-8, AC-10, AC-13, AC-14, AC-17, AC-19, AC-20) now also makes that source's route the RIB's best for C (the tests give the other sources one more AS in AS_PATH), because at a non-ADD-PATH client order no longer decides ownership; where the AC needs another source to be the survivor of choice, the AS_PATH lengths say so.

-> Constraint (owner, 2026-10-13): the replacement must hold for any number of sources and any number of simultaneous losses, unbounded. "Two drains" in Q-1 named two pipeline flushes before one RIB lookup, not a limit of two sources; the design must show (Phase 3) that back-to-back or concurrent withdrawals and session losses for one prefix converge on the RIB's current best with no per-prefix bound, and AC-12 proves it.

## End-to-End User Stories

<!-- One row per user-facing operation the feature enables. ACs verify that
     components work; stories verify the chain is connected. A broken link in a
     path is a spec gap: add the missing component to ACs, Files, and Test Plan
     before proceeding. Delete this section when Scope is tooling or docs. -->
| # | User does | Path through system | Test proving it works |
|---|-----------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| 1 | IXP member A withdraws a prefix another member B still announces; member C keeps reachability | wire -> reactor receive -> bgp-rs forward (or fast path) -> destination writer -> recovery -> bgp-rib election -> writer -> C's wire | `test/plugin/bgp-rs-withdrawal-replacement.ci`, interop `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` |
| 2 | Last member withdraws the prefix; C loses it | same path, election answers no survivor -> withdrawal | `test/plugin/bgp-rs-withdrawal-replacement.ci` (second phase) |
| 4 | IXP member B announces a prefix member A already offers with a better route; client C (no ADD-PATH) keeps A's route; then A's route worsens below B's and C moves to B (owner, Q-3) | wire -> reactor receive -> bgp-rs forward (or fast path) -> destination writer owes the announcement -> recovery -> bgp-rib election -> writer -> C's wire | `test/plugin/bgp-rs-announcement-election.ci`, interop `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` steps (2) and (3) |
| 5 | An RR client keeps the reflector's elected route when a worse iBGP route arrives later | wire -> bgp-rr `forwardUpdate` -> writer -> recovery -> bgp-rib -> writer | `test/plugin/bgp-rr-announcement-election.ci` |
| 3 | An iBGP source behind a route reflector withdraws a prefix, then its session drops; the RR client keeps the other source's route | wire -> reactor receive -> bgp-rr `forwardUpdate` / `handleStateDown` -> destination writer or owed set -> recovery worker -> bgp-rib -> writer -> client's wire | `test/plugin/bgp-rr-withdrawal-replacement.ci` |

| `TestAdjOutDepartureOwesOnlyDepartedSession` | same | Round-3 N-6, AC-18: entry owned by the departed source's current session -> nothing owed; by an earlier session (including a GR-retained route the reconnected session never re-announced) -> owed; owned by another source -> nothing owed; at an ADD-PATH destination the admission writes only identifiers owned by a no-longer-current session of the departed source; when every identifier of the WITHDRAW answer is owned by the reconnected, current session, the admission writes nothing, removes the record, completes its waiters, and C is NOT retired (outcome "decided with nothing to write", round 8 I-2) | |
| `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalRecoversOnCachedRail` | `internal/component/bgp/reactor/rs_withdrawal_replacement_test.go` | AC-1 on the cached rail, real sessions over `net.Pipe`, wire bytes parsed | |
| `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalRecoversOnFastPath` | same | AC-1 on `reactorForwardRS`; an UPDATE whose only content for C is the owed key reports handled and delivered, and C's wire receives no second copy from the cached rail (round-2 N-6) | |
| `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalAddPathSourceKeepsOtherPath` | same | AC-9 on C's wire: A received over ADD-PATH with two paths, withdraws one, C gets A's remaining path (round-2 N-5) | |

| `TestRSDepartureAfterReconnectKeepsNewerRoute` | same | AC-18: A departs, reconnects and re-announces, in both orders relative to the owe; C ends on A's newer route | |

Order: the two units lose their tags only in the commit that adds the carriers named for each requirement, so no requirement is ever without a carrier of its polarity. Before removing them the implementation re-reads `rfc4271_receive_decision_test.go` and confirms the 9-3 positive, 9-3 negative and 9.1.2-2 negative tags are still present there; the design-time read (2026-10-13) found them at `TestRFC4271AdjRIBInUpdateRunsTheDecisionProcess`, `TestRFC4271AdjRIBInUpdateThatDisplacesTheBestReselects` and `TestRFC4271LocRIBHoldsOnlyTheReplacingRoute`, and found NO other 9.2-8 negative carrier, which is why the new wire carrier is owed.

#### Owner approval commands

Approved by the owner on 2026-10-13 (Q-4, "approve all RFC-tagged test changes", then "Can you change the two tests to be normal tests instead?" and "yes"). The gate reads approvals from the committing session's own file (`tmp/commit-rfc-approved-<session>.md`), and a row is used up by the commit that carries its `RFC-approved:` trailer. So the owner runs these at the prompt of the session that implements and commits the tag removals, and runs a unit's command again if a later commit changes that unit a second time. A unit is `<package directory>.<top-level test function>`; both were checked to exist at HEAD `051876c3f4`. The new wire carriers in `rfc4271_rs_replacement_test.go` and `rfc7947_rs_decision_test.go` are new units and need none.

| Unit | Command the owner runs |
|------|------------------------|
| `rib.TestRIBBestChangeWithdraw` | `./le rfc approve unit rib.TestRIBBestChangeWithdraw reason 'Thomas, 2026-10-13: "Can you change the two tests to be normal tests instead?" (spec-bgp-rs-replacement-on-withdrawal: every RFC tag removed; 9.2-7 carried by the new wire carriers, 9-3 and 9.1.2-2 by rfc4271_receive_decision_test.go)'` |
| `rib.TestRIBBestChangeNoPublishSameBest` | `./le rfc approve unit rib.TestRIBBestChangeNoPublishSameBest reason 'Thomas, 2026-10-13: "Can you change the two tests to be normal tests instead?" (spec-bgp-rs-replacement-on-withdrawal: every RFC tag removed; 9.2-7 and 9.2-8 negative carried by new wire carriers, 9-3 by rfc4271_receive_decision_test.go)'` |

No other RFC-tagged test changes. Tags on `forward_withdrawal_ownership_test.go`
(`TestRSWithdrawalOwnershipRails` and siblings) are read for regressions only;
AC-8 keeps them green. The existing RFC7947 carriers (x-1 to x-4, 2.2-1,
2.2.2.2-1) are unchanged; x-5 gains the two new carriers above. The "newly
installed routes" half of the 9.2-7 sentence is exercised by
`TestRFC7947RouteServerWorseReannouncementYieldsOtherSourceRoute` but not claimed under 9.2-7,
which keeps one claim per carrier.
