### Interop Tests (Scope: protocol)
<!-- REQUIRED when wire-visible behavior changes. See
     ai/rules/interop-and-goal-validation.md, including the vacuity traps: prove
     the test FAILS when the behavior under test is reverted. -->
| Scenario | Directory | Peer Daemon | What It Proves | Status |
|----------|-----------|-------------|----------------|--------|
| `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/bgp-route-server-replacement-frr/` (ze.conf, frr.conf, gobgp config, bird.conf) + checker `checkRouteServerReplacement` registered in `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_special.go`, listed in `bgp_test.go` | FRR and GoBGP as sources, BIRD as the non-ADD-PATH client (three-daemon layout already runs in `bgp-addpath-readvertise-collision-frr`, round-1 N3) | AC-4. Checker: sessions up; BIRD's session to Ze did not negotiate ADD-PATH for the family (asserted, not assumed); GoBGP announces the prefix with one prepended AS (`opExec` `gobgp global rib add ... aspath`), and BIRD holds GoBGP's path; FRR announces (`opExec` vtysh `network`), and BIRD's path for the prefix shows FRR's AS_PATH (better later route displaces, owner Q-3); GoBGP re-announces the prefix, still prepended, with a changed community, and BIRD still shows FRR's AS_PATH through a stability window (worse later route does not displace, owner Q-3); FRR withdraws (`opExec` vtysh `no network`), and BIRD still holds the prefix with GoBGP's AS_PATH and holds it for a stability window. Revert-to-red, twice, each with a rebuilt Ze image: the announcement half reverted fails at the "still FRR" assertion; the withdrawal half reverted fails at the "still present" assertion; record both red runs | |
| `bgp-vpn-withheld-withdraw-frr` (round 12, I-2, AC-27; renamed and re-shaped by round 13, I-1 and I-2, from `bgp-labeled-withheld-withdraw-frr`) | `test/interop/scenarios/bgp-vpn-withheld-withdraw-frr/` (ze.conf, frr.conf, inject-args, inject.msg), checker registered beside `checkLabeledWithdrawCompatibility` in `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/` | raw injector as the source, FRR as the client | The sending half of RFC 8277 Section 2.4 on the forward rail, discriminated on SAFI 128. The injector announces, in ipv4/mpls-vpn, 10.10.0.0/24 and 10.11.0.0/24 under one RD, each with ONE label, which ze forwards and FRR holds (ze never sends a multi-label announcement: RFC 8277 Section 2.1, ze sends no Multiple Labels Capability). It then re-announces 10.10.0.0/24 under a THREE-label stack with an attribute ze's egress policy denies to FRR, so ze converts that UPDATE into FRR's withdrawal. After the fix: FRR loses exactly 10.10.0.0/24, keeps 10.11.0.0/24, its session with ze stays Established, and ze's sent withdrawal (ze's own sent log) carries 0x800000 and the length minus 48. A second, NON-discriminating leg does the same in ipv4/mpls-label (10.20.0.0/24 and 10.21.0.0/24) and asserts FRR loses exactly 10.20.0.0/24: it proves FRR accepts the converted SAFI 4 form, and it is excluded from the revert claim. HEAD, read at FRR 10.3.1's producers (round 13, I-1): `bgp_nlri_parse_vpn` (`bgpd/bgp_mplsvpn.c`) reads one 3-octet label, then the 8-octet RD, and checks `(psize - VPN_PREFIXLEN_MIN_BYTES) > prefix_blen(&p)` first; HEAD's copied three-label withdrawal has prefix length 72+64+24 = 160 bits, psize 20, and 20 - 11 = 9 exceeds IPv4's 4, so the parser returns `BGP_NLRI_PARSE_ERROR_PACKET_LENGTH`, and `bgp_update_receive` (`bgpd/bgp_packet.c`) answers any NLRI parse error other than prefix overflow with `bgp_notify_send` (UPDATE Message Error) and `BGP_Stop`. So at HEAD the session drops and FRR loses both VPN routes: the "keeps 10.11.0.0/24" and "stays Established" assertions go red. At HEAD the SAFI 4 leg is green: `bgp_nlri_parse_label` (`bgpd/bgp_label.c`) walks the copied stack to its bottom-of-stack bit and withdraws 10.20.0.0/24, and GoBGP's `MPLSLabelStack.DecodeFromBytes` walks the same way, so neither peer discriminates SAFI 4 and its proof is `TestFwdWithdrawalLabelledCompatibility`'s hex. Revert-to-red with a rebuilt Ze image: `writeWithdrawal` restored to copy `mp.reach` fails the SAFI 128 assertions above; record the red run. The injector's three-label announcement depends on ze accepting it at reception, as HEAD does by the bottom-of-stack walk (RFC8277-2.2-2); it is an input ze receives, never one ze sends. Read in round 13 at FRR 10.3.1's source (`bgp_mplsvpn.c`, `bgp_packet.c`, `bgp_label.c` `bgp_nlri_get_labels`): a three-label VPN route can never be held by FRR (the same psize check), which is why the earlier revision carries one label | |

### Goal Validation (planned evidence)

| Goal (Task section) | Evidence that will prove it |
|---------------------|-----------------------------|
| Client without ADD-PATH receives the remaining source's route after a withdrawal | `bgp-rs-withdrawal-replacement.ci` exact-hex assertion on conn 3, and interop `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` against BIRD with the recorded red |
| Withdrawal still sent when nothing remains | `.ci` second phase exact withdrawal hex; `TestRFC4271RouteServerLastSourceWithdrawalIsSent` |
| RFC4271-9.2-7 verdict enforced | discrimination records in `rfc/discrimination/rfc4271.json` for both new carriers; re-judged audit verdict |
| Replacement holds for any number of sources and simultaneous losses (owner constraint, AC-12) | `TestOwedKeyRealRIBConvergenceProperty` output with its seed range, `TestOwedKeyLivenessUnderChurn`, `TestOwedKeyGRPurgeRecoversSurvivor` (session loss of a GR-capable source, AC-19), and `bgp-rs-withdrawal-replacement-many.ci` exact hex |
| bgp-rr clients keep a reachable prefix after a source withdraws or departs | `bgp-rr-withdrawal-replacement.ci` exact hex on the client |
| A non-ADD-PATH client holds the RIB-elected route on every announcement, bgp-rs and bgp-rr; an ADD-PATH client holds every path (owner, Q-3; RFC7947-x-5) | `bgp-rs-announcement-election.ci`, its fast-path twin and `bgp-rr-announcement-election.ci` exact hex; interop `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` steps (2) and (3) against BIRD with the recorded red; discrimination records for `TestRFC7947RouteServerWorseReannouncementYieldsOtherSourceRoute` and `TestRFC7947RouteServerWorseLaterRouteDoesNotDisplace` in `rfc/discrimination/rfc7947.json`, each red under the announcement-half break and the positive red at HEAD (round 11, I-5); `TestRSAnnouncementAddPathClientGetsEveryPath` |

## Files to Modify
<!-- MUST include feature code (internal/*, cmd/*), not only test files.
     Check each file's // Design: annotation: if the change alters behavior the
     referenced architecture doc describes, list that doc here too. -->
- `internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_ownership.go` - `adjOutWrite.allow` / `adjOutWrite.record` (round-3 I-5 rewrote this row to match Data Flow 3 and 7): an owner's forwarded withdrawal (received or synthesized) and a bgp-rib lifecycle removal (`adjOutExpected` withdrawal) on a non-ADD-PATH destination with a registered recovery owner become "owed" instead of written; for an owed key, an originated announcement installs the originated owner and removes the record, a forwarded or `adjOutInitial` announcement merges and notes an ANNOUNCE mover (owner Q-3), an `adjOutRecovery` announcement is the admission's own write and installs the candidate (round 11, B-1), an `adjOutExpected` announcement is refused and leaves it, an `adjOutExpected` withdrawal merges into it, an originated withdrawal is written and leaves it, and a forwarded withdrawal notes a mover; the epoch moves on the four events Data Flow 7 lists (round 11, N-1); `case adjOutRecovery` gains its announcement branch (Data Flow 3a); the contested bit and the tombstone set are read and staged here (round 11, I-3, I-4); an announcement-created record's NLRI copy is taken in withdrawal framing (round 11, I-6); every owner-installing write records the owner's session generation; every effect on the owed set (owe, merge, removal, mover note, owed-refused count, wake) is staged on the `adjOutWrite` during `filter`, reset between the two filter passes with `owners` and `withdrawn`, and applied once per write (Data Flow 3, round 7 I-1); the owed key is copied out before `adjOutWrite.release` (Design: `docs/architecture/update-building.md`). Owner, Q-3 (Data Flow 3a): at a non-ADD-PATH destination with a registered recovery owner, a forwarded or `adjOutInitial` announcement of a key with an entry or a record is owed (empty set, or merged) instead of admitted, where HEAD admits the forwarded one over any entry and refuses the initial one; on a record it notes an ANNOUNCE mover and a current-announcer entry (source, generation); with no entry and no record it is admitted as today; an originated announcement installs and removes the record
- `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api_forward.go` - `forwardUpdateSection` labels EVERY section of a recovery operation `adjOutRecovery`, announcements included, where HEAD relabels only withdrawals and synthesized withdrawals (round 11, B-1, Data Flow 3a); the comment above the relabel is rewritten to say so (`ai/rules/stale-comments.md`)
- `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_replay.go`, `internal/component/bgp/types/types.go`, `internal/component/bgp/plugins/cmd/update/update_text.go`, `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api_batch.go`, `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_provenance.go` - round 11, I-2: `replayGroup` gains the received `SourceMessageID` and `groupKey` splits by it; `replayRoutesWithCursor` sets the new `InitialSourceMessageMeta` (`initial-source-message`, declared beside `InitialSourceOwnerMeta`); `NLRIBatch` gains `InitialSourceMessage`, read by `initialReplayFromMeta` and required for a non-local replay; `sendBatchUpdate` sets the `adjOutInitial` owner's `sourceMessageID`; `writePath`'s `adjOutInitial` branch returns it
- `internal/core/bgp/nlri/nlrisplit/` - round 11, I-6, re-declared by round 12, I-2: `prefix_key.go` and `register.go` gain, beside `keyLabeled` / `keyVPN` (round 13, I-3, Data Flow 3a): ONE unexported step writing one withdrawal NLRI `[bits+24][0x80 0x00 0x00][RD?][prefix]` with host bits cleared (RFC 8277 Section 2.4 quoted above the statement that writes the Compatibility field; RFC 4760 Section 5 and RFC 4271 Section 4.3, "Note that the value of trailing bits is irrelevant.", quoted above the statement that clears the host bits, round 14, N-2), the one declaration of the framing; the `WithdrawalWriter` type, the `withdrawalWriters` map beside `withdrawalSplitters` and `GetWithdrawalWriter`, registered for IPv4 and IPv6 SAFI 4 and SAFI 128, a field-level writer that loops internally with no callback; and `WithdrawalNLRIFromKey`, the per-key inverse built on the same step, answering `ErrUnsupported` for a family with no writer. `keyLabeled`'s label loop and `splitByBitLength`'s per-entry length check (`cidr.go`) each move into one unexported step their old owner and the writer both call, so neither is written twice in new code (`SplitLabeled` and `ExtractLabels` in `labeled.go` keep their own label walks, unchanged). Round 14, N-1: the writer passes `maxBits` 255 (`maxLengthOctetBits`, as `splitVPN` does), because the SAFI 4 and SAFI 128 length octet counts the labels; for a well-formed stack `SplitLabeled` and length-octet framing give the same entry length (`1+labelBytes+ceil(prefixBits/8)` = `1+ceil(totalBits/8)`). The shared per-entry step returns the entry length and a failure kind, never an `error`, and each caller formats its own `fmt.Errorf` text (today's texts and the `count` returned on error unchanged), so the step stays inlinable and `splitCIDR`, the unicast NLRI hot path, gains no real call (the round-14 probe found a step holding the three `fmt.Errorf` calls not inlinable). `BenchmarkSplitCIDR` (new benchmark in the existing `nlrisplit/cidr_test.go`, `b.ReportAllocs`) is run at HEAD before the move and after it, both results pasted in Evidence. Callers: the reactor's owe of an announcement-created record and bgp-rib's `recoverySentNLRI` call `WithdrawalNLRIFromKey`; the forward rail's `writeWithdrawal` calls the writer (Data Flow 3b)
- `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_build.go` - round 12, I-2, Data Flow 3b: `writeWithdrawal` (with `buildWithdrawalPayload`'s sizing) for a family `nlrisplit.GetWithdrawalWriter` answers, passes both MP halves through the writer (MP_REACH announcement framed, MP_UNREACH withdrawal framed, round 13, N-5) under the source context's ADD-PATH state instead of copying them verbatim; `buildWithdrawalPayload` gains the source encoding context parameter, `fwdWithdrawal.of` passes `bgpctx.Registry.Get(base.SourceCtxID())`, and a nil context for a labelled family returns 0 (round 13, N-3); the header size comes from the unconverted bound and both length fields are backfilled, so no sizing walk runs (round 13, N-4); allocation-free (no closure, no callback, round 13, I-3), within the `len(payload)+4` bound; the doc comments of `buildWithdrawalPayload`, `withdrawalMPHalves` and `writeWithdrawal` updated to say so (`ai/rules/stale-comments.md`)
- `internal/component/bgp/reactor/adj_rib_out.go` - `adjRIBOutRoute` gains the sticky `contested` bool and `adjRIBOut` gains the key-only tombstone set beside `routes`, created only at a non-ADD-PATH destination and cleared at all three sites that clear `routes`: `adjRIBOut.reset`, `Session.beginAdjOut`'s session-change clear (`session_ownership.go`) and `Session.retireWrite`'s write-failure clear (`session_write.go`) (round 11, I-3, I-4; round 13, N-6); `adjRIBOut` holds the owed set beside `routes`; `adjRIBOut.reset` clears it and releases waiters; `adjOutPath` gains the owner's source session generation (round-3 N-6) and the owner's received message ID (owner Q-3: the "already holds the elected route" test, Data Flow 3a; `adjOutSameOwner` ignores both); the `adjRIBOut` comment "Its only readers are final writers under writeMu" is rewritten (round-3 N-10)
- `internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_write.go` - `Session.retireWrite` clears the owed set and the tombstone set (round 13, N-6) with `routes` and stops (never joins) the recovery worker; `writeUpdateBody` resets the staged owed-set effects with `owners` and `withdrawn` before its second filter pass and applies them once on each exit after the last pass that ran (the `length == 0` return, the PATHS-LIMIT `n == 0` return, the write path), never on a filter error; a raw write whose filter pass returned no error applies only its staged record removals, before `record`, and an opaque raw write applies nothing (Data Flow 3, round 7 I-1, round 8 I-1)

The leave-Established arm calls the release only and stores `done` in the leave record; the down defer waits on it after the join (Data Flow 5, step 3). `applyBFDOptInChange` and `Peer.cleanup` keep the release-then-wait form. The `stopBFDClient` doc comment ("Called from the FSM callback on exit from StateEstablished ... waits for the subscriber goroutine to exit") and the leave arm's "Runs before clearEncodingContexts so the subscriber goroutine has observed the final StateChange" comment are rewritten to say where the wait now runs (`ai/rules/stale-comments.md`)

A case that only parsed `recovery-cut` is deleted with the input it tested; every other guard keeps its assertion at the carrier named in the table below. None is relaxed

### Guards of deleted code and their new carriers (round-4 I-3)

Every existing unit that drives the deleted inline loop, `recoverySnapshot`, or a cut, with the guard it carries, where that guard lives after this spec, and whether its assertion moves between layers. "Rewritten" means the unit stays, drives departure through the owed set and the recovery worker, and keeps its assertion; "retired" means the function it calls is deleted (no-layering) and the guard is asserted at the named carrier. A row whose assertion changes meaning is marked BEHAVIOUR CHANGES.

Setup (round 5, I-4). Under Data Flow 4 a departure owes a key only where the departed source owns the destination's entry through a session that is no longer current, so a unit rewritten mechanically, with no seeded ownership or with the departed source still Established, would write nothing, never call the provider, go red, and invite a loosened assertion. The Setup column names what each rewritten departure unit adds. **S** means: seed the departed source's ownership at the destination for EVERY key the unit requests, then end the departed source's session before the departure command runs, so that `forwardSourceCurrent` is false for the stored generation because the state is no longer Established (`setState` moves `forwardGeneration` only on `PeerStateEstablished`, and `Peer.Stop` also moves it; round-6 I-3 a). `recoveryAdvertiseFailed` (`relay_recovery_test.go`) does both: it creates the departed peer 192.0.2.10, adds it to `r.peers`, forwards each seed body to the destination and then calls `setState(PeerStateStopped)`, so a unit that seeds through it already has its session ended (round-6 I-3 b). A unit with live sessions ends the source the way a real departure does instead (N-7 row below). A unit that departs no source at all has no S and names its own Setup.

| Existing unit | Guard it carries | Setup | New carrier | Assertion |
|---------------|------------------|-------|-------------|-----------|
| `reactor.TestRecoveryFinalWorkerRequestsCausalReresolution` | unrelated traffic never drops a required replacement; an old DOWN never overwrites a newer target advertisement | S: today it departs `192.0.2.10` with no seeded ownership | rewritten; old-DOWN half also `TestRSDepartureAfterReconnectKeepsNewerRoute`, `TestAdjOutDepartureOwesOnlyDepartedSession` (AC-18) | unrelated-prefix subcase becomes stricter: replacement written once, no retry |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryFinalWorkerRejectsChangedSourceGeneration` | a stale candidate whose source session was replaced is never written | N-A (retired) | retired (calls `recoverySnapshot` and the admission fence directly); `TestOwedKeyFenceInterleavings` (j) | BEHAVIOUR CHANGES: from "retry the election" to "re-owe the key with that source excluded and re-elect in the next round" (round-5 N-7, replacing round-3 N-1's step to the next candidate); "never written" kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryLookupRejectsReconnectedSource` | the elected source reconnects inside the lookup; its old bytes never reach the writer | S: today no ownership is seeded for the requested key | rewritten against the worker; plus (j) | old bytes never written, kept; the reconnected source's new bytes now reach C through its own forward, not a re-election |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryMixedPolicySectionsCompleteOnce` | one completion per operation over mixed policy sections; no duplicate completion | S: today it seeds IPv6 and 198.51.100.0/24 but requests `syncOrderPrefixWire`, which must be seeded too | rewritten; completion half also `TestOwedKeyMergesDepartureAndWithdrawal` (every waiter released once) | the sibling "self-induced sequence conflict" half retires with the sequence fence; completion-once kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryCanceledAndStoppedOperation` | child resource owners released, exactly one completion, including an operation never written | Not S (round-6 I-3 c): the unit departs no source and requests no key; it builds a `recoveryAdmission` from `recoverySnapshot` and hands it straight to `dispatchOverflow` / `safeBatchHandle`, so rewritten with no owed record its admission would find none and `require.Empty(conn.written())` would pass vacuously. Its own Setup: put an owed record for the admission's key in the destination's owed set, carrying the captured epoch and the destination session, so the cancelled admission acts on a live record and both halves can assert the record survives (round 7, N-2: the stopped half never reaches the handler, because `dispatchOverflow` on a stopped pool calls `releaseItem` and returns false, so its `require.Empty(conn.written())` was never vacuous; the seed makes the cancel half's `recovery.ctx.Err()` check discriminating) | rewritten; `TestOwedKeyMergesDepartureAndWithdrawal` (cancelled waiter) and `TestOwedKeyWorkerLifecycle` ("session retired") | kept, plus: the record survives the cancelled admission (left owed for the next round) |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryParserRetainsNativeAddPathAndPrunesSent` | real hex parser, registered RIB, delivery drain and TCP keep native ADD-PATH identities 0 and 7 apart | S: today the departed source (`extendedRecoveryPeers`) is still Established, so its generation is current. `extendedRecoveryPeers` runs real sessions with real delivery workers, so the source is NOT ended with `setState(PeerStateStopped)`, which would leave the FSM and the reactor state disagreeing under a running `Session.Run` (round-6 N-7): the unit closes the source's `lowLivePeer.remote` connection, a real departure, and waits until the peer has left Established before the departure command runs | rewritten; `RecoveryCut` input dropped | kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryHardFailureRetiresDestination` | a refusing lookup owner retires the destination with Cease | S: today `RecoverySource` names a live `extendedRecoveryPeers` source, ended by closing its connection as in the `ParserRetainsNativeAddPathAndPrunesSent` row (round-6 N-7) | rewritten for departure; owner-withdrawal twin `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalLookupFailureRetiresDestination` (AC-11) | kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoverySourceLossBeforeEnqueueReresolves` | `errForwardNoSource` from the real forward gate (`forwardUpdateValidated`, reached from `queueRecovery`; round-6 N-3) | already S (seeds and stops) | rewritten against the worker's admission; plus (j) | BEHAVIOUR CHANGES: from re-resolution to re-owing the key with the lost source excluded (round-5 N-7); "the lost source's route is never written" kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryAddPathWritesEntireLookup` | several destination-framed identifiers, including zero, admitted as one operation without a rescan per sibling | S: already met, because it seeds through `recoveryAdvertiseFailed`, which creates and stops the departed peer (round-6 I-3 b) | rewritten for the worker's ADD-PATH admission | kept, plus the per-identifier owner test of Data Flow 7 |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryDestinationReconnectInvalidatesLookup` | a destination session swapped during selection (send counter unchanged) never receives the old lookup's write | N-A (retired) | retired with the sequence fence; `TestOwedKeyWorkerLifecycle` (table reset clears the owed set; an old session's record is never admitted on the next session) | kept, now met by the record's session check |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryWriterFailureOverridesPartialRetry` | a body, deadline or flush failure after a partial retry ends the destination with Cease / Out of Resources; no later section escapes | already S (seeds and stops) | rewritten: a key left owed for the next round never masks a write failure of another key in the same admission | kept |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryRetryOwnsReplacementSession` | a failed attempt never retires a reconnect it did not own; "admitted-replacement" half: a retry started on the replacement session fails and retires it | S: today it departs `192.0.2.10` with no seeded ownership | first guard rewritten; `TestOwedKeyWorkerLifecycle` (AC-11 self-retire targets only the session the record names). "Admitted-replacement" half RETIRED: records are per destination session, a table reset completes them with "session retired", and nothing carries over to the replacement session, so no retry can start there | first guard kept; second half retired, no counterpart by construction |
| `reactor.TestRecoveryWaitsForFilteredReceivedPublication` (`forward_publication_ownership_test.go`) | recovery waits for the survivor B's filtered received publication, marker included; on a failed publication no RIB lookup runs and the DESTINATION is sent Cease / Out of Resources | S for the departed source A | rewritten: the worker's drain in the snapshot-then-cut order (Data Flow 5); `RecoveryCut = ^uint64(0)` dropped; worker-failure half also `TestOwedKeyPublicationFailureTearsDownSource` | success half kept. BEHAVIOUR CHANGES, worker-failure half (round 5, B-1): "the destination is retired" becomes "the source B is torn down with Cease / Out of Resources and C is not retired"; "no lookup over an unpublished source" becomes "the lookup runs with B in every key's excluded-source set", so C receives the withdrawal (no candidate remains) and never B's withdrawn route |
| `reactor.TestFinalWriterMixedWithdrawalIntent` (`session_ownership_writer_test.go`) | a filtered source withdrawal moves `sentUpdateSequence`, so a pending recovery sees it | N-A | the sequence assertion is deleted with `sentUpdateSequence` and `noteFilteredOperation` (round-5 N-3); the purpose, that a dropped withdrawal is never invisible to a pending decision, is carried by `TestOwedKeyFenceInterleavings` (a) and (e): a dropped non-owner withdrawal for an owed key notes a mover | the rest of the unit kept |
| `rib.TestRecoveryNativeSentIdentityAndNewerOwner` | the departed source's reconnect advertisement (id 99, message 91 above cut 90) is never withdrawn, and a repeated DOWN withdraws nothing | N-A (RIB unit) | MOVES LAYER: the RIB's cut-less `SentAddPath` branch now answers WITHDRAW for id 99 too, so the RIB assertion changes to "every sent identifier of the source"; the guard moves to the reactor's ADD-PATH admission (Data Flow 7: only identifiers owned by a no-longer-current session are written), carried by `TestAdjOutDepartureOwesOnlyDepartedSession` (ADD-PATH half) and a reconnect case in `TestRSDepartureAfterReconnectKeepsNewerRoute` at an ADD-PATH destination | RIB assertion changes; wire assertion kept at the reactor |
| `rib.TestRecoveryBatchedIdentitiesShareOneElectionResult` | one batched result covers every prefix and failed identifier once; "skips new ownership" (id 99) | N-A (RIB unit) | same move as the unit above for the id-99 half; the batching half stays in the RIB (the count becomes three identifiers per prefix) | RIB count changes; skip-new-ownership guard at the reactor |
| `rib.TestRetainRoutesLiveSourceWithdrawsPrunedFamilies` (`llgr_sent_lifecycle_test.go`, `Cut: 91` in the DOWN handoff) | the DOWN handoff withdraws the unretained identity once and its sent withdrawal prunes ownership | N-A (RIB unit) | rewritten: excluded set {source}, no cut; outcome WITHDRAW | kept |

## Implementation Steps

<!-- Concrete phases of work, not a restatement of the /ze-implement stages

     schema before resolution, resolution before CLI. Each phase follows TDD
     (write test -> fail -> implement -> pass) and ends with a self-critical
     review; fix what it finds before starting the next phase. -->

1. **Phase: Wiring (MANDATORY FIRST)** -- recreate the red probe, write the failing rail tests

2. **Phase: RIB per-key exclusion** -- `RecoveryRequest` per-key excluded-source sets, no mode field and no cut; `RecoveryRoute` received path identifier and ADD-PATH flag; `recoveryRoutes` / `recoveryRouteLocked` skip each excluded source's candidates and the destination's own, elect without requiring a `ribOut` entry, answer every key with SURVIVORS (repeated-`SelectBest` order) or WITHDRAW, the `SentAddPath` branch without its cut, drop the withdraw-branch owner check; per-key IPC form in `recoveryCommand`; R-12's sent-projection check
   - Tests: `TestRecoveryOwnerWithdrawalKeepsSourceOtherPaths`, `TestRecoveryOwnerWithdrawalAnswersWithdrawWhenNoSurvivor`, `TestRecoveryElectsWithoutSentEntry`, `TestRecoveryDepartureExcludesEverySourceCandidate`, `TestRecoveryMergedExclusionSet`, `TestRecoveryAddPathDepartureWithdrawsEverySentIdentifier`, `TestRecoveryAnswersEveryRequestedKey`, `TestRecoveryExcludesDestinationCandidate`, `TestRecoveryAnswersOrderedCandidates`, `TestRIBSentProjectionOfPurgedOwnerRemoves`
   - Files: `ribevents/recovery.go`, `plugins/rib/rib_recovery.go`, `plugins/rib/rib_structured.go`, `plugins/rib/rib.go`, `plugins/rib/sent_owner.go`, `plugins/rib/rib_sent_lifecycle.go` (R-12's check keyed on `recovery-write`, its dispatcher; round-5 N-5), `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md`, and one row in `plan/journal/` (grep the classes first) for the HEAD window in which a purge lands before the sent projection of a FORWARDED write of a just-retained source, which this spec does not change (round-5 I-1)
   - Verify: tests fail, implement, pass; existing `rib_recovery_test.go` green
2b. **Phase: publication-failure teardown (defect fix, its own commit, before Phase 3; round-5 I-2)** -- the receive delivery worker's three `publication.fail()` sites, which already set `receivePublication.failed`, call `Session.teardownAutomatic` with Cease / Out of Resources on the session the worker serves (A-11), and the false comment is corrected. The same commit moves the down event after the session's buffered deliveries and after its leave transition (round 7, I-2; round 8, B-1 and I-3; Data Flow 5): `close(deliverChan)`, `<-deliveryDone` and the down producer become `runOnce`'s down defer, which fires `EventTCPConnectionFails` when the FSM still reads Established, drains, then waits on the leave signal when the FSM's entered-Established bit is set; `notifyPeerClosed` is split, its observer loop, `raiseSessionDropped`, the "session closed" log line, the leave-Established history row and `scheduleDynamicPeerCleanup` run in the down defer after both waits, and the callback keeps the closing mark, the other reactor bookkeeping, `clearEncodingContexts` and `setState(PeerStateConnecting)`, then closes the leave signal. This also fixes the pre-existing "session ends with no down event" defect (a `Session.Run` that returns with the FSM Established, a read-goroutine panic), which this spec relies on, so no journal row is written for it; the reason values do not change in this half, so it needs nothing from D1. Its second half, the no-retention input, makes D1's reason producer, now running after `<-deliveryDone`, read `receivePublication.failed` beside `notificationExchanged`: the reason is `rpc.ReasonNotification` when the flag is set, else the new declared `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid` when the fact is set, so the reason declares "no retention" whether or not the Cease reached the wire without claiming a NOTIFICATION that was never delivered; GR's no-retention test calls the declared predicate `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention`, and the Close Reasons table gains the row (round-6 I-2, "adj-rib-in invalid", owner Q-6). That half lands in this phase when D1 has landed, otherwise as the first step after D1 lands, with the GR-capable half of AC-17
   - Tests: `TestOwedKeyPublicationFailureTearsDownSource` teardown half (a panicking receiver and a nil receiver tear X down with Cease / Out of Resources; no wait on `deliveryDone`; `-race`, deadline), `TestSessionDownFollowsBufferedDeliveries` (round 7, I-2; with round 8's contended teardown, hold-timer, Established-at-return, read-goroutine panic and Idle-race cases, and round 9's BFD-subscriber-drained leave and reactor-stop cases, the latter asserting on `Reactor.Wait` and then `Peer.Wait`, and round 10's forward-worker GR congestion case; the reactor-stop red at HEAD is owed at implementation), then the GR-capable half of `TestOwedKeyPublicationFailureTearsDownSource` (Cease delivered, Cease unsent, and the late failure: no retention)
   - Files: `reactor/peer_run.go` (the down defer, the leave signal and leave record), `reactor/peer_bfd.go` (`stopBFDClient`'s non-waiting release and the wait moved to the down defer, round 9 B-1), `fsm/fsm.go` (the entered-Established bit, round 8 B-1), `reactor/reactor_notify.go` (`notifyPeerClosed` split, `scheduleDynamicPeerCleanup` after the observer loop, round 8 N-3), `reactor/peer_derived_bindings.go` (`announceClosed`: the mark stays in the callback, the down read moves), `reactor/reactor_api.go` (unchanged: `apiStateObserver.OnPeerClosed` reads `peer.State()` later, and no reader of the down event's `State` field depends on it, checked in round 8 N-6), `docs/architecture/behavior/fsm.md` ("Reactor Notification"), no new fact field (`receivePublication.failed` is the fact), `pkg/plugin/rpc/enums.go` (the second reason constant and `ReasonForbidsRetention`), `plugins/gr/gr.go` (always: both handlers call the predicate; round-6 I-2), `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` (Close Reasons row)
   - Verify: red at HEAD (the source stays Established after the failure), then green; commit on its own before Phase 3
3. **Phase: Owed set, recovery worker, per-key fence** -- `adjOutWrite.allow` records owed keys (received and synthesized owner withdrawals on both rails, and bgp-rib lifecycle removals), applies the per-authority rule to owed keys and notes movers, and records the owner's session generation; the recovery-owner check through the frozen registry `Lookup`; the per-destination recovery worker with its non-blocking wake, its own per-round drain in the snapshot-then-cut order, its 500-key chunks and its lifecycle (start before Established, stop from any teardown path, stop-then-join in the `runOnce` defer); every adj-out table reset clears the owed set; departure owes a key only where a no-longer-current session of the departed source owns it, instead of the inline loop (deleted), and `recovery-cut` is deleted end to end; admission checks record, session, epoch and movers; `queueRecovery` steps past a denied candidate and re-owes a key whose candidate no longer resolves, with that source excluded (round-5 N-7); the round excludes every source whose publication failed or stopped (round-5 B-1); the recovery write carries `recovery-write`; the per-peer owed-refusal counter (round-5 I-3, round-6 B-1)

The owe in Phase 3a calls `WithdrawalNLRIFromKey`, so this phase RUNS BEFORE Phase 3a; its number follows Data Flow 3b, not the run order

### Critical Review Checklist

<!-- Feature-SPECIFIC checks. The generic ones in ai/rules/quality.md always
     apply and are not repeated here. A row that would read the same on any spec
     is not worth a row. -->
| Check | What to verify for this spec |
|-------|------------------------------|
| Completeness | Every AC-N has an implementation at file and symbol |
| Feature completeness | Both user stories have a passing `.ci` and the interop run |
| Correctness | A replacement carries the survivor's own attributes and next hop, never A's; the replacement's sent owner is the survivor so a later B withdrawal is admitted; an owner-withdrawal key never excludes A's other paths |
| Correctness | The owed set is never dropped: every owed key ends as a written replacement, a written withdrawal, a decision with nothing to write, a removal by an originated announcement (a forwarded or initial one merges, owner Q-3; round 11, N-1), or a retired session, and a decision flagged RE-OWED BY A MOVER keeps the key owed (round 11, B-2); a bgp-rib replay never removes a record (round-3 B-1); a key whose epoch moved is re-decided, never discarded |
| Correctness | No self-wait: nothing that runs on a destination's forward worker or on a source read goroutine waits for a drain, a lookup or an admission (round-1 B1) |
| Correctness | No livelock: a key's admission is retried only when a departure merged into it, the candidate about to be written withdrew it between snapshot and admission, or the chosen survivor's session ended or restarted (`errRelayNoSource`, `errForwardNoSource` or the write-time source check, a re-owe with that source excluded, at most one per source per record) (round-1 B2, round-2 I-2, round-5 N-7, round-6 N-2). No stall either: every owe, merge and re-owe wakes the worker, an admission that leaves a key owed starts the next round itself, a lost source re-owes only its own key and never blocks its siblings, and no `retry` flag survives beside the re-owe (round 7, I-3) |
| Correctness | No message-ID comparison decides ownership (round-1 I1, round-3 N-6): the writer's record decides "owner moved", and the owner's session generation, read under the table mutex, decides whether a departure concerns the key |
| Correctness | A departure writes to a destination only where the departed source owns the key there through a session that is no longer current (round-2 B-1, round-3 N-6); an old DOWN never overwrites the reconnected source's newer route (AC-18); a GR-retained purge is decided like a departure (AC-19) |
| Correctness | No silent "nothing": every requested key gets one RIB outcome, a missing one is a lookup error; another source's stopped publication never retires the destination (AC-17), the round excludes that source from every key (round-5 B-1), and a failed publication ends that source's session rather than leaving it silently unpublished (round-3 I-1); an owed-key refusal is counted on the destination's per-peer owed-refusal counter, never silent, and no command waits for a queued write to count it (round-5 I-3, round-6 B-1) |
| Naming | The per-key set is named for what it holds (excluded sources), and the two outcomes for what the writer does (SURVIVORS, WITHDRAW); no mode field exists |
| Data flow | No RIB lookup or IPC under `writeMu`; no new RS-side route store; reactor reaches the RIB only through `ribevents` or the registered command |
| Rule: goroutine-lifecycle | One long-lived recovery worker per destination session, started before Established is published and stopped with it; stop never joins, the `runOnce` defer stops then joins; no goroutine per withdrawal or per recovery round; the forward worker and the read goroutine never run recovery |
| Rule: no-layering | `recoverNLRIBatch`'s inline loop and the destination-wide sequence fence are deleted, not kept as a departure fallback |
| Rule: performance | Announced NLRIs keep the zero-copy cached write; allocations confined to the cold recovery copy; withdrawal-heavy perf run handed to the owner (Mac) |
| Rule: rfc-compliance | RFC 4271 Section 9.2 quote above the statement choosing replacement or withdrawal; callers carry `// RFC 4271 Section 9.2` |

|-------------|---------------------|
