### Security Review Checklist

<!-- Feature-specific: untrusted input, injection, resource exhaustion, error
     leakage, authorization that could fail open. -->
| Check | What to look for |
|-------|-----------------|
| Input validation | Withdrawn NLRIs come from a peer; recovery re-parses them through the existing `recoveryIdentity` and refuses malformed framing with an error, never a silent skip |
| Resource exhaustion | The owed set is unbounded by owner decision (AC-12), so the defence is coalescing, not a cap: one record per distinct key per destination, however many withdrawals, departures, lifecycle removals or re-announcements name it; a round decides every key owed when it started and the keys owed during it join the next round. A withdrawal storm therefore costs memory proportional to distinct keys awaiting a decision, never to events, and no queue, slot or table drops or blocks (round-1 I6 rewrote the earlier "bounded by one UPDATE's NLRIs ... processed before the next item on that worker", which contradicted AC-12 and B1). Each lookup request carries at most 500 keys per family, so a full-table departure never holds `peerMu` or builds one IPC argument for the whole table (round-3 I-3) |
| Fail open | A lookup failure must never leave the owed withdrawal unsent with the stale route installed: AC-11 |
| Authority | Only the current owner's withdrawal, a departure of the owner's ended session, or bgp-rib's lifecycle purge of the owner's route can trigger a replacement; a non-owner still cannot change the client's route (AC-8, and AC-10 (b) for departure); a non-owner withdrawal for an owed key is only noted as a mover, it writes nothing and moves no epoch; a bgp-rib replay cannot reinstall the route being replaced (AC-20) |
| Peer-driven churn | A peer cannot postpone another peer's replacement by sending unrelated UPDATEs, nor by repeating withdrawals for the same prefix (AC-15): the fence is per key and a mover causes a retry only when it is the candidate about to be written, whose own withdrawal removes it from the next election (round-2 I-2) |

### Failure Routing

| Failure | Route To |
|---------|----------|
| Compilation error | Fix in the phase that introduced it |
| Test fails for the wrong reason | Fix the test assertion or setup |
| Test fails on behavior mismatch | Re-read the source in Current Behavior. If misunderstood → RESEARCH |
| Lint failure | Fix inline. If architectural → DESIGN |
| Functional test fails | Check the AC: wrong AC → DESIGN, correct AC → IMPLEMENT |
| Audit finds a missing AC | Back to the relevant phase and implement |
| 3 fix attempts failed | STOP. Report all 3 approaches. Ask the user |

## Design Insights
<!-- LIVE: write immediately when you learn something. Route each lesson to its
     governing surface under ai/rules/planning.md. A problem-class journal row
     is appropriate only when no surface governs the lesson yet; closure alone
     requires no lesson artifact. -->
- 2026-10-13: the skeleton's premise ("the fix needs a per-source path store in the relay") is superseded. bgp-rib, a hard dependency of bgp-rs, already holds every source's retained paths and already answers survivor-or-withdrawal for source departure; the store exists, the rail does not reach it for a per-prefix withdrawal.
- 2026-10-13: half the defect was closed by `b880c5a9a7` without anyone recording it: a non-owner withdrawal is already dropped. A test must pin the order B, A, A-withdraw, because A, B, A-withdraw is green at HEAD and would prove nothing.
- 2026-10-13: an untracked red probe named in a ruling was lost. A probe a spec depends on belongs in the spec's Evidence as a shape (done here), not only as a path.
- 2026-10-13 (design review round 1): "reuse the departure rail" was read as reuse of its thread and its fence, and both are wrong for a per-prefix trigger. The rail's admission is dispatched to the destination's own forward worker and awaited, so the writer cannot run it; and its fence retries on any traffic, which is tolerable for a rare session loss and a livelock for a per-prefix withdrawal. The reusable parts are the election, the transports and the admission item; the thread and the fence are replaced, for departure too.
- 2026-10-13 (round 1): the reviewer's per-key fence ("adj-out entry still holds the withdrawn owner and the survivor's path is current") has a hole when taken literally: while A's withdrawal is owed, the entry still names A, so the survivor's own later withdrawal is dropped as non-owner, and the entry check alone would then admit the dead survivor. Round 1 closed it by moving the epoch on EVERY forwarded write for the key, admitted or dropped; round 2 (I-2) showed that lets any peer postpone the replacement forever by repeating withdrawals, so the hole is now closed by noting each withdrawing owner as a mover and retrying only when the mover is the candidate about to be written (Data Flow step 7, `TestOwedKeyFenceInterleavings` cases a and e).
- 2026-10-13 (design review round 2): moving the departure off the destination-wide fence removed a guard nobody had named. The sequence fence was what stopped a departure from rewriting a destination whose route came from another source, and an old DOWN from overwriting a newer advertisement; `recoveryRouteLocked` checks the owner only on its withdrawal branch and the `adjOutRecovery` admission checks none for an announcement. When a fence is replaced, list every outcome the old fence prevented, not only the one it was built for: here the owner check at owe time and the same-source cut carry the two it carried silently (round 3 folded the cut into the owner check by storing the owner's session generation, N-6).
- 2026-10-13 (round 2): "empty answer" meant two different things (no ribOut entry, and owner moved) and the reactor would have had to guess. Every key now gets exactly one named outcome, and a missing one is an error, not a default.
- 2026-10-13 (design review round 3): "any announcement removes the owed record" read every announcement as a new owner, and one authority is not: a bgp-rib replay re-sends `ribOut[Destination]`, which still names the owner whose withdrawal the writer deferred, because a deferred withdrawal produces no sent event. A record rule keyed on "announcement or withdrawal" has to be keyed on authority instead, and every authority the writer admits needs its own row (Data Flow 7).
- 2026-10-13 (round 3): an assumption that "a failed X ends the session" had no producer behind it: the failure was logged and the session read on. Before a design treats a failure as the start of a cleanup, read the code that is supposed to start the cleanup.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 1): the red probe now lives at its final carrier, `reactor/rs_withdrawal_replacement_test.go`, beside `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalRecoversOnCachedRail` and `TestRSOwnerWithdrawalRecoversOnFastPath`, and is committed so it cannot be lost again. All three are red at HEAD `99228a5b3d` for the defect's reason: C's history for 10.9.0.0/24 is B, A, then one withdrawal, and B is never re-sent. A receiver wrapper reads `RawMessage.ReactorForwarded` on A's withdrawal body, so each test proves which rail carried it. The order A, B, A-withdraw stays green in `TestRSWithdrawalKeepsCurrentDestinationOwner`.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 2): the RIB's per-key contract landed without deleting `RecoveryRequest.Cut` or the sent-owner test, read against the spec's own rule that every guard is moved to its new carrier BEFORE the code it drives is deleted ("Guards of deleted code", round-4 I-3). Both now live in one place, the outcome's `Sent` list (the destination's advertisement not newer than the cut; on WITHDRAW only when an excluded source owns it, HEAD's rule, because `TestRFC8654FatalLengthReelectsAlternateBest`, tagged RFC8654-5-4, asserts the survivor is re-sent to a destination whose entry the survivor already owns, and changing an RFC-tagged assertion is AC-10's and the owner's call, not Phase 2's), and Phase 3 deletes the cut end to end together with `Sent` for non-ADD-PATH keys once the owner-generation check at owe time carries AC-18. Consequence for Phase 3: `TestRecoveryAddPathDepartureWithdrawsEverySentIdentifier` (the cut-less `SentAddPath` branch) is written there, not in Phase 2, and `rib.TestRecoveryNativeSentIdentityAndNewerOwner` keeps asserting the cut until then.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 2, R-12): the RIB half landed ahead of its producer. `bgptypes.RecoveryWriteMeta` is declared, the three sent-store sites run `projectRecoveryWriteLocked` for an event carrying it, and the lifecycle dispatcher (`recoveryRemovals`, started in `runRIBPlugin` before `p.Run`, stopped after it: cancel, join, drop) sends the queued removals through `updateRouteWithMetaContext`; `updateRouteWithMeta` keeps its behavior and now delegates with `context.Background()`. No sent event carries the key until Phase 3 relabels recovery sections `adjOutRecovery` and sets it, so Phase 3 owes the producer and `TestOwedKeyPurgeBetweenLookupAndAdmission`. "No newer revision" is read as no received path with the candidate's add-path mode and path identifier whose message ID is at least the candidate's (`recoveryCandidateGone`); a source with no received inventory at all counts as gone.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 2b first half, tests): `TestSessionDownFollowsBufferedDeliveries` now carries the remote-close case and subcases (1) to (8), and `fsm/entered_established_test.go` pins the entered-Established bit (`TestFSMEnteredEstablishedIsSticky`, `TestFSMEnteredEstablishedNeedsQueuedCallback`). Case (4) needed one new seam, `sessionEndHooks.beforeHandOff`, which `notifyMessageReceiver` runs after the route-server fast path and before the hand-off, with no lock held. The trap the handoff named is closed by parking the read loop. The pause gate sits before the read, so a loop blocked in its read would read once more. `parkReadLoop` sends one KEEPALIVE after `Pause` so that read happens and the loop then stops at the gate. A paused loop cannot fire Event 18 on a closed connection. Every leave arm records whether the loop was paused when it started, which proves the arm ran off the read goroutine in cases (1), (2), (6) and (8). Green ten times under `-race` (`./le job run label p2bdown3`). Red at the parent of 9886507d51, recorded with a `-overlay` that replaces only `peer_session_end.go` with the parent's ordering: the arm waits on the BFD subscriber through `stopBFDClient` and announces the down and appends the history row itself, and the down defer only closes `deliverChan` and joins the worker, firing no leave. This models the parent's ordering under the new seams, because the parent itself cannot compile the new tests. All ten subtests went red, each for its own reason. Remote close, hold timer and GR congestion: "down announced while UPDATEs were still held" (published 0). Contended teardown: "down arrived without the closing mark" (the down landed after runOnce had returned). Body-read error, paused cancel, read-goroutine panic and the Idle race: "timed out waiting for leave arm ran" (no leave arm and no down at all, the pre-existing defect). BFD: the same timeout, because the arm hung in `stopBFDClient` waiting on its own subscriber. Reactor stop: "downs = 0, want exactly 1". Case (8) was meant as the green-at-HEAD guard that the join completes when the forward worker drains the leave; it goes red here only because it also holds the deliveries.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 3 package a): the writer owes, the worker decides, and the three Phase 1 probes are green with no change to the RIB or the cut. `adjOutWrite.owesWithdrawal` stages a forwarded owner withdrawal or an `adjOutExpected` lifecycle removal of a source-owned key at a non-ADD-PATH destination when the session has a worker and `request bgp rib recovery` is registered; `applyOwes` applies once after the last pass (all-owed body, PATHS-LIMIT zero, and before `record`) and wakes the worker. A second owe of a key REPLACES its record instead of merging, so `recoveryWorker.settle` removes only the record its round read (pointer equality); epochs, movers and excluded sets are package (b). The worker (`reactor/recovery_owed.go`) reuses the lookup and admission halves split out of `relayRecovery` (`lookupRecovery`, `admitRecovery`) and sets each outcome's `Sent` to the record's NLRI, because the RIB's cut-based `Sent` is empty for a key with no excluded source; package (c) deletes that `Sent` with the cut. Stop lives in `Session.seal`, so every teardown path stops the worker; the join is in `runOnce`'s first-registered defer, after the down defer. Three live ownership tests (`TestRSReturnedWithdrawalPreservesOtherSource`, `TestRSMixedWithdrawalPreservesAnnouncementSibling`, `TestRSWithdrawalKeepsCurrentDestinationOwner`) now wait for the owner withdrawal through `ownershipLiveDecided`: their end state is unchanged, but the withdrawal is now the election's and may follow the causal marker. Red recorded with a `-overlay` that makes `owesWithdrawal` return false: the three probes, `TestAdjOutOwnerWithdrawalOwedToRecovery` and `TestOwedKeyHandOffNeverBlocksWriter` fail. `TestRFC8654FatalLengthReelectsAlternateBest` still passes unchanged, so its AC-10 change belongs to the package that changes departure and the cut (c). The unit tests live in `reactor/recovery_owed_worker_test.go`, not the `session_ownership_test.go` the TDD plan names.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 3 package b): the destination-wide sequence fence (`recoverySnapshot`, `relayRecovery`, `sentUpdateSequence`, `noteFilteredOperation`) is deleted; one owed record per key carries excluded sources, epoch, movers and a done channel, the admission decides per key, and a departure owes only where its ended session owns the entry. Every unit of "Guards of deleted code" in the reactor and process packages is migrated (the `rib`/`rs`/`update` rows wait on package c, which deletes the cut). Readings taken: the departure command now returns once its records complete, so a hard lookup or writer failure retires the destination but is no longer the command's error (`TestRecoveryHardFailureRetiresDestination`, `TestRecoveryWriterFailureOverridesPartialRetry` assert the retirement and the admission's error instead); a record of a session retired without a write failure completes only at `runOnce`'s table reset. Stopped-source exclusion: `drainRecoverySources` returns the sources whose received publication failed, and `decide` excludes them from every key of the round (the B-1 behaviour change of `TestRecoveryWaitsForFilteredReceivedPublication`). `recoveryKeyNothing` is deleted: a decision with no section settles as `recoveryKeyWrite`. `TestRFC8654FatalLengthReelectsAlternateBest` (RFC8654-5-4) went red here, not in package c, because the recipient's entry is owned by the alternate, so nothing is owed: it now asserts AC-10 (nothing re-sent to a recipient holding the alternate), claim narrowed to match, discrimination re-recorded (owner approval under session 3f5fcb0a, Q-4). Red proofs, each by `-overlay`: dropping the mover check reddens `TestRecoveryRefusesFilteredCandidateWithdrawalAfterLookup` and fence (a); dropping both generation fences reddens fence (j); dropping the receipt check in `queueRecovery` reddens `TestRecoveryLookupRejectsReconnectedSource`; owing every key reddens `newer-target-owner` (reactor and process) and `current-session-keeps-its-route`; dropping the ADD-PATH writer refusal reddens the ADD-PATH departure subtest; dropping the stopped-source exclusion reddens the publication worker-failure half. Fence (j) needed both fences broken: `fwdBatchHandler`'s per-section source check is a second, independent layer.
- 2026-10-18 (Phase 3 package c, first half): the cut is deleted end to end: `RecoveryRequest.Cut`, the IPC `<cut>` argument (`recoveryCommand` now takes five fixed arguments), `types.NLRIBatch.RecoveryCut`, the `recovery-cut` meta and its parser (`recoveryFromMeta` returns the source only), and the rs `cut` parameter through `handleStateDown`, `sendPeerDown`, `sendBatchedWithdrawals` and `recoverRouteSel` (`seenMsgID` stays for the replay). `Sent` for non-ADD-PATH keys is deleted with it (`recoverySentLocked`): the reactor already wrote from the owed record's NLRI. AC-18's guard is carried by the writer's generation check alone; proven by `-overlay` that makes `departedOwner` return true and drops `allow`'s ADD-PATH `forwardSourceCurrent` refusal: `TestAdjOutDepartureOwesOnlyDepartedSession/current-session-keeps-its-route` and `/add-path-reconnect-keeps-newer-identifier` go red. Guard rows moved: `rib.TestRecoveryNativeSentIdentityAndNewerOwner` is renamed `TestRecoveryAddPathDepartureWithdrawsEverySentIdentifier` (the owed TDD name) and asserts identifiers 0, 7 and 99; `TestRecoveryBatchedIdentitiesShareOneElectionResult` asserts the same per prefix; `TestRecoverySelectsSurvivorWithItsOwnAttributes` and the LLGR DOWN handoff assert an empty `Sent`; `TestRecoveryLabeledTextPrefixMatchesNativeSentStack` asserts the text key's identity at `recoveryIdentity`, where it is still read; `update.TestRecoveryMetadataPreservesCutAndRejectsMalformedOwnership` is renamed `TestRecoveryMetadataRejectsMalformedOwnership`. R-12 producer: NOT landed. Written and withdrawn: in `forwardUpdateSection`'s `srcInfo.recovery != nil` branch, `meta := make(map[string]any, len(item.meta)+1); maps.Copy(meta, item.meta); meta[bgptypes.RecoveryWriteMeta] = true; item.meta = meta`, with an `OnMessageSent` hook on `replacementRailObserver` asserting C's sent announcements of 10.9.0.0/24 carry the marks false, false, true (red before, green after, all three probes). But with it, `process.TestRecoveryQueuedDeliveryTransport/direct/unrelated-prefix/held-sent` failed 3 of 25 (`-race -count=25`: "surviving target was withdrawn before replacement"), and 0 of 25 with the producer removed by `-overlay`: the RIB's R-12 check (`projectRecoveryWriteLocked` / `recoveryCandidateGone`) misjudges a live survivor as gone in the held-sent schedule, or the mark reaches a write it should not. Root cause not investigated. Found, not changed: `RecoveryRequest.PrefixOnly` has no producer since package (a); Phase 3b's framing work owns it. Owed in this package: the producer after that cause is fixed, then `TestOwedKeyPurgeBetweenLookupAndAdmission` (needs a real-RIB purge of the chosen survivor between lookup and admission) and the rib-storage-design sentence that says the key is set.
- 2026-10-13 (round 3): the cleanest simplification came from data the writer already held. The departure cut existed because `adjOutSameOwner` ignores the source's session generation, but nothing stopped the table from STORING the generation while equality ignores it; one field removed an outcome, an IPC field, a meta key and an assumption.

## Key Design Decisions
<!-- "Chose X over Y because Z." The rejected alternative is the valuable half. -->
| Decision | Alternatives Considered | Rationale |
|----------|------------------------|-----------|
| Recommended: Approach B, the final writer defers an RS owner withdrawal on a non-ADD-PATH destination to the existing recovery election (round 2: as a key with an empty excluded-source set, not a mode) | A: per-source path store inside bgp-rs (the skeleton's wording). C: bgp-rs strips every withdrawal from forwarding and submits it through `recoverRouteSel`; the fast path stops forwarding withdrawals. D: bgp-rs subscribes to bgp-rib best-change events and re-announces the RIB best | B decides at the one point every rail shares and only for the owner case, so non-owner withdrawals keep costing nothing and the fast path needs no new exclusion. A duplicates bgp-rib's Adj-RIB-In and violates "no second route inventory". C puts a RIB round trip on EVERY withdrawal (owner or not) and needs a fast-path change. D races the forward-all writes (best-change and forward are unordered); its other objection, that it turns RS into best-path selection for announcements, no longer holds since the owner's Q-3 answer asks for exactly that, which this spec does through B's own writer and election (row "Announcements" below) |
| Survivor = the RIB's `SelectBest` over retained candidates | "Latest remaining advertisement" | The RIB records no advertisement order across sources; departure recovery already uses `SelectBest` and the guide documents it. Owner confirms in Q-1 |
| One lookup, and each key carries its excluded-source set (empty for an owner withdrawal or a lifecycle removal, the departed sources for a departure, unioned on merge) (round-2 I-3; round 3 removed the per-source cuts, N-6) | A mode field on `RecoveryRequest` (round 1's design); a separate lookup for withdrawals | One producer, one election. A mode had no defined value for a key owed by a withdrawal and then merged with a departure, and several departed sources had no per-key home; the empty set IS the withdrawal case, so the mode was redundant and R-6's misrouting risk disappears with it |
| No config knob | `rs replacement on-withdrawal enable` leaf | A correct-by-default behaviour needs no switch; a knob would leave the RFC 4271 Section 9.2 defect reachable |
| Recovery on a per-destination recovery worker fed by a non-blocking hand-off into a coalescing owed set (round-1 B1) | Run recovery on the destination forward worker after `writeMu` release (the previous design); run it on the source read goroutine; one reactor-wide recovery goroutine | The forward worker would wait on its own admission (`relayRecovery` dispatches to it and blocks on `done`); the read goroutine would wait for its own message's publication (`drainRecoverySources`); one shared goroutine lets a slow destination's admission stall every other destination's recovery |
| Per-key epoch fence, for departure and withdrawal alike (round-1 B2) | Keep the destination-wide send sequence and whole-source publication cut (livelocks under churn); fence owner withdrawals only and leave departure on the old fence (AC-12 covers session loss, and two fences is layering) | A key is retried only on a real change of that key, which is the strongest liveness a lookup outside `writeMu` can have |
| The writer's owed record is the only authority on "the owner moved": an originated announcement installs a new owner and removes it, a forwarded or initial one merges into it as a candidate of the election (owner Q-3; round 11, N-1), and the admission writes nothing for a missing record (round-2 I-3, replacing round 1's RIB-side check); a bgp-rib replay (`adjOutExpected`) installs no new owner and never removes it (round-3 B-1) | `SourceMessageID <= Cut` across sources (round-1 I1: one process-wide counter, so ID order is not writer order); a RIB check of `ribOut[Destination]` owner beside the writer's record (round 1's answer, a second declaration of one fact); "any announcement removes the record" (round 2's rule, which let a ROUTE-REFRESH replay reinstall A's withdrawn route for good) | The writer sees every forwarded write under the table mutex; the RIB sees them only after a drain. A replay re-sends `ribOut[Destination]`, which still names the owner whose withdrawal is owed, so only an originated write, which no election overrides, can mean "the owner moved" |
| The departure's owner test reads the owner's session generation stored in `adjOutPath`: owed only where the departed source owns the key through a session `forwardSourceCurrent` no longer accepts (round-3 N-6) | Round 2's same-source cut with the RIB's KEEP outcome; the reviewer's N-6 wording, "owe only where the entry's owner is the departed SESSION" (generation equality) | Simpler and exact: the generation is read under the table mutex every write takes, where the cut was a RIB-side reading after a drain that needed A-7. It removes the KEEP outcome, the per-source cuts in the excluded-source set and the IPC, `recovery-cut` end to end, A-7 and the Boundary row. "Not current" is used instead of generation equality for two reasons: the departure command names no generation (bgp-rs and bgp-rr learn of the down from a state event), and equality would miss a GR-retained route from an EARLIER session that the reconnected session never re-announced, which must still be decided when the later session departs. `adjOutSameOwner` keeps ignoring the generation, so a retained path stays withdrawable by the same source on its new session |
| A bgp-rib sent-lifecycle removal at a non-ADD-PATH destination is owed like an owner withdrawal, with an empty excluded-source set (round-3 I-2) | (b) of the reviewer's I-2: record the GR purge as a Known Limitation and narrow AC-12 to non-retained departures; exclude the purged source in the record | The owner required replacement unbounded for session loss (AC-12), and narrowing it is his decision, not a design choice (main-thread ruling for round 3). The purge is the only way a GR-capable source that never returns stops offering its routes, so leaving it bare reproduces the RFC 4271 Section 9.2 defect for every GR peer. The set is empty because the RIB removes the received paths before it dispatches the removal (A-9); excluding the source would also skip its fresh paths on the new session |
| A receive-publication failure tears the source session down with Cease / Out of Resources (round-3 I-1), and the round that meets it excludes the source from every key (round-5 B-1) | Treat the failed source as drained with no teardown (round 2: elects over a RIB that never hears the source again, and no departure comes); treat it as drained WITH the teardown (round 3: the round still elects the source's withdrawn path while the FSM keeps it Established, round-5 B-1); retry that source's drain (a hot loop, `failed` is sticky); retire the destination (today's answer: punishes a peer for another peer's fault) | RFC 4271 Section 6.7 lets a speaker close a connection with Cease at any time. The subcode is Ze's precedent, not RFC 4486's letter (round-4 N-4): Section 4's Out of Resources text is "runs out of resources (e.g., memory)", and a recovered panic is an internal fault, but RFC 4486 Section 3 has no subcode closer to it, and the reactor already sends Out of Resources for the same class of internal failure (`relay_recovery.go` recovery hard failure, `forward_pool_congestion.go`). Cost: RFC 4486 Section 4 RECOMMENDS that the receiver damp its retries after Out of Resources, so X reconnects more slowly; accepted, because the session cannot be served until it restarts anyway. For a GR-capable X no GR retention may follow (RFC 4724 Section 4 when the Cease reached the wire; X's Adj-RIB-In is known-invalid either way), so D1's reason producer reads the "Adj-RIB-In invalid" fact, `receivePublication.failed`, beside `notificationExchanged`, after `<-deliveryDone` so that a late failure is seen too, in a `runOnce` defer that also joins the leave callback so the down follows the source's leaving Established, and that also runs on a read-goroutine panic that leaves no lock held (round 9, I-3) (round 7, I-2; round 8, B-1 and I-3; rejected: (a) scoping "no retention" to failures seen before the session leaves Established and releasing a late failure's retained families afterwards, a second path that undoes a decision instead of making it once), because an unsent Cease leaves that flag false (`recordNotificationUnsent`; round-5 I-2, which replaces round 4's reliance on D1 alone). The fact reports its own declared reason, `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid`, not "notification", which would be false on the operator surfaces when no NOTIFICATION crossed the wire, and GR asks one declared predicate, `rpc.ReasonForbidsRetention`, instead of comparing literals (round-6 I-2; rejected: reporting "notification" for both, false; a second literal in `gr.go`, a hand list beside the constants). Recording the unsent-Cease case as a gap was the reviewer's other option and was not taken: it leaves a known-invalid Adj-RIB-In electable. The teardown is its own defect-fix commit (Phase 2b). Within the round that meets the failure, X is excluded from every key (round-5 B-1), so the teardown's speed decides nothing; destination retirement, today's answer, is not kept. The teardown targets the session the delivery worker serves, so a later session is never ended, and it must not wait for `deliveryDone`, which the worker itself closes (A-11) |
| A departure owes a destination a key only where the departed source is the destination's current owner, read under the table mutex (round-2 B-1) | Today's behaviour: re-elect at every selected destination | Re-electing where another source owns the key overrides forward-all on an unrelated departure and, without the deleted destination-wide sequence fence, would let an old DOWN overwrite a newer advertisement |
| Each recovery worker drains for its own round; no shared reactor-wide drain (round-2 I-1) | One shared drain serving every waiting worker (round-1 N1) | The shared drain had no owner, lifetime or failure mapping: a leading waiter's cancellation, or one source's stopped publication, would fail it for every follower and retire every waiting client. The owed set already coalesces per round, and no measurement shows a cost to remove (`ai/rules/simplicity.md`); a measured regression is what would re-open it |
| The answer is an ordered survivor list and the reactor steps past egress-denied candidates (round-1 I2) | Withdraw on the first denial (today's departure behaviour); pass the destination's egress policy into the RIB | Withdrawing while a permitted survivor exists is the defect this spec fixes; egress policy belongs to the reactor's forward rail, which already applies it per destination |
| No RIB owner: the writer asks the command registry whether `request bgp rib recovery` is registered, through `Registry().Lookup`, the same lookup `recoverNLRIBatch` makes today, and reads "no owner" from the same two conditions, `command == nil` or `command.Process == nil` (round-3 I-6, round-4 N-6). Not registered: an owner withdrawal is written as today, with one log line per destination; a departure retires its destinations as today (N-7). Registered when the key was owed and gone by lookup time: the destination is retired (AC-11) | Read `ribevents.RecoveryProvider()` (nil whenever bgp-rib runs out of process, so it cannot mean "no owner"); a reactor atomic set and cleared at registration (round 2's choice: a second declaration of a fact the registry holds, `ai/rules/principles.md`, and placing it would teach the generic `plugin/server.CommandRegistry` a BGP command name or add an unnamed callback seam); fail closed and retire whenever no owner is registered | After `Freeze`, `Lookup` is one map read on an immutable snapshot loaded atomically, and `Register`, `Unregister` and `unregisterAll` republish that snapshot (`republishFrozen`), so it tracks bgp-rib's registration in process and over IPC. Round 2's reason for the atomic, "Lookup takes the registry read lock under writeMu", holds only before `Freeze`, and even then no holder of the registry lock takes a session `writeMu`, so there is no lock-order hazard (Phase 3 confirms by reading `Register`, `Unregister` and `unregisterAll`). With no selecting RIB, no process retains a route to elect, so there is no replacement and RFC 4271 Section 9.2 owes the withdrawal; failing closed would retire every client of an external forwarder that runs without bgp-rib. For bgp-rs and bgp-rr no owner is a fault window (bgp-rib restarting), so writing the bare withdrawal there is the defect for that window: the log line makes it visible rather than silent (`ai/rules/principles.md`) |
| Announcements (owner, Q-3): an announcement of a key the non-ADD-PATH destination already holds or owes is owed to the same recovery election; the first route of a key, and an uncontested owner's re-announcement, are written directly (round 11, I-3), and every owner withdrawal or lifecycle removal is owed whatever the contested bit says (round 12, I-1); an ANNOUNCE mover re-owes the key whatever the decision wrote (round 11, B-2); the admission's own survivor write carries `adjOutRecovery` (round 11, B-1) | (i) Owe EVERY announcement at a non-ADD-PATH destination; (ii) D, bgp-rs subscribes to bgp-rib best-change events and announces the RIB best; (iii) a per-destination, per-key inventory of candidate identities so the writer could tell a sole source's re-announcement from a contested one; (iv) on an ANNOUNCE mover, retry without writing, as for a WITHDRAW mover | One writer rule, one election, one fence: the owed set, the worker, the drain, the lookup and the admission are the withdrawal design's, so announcements add a branch in `allow` (and its `adjOutRecovery` announcement branch), a mover kind, the current-announcer set, a received message ID in `adjOutPath`, the contested bit with its tombstone, and one outcome flag. (i) costs a round for the first route of every prefix, the full-table initial sync included, where no other candidate has reached the destination, so the writer can only be wrong transiently and the next arrival corrects it (A-14). (ii) races the forward rails (best-change and forward are unordered, the reason D was rejected for withdrawals) and leaves the writer installing the newest arrival meanwhile. (iii) is a second route inventory (`rib-storage-design.md`) and costs memory proportional to prefixes, sources and clients. What (iii) would have bought, telling a sole source's re-announcement from a contested one, the CONTESTED BIT buys instead (round 11, I-3, adopted): one sticky bool on the existing per-key `adjRIBOutRoute`, set when a second identity meets the entry or a record, so the writer knows "contested", never which candidates; a sole source's re-announcement stays zero-copy, with no round, while every owner withdrawal and lifecycle removal is owed whatever the bit says, because a clear bit cannot see a candidate still in flight to the writer (round 12, I-1), and the tombstone left by any deletion of a source-owned entry outside a recovery decision closes A-14's two exits (round 11, I-4; round 12, I-1). (iv) lets a source re-announcing the owed prefix postpone the decision forever (R-9) |
| bgp-rr in this spec (round-1 I4) | A journal row and a follow-up spec | Owner reading of Q-1 ("anything else in unacceptable", recorded under Q-2); the writer change reaches bgp-rr's cached forwards anyway, so leaving rr's departure on plain unowned withdrawals would ship the fix half-applied to one plugin |

### Triple challenge

| Challenge | Answer |
|-----------|--------|
| Simplicity | Minimum change that reaches every rail: one writer outcome, one per-key excluded-source set in place of the batch source (no mode field), one election, one worker per destination that both triggers share, each draining for itself (round 2 removed the shared drain and the RIB-side owner check as machinery and a second declaration). Round 3 removed the per-source cut, the KEEP outcome, `recovery-cut` and A-7 by storing the owner's session generation the writer already holds (N-6), and the second recovery-owner atomic by reading the frozen registry (I-6). No route is stored: the owed set holds keys awaiting a decision and empties as they are decided. Simpler C was rejected because it costs every withdrawal a RIB round trip and needs a fast-path change. The worker and the per-key fence are not optional machinery: round 1 showed the simpler "run it after the write on the same worker" deadlocks and the reused fence livelocks |
| Uniformity | Departure and owner withdrawal share one lookup, one per-key fence and one fail-closed policy; the operator gets one rule for "a source stops offering a route" |
| Performance | The first route of a key at a non-ADD-PATH destination, a sole (uncontested) source's re-announcement of its own prefix (round 11, I-3: the contested bit, so the dominant IXP churn pays no drain, no `DrainEventsApplied` barrier, no lookup and no copy; its WITHDRAWAL pays one round, round 12, I-1, R-1), every write to an ADD-PATH destination and every non-owner withdrawal keep the zero-copy write; an announcement of a CONTESTED key a non-ADD-PATH destination already holds takes the cold recovery path, costed in R-1 (owner, Q-3). The bit adds one bool read per non-ADD-PATH source-bound write under the table mutex `allow` already holds, and no allocation. Every forwarded write gains one check that the destination's owed set is empty, and only while it is non-empty a per-NLRI lookup in it (an announcement may remove a record, a withdrawal may note a mover) (round-2 N-9); the writer's added cost for an owed key is one map insert, one key copy and one non-blocking wake. Owner withdrawals to non-ADD-PATH clients take the cold recovery path off the writer: copies of the survivor's NLRI and attributes (`recoveryCandidate`), one drain per destination per round, one lookup per family per destination per round. This is the R-1 cost the owner accepted (Q-1) |

### Owner questions

| ID | Question | Recommended answer | If answered otherwise |
|----|----------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| Q-1 | Approach B costs a publication drain, a RIB applied-event drain and a lookup for every withdrawal the destination's current owner sends to a non-ADD-PATH client, including the common case where no other source exists and the answer is a plain withdrawal. A recovery hard failure resets that client session, as departure recovery does today. Is that cost and that fail-closed policy acceptable, and is the RIB-elected best the survivor you want? | Yes to all three | C or D changes Phases 3 to 5; "latest remaining" needs per-source ordering the RIB lacks (larger spec). **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-13: "RFC 4271 §9.2 requires the replacement: yes anything else in unacceptable". Approach B and its cost are accepted** |
| Q-2 | Other cached forwarders reach the same writer. bgp-rr may share the shape: nothing under `plugins/rr` uses `recovery-source` or `recoverRouteSel`, but its withdrawal behaviour was NOT traced at its producer in this design pass. Widen this spec to it (after tracing), or journal it? | Superseded (round-2 N-3): the design-time recommendation was "keep this spec to bgp-rs; journal row for bgp-rr"; the owner's Q-1 answer put bgp-rr in scope, so no recommendation stands | Widening adds RR ACs, RR `.ci` and RFC 4456 reading. **Reading taken from the owner's answer to Q-1 ("anything else in unacceptable"), not a separate answer, and confirmed by the main thread for design review round 1: bgp-rr is IN scope. Traced at the producer in round 1 (Design-time re-check, bgp-rr row): it has the same per-prefix gap and a worse departure gap, so AC-10, `TestRROwnerWithdrawalRecovers`, `TestRRDepartureRecoversThroughOwedSet`, `bgp-rr-withdrawal-replacement.ci` and Phase 4 carry it; RFC 4456 Section 6 read. The owner may still narrow this** |
| Q-3 | RFC 7947 Section 2.1's SHOULD (row RFC7947-x-5, "SHOULD perform one or more BGP Decision Processes"): does this spec claim it? | - | **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-13: "I believe the wording is unfortunate and designed for human. The Should may mean that you may have done already the decision process in advance, therefore you may have cached the result, but we need to give the right route replacement otherwise the routing behavior is wrong." then "a".** Applied: the spec extends to announcements (a non-ADD-PATH client is sent the RIB-elected route on each announcement, an ADD-PATH client every path, bgp-rs and bgp-rr; Task, Data Flow 3a, AC-22 to AC-26) and claims RFC7947-x-5 once delivered (TDD plan, `rfc7947_rs_decision_test.go`) |
| Q-4 | The two existing 9.2-7 carriers in `rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go` claim an advertisement but assert a RIB event. What happens to their tags? This changes RFC-tagged tests | - | **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-13: "approve all RFC-tagged test changes", then "Can you change the two tests to be normal tests instead?" and "yes".** Applied: `TestRIBBestChangeWithdraw` and `TestRIBBestChangeNoPublishSameBest` lose every RFC tag and stay as plain unit tests; 9.2-7 in both halves is carried by the new wire carriers, the 9.2-8 negative half by the new `TestRFC4271RouteServerStillFeasibleRouteIsNotWithdrawn`, and the 9-3 halves and the 9.1.2-2 negative stay carried by `rfc4271_receive_decision_test.go`, re-confirmed at implementation (TDD plan, "RFC-tagged tests that change", and the approval commands) |
| Q-5 | Round 3 (I-2). bgp-rr's source departure ignores Graceful Restart today: no file under `plugins/rr` calls `retention.`, so a GR-capable source's routes are withdrawn from RR clients the moment its session drops, while bgp-rs keeps them (`retention.Family`, `rs/server_handlers.go`). Moving rr's departure onto recovery forces the choice. Should bgp-rr honour retention as bgp-rs does: retained families are not withdrawn at departure, and leave at restart-time expiry, End-of-RIB or `release-routes` through the RIB's purge, which now carries the survivor (AC-19)? | Yes, honour retention (the design above is written under this answer: Data Flow 3, Integration Points `rr/rr.go`, AC-10 (c), AC-19, `TestRRDepartureHonoursRetention`). Reason: RFC 4724 Section 4.2 says the receiving speaker "MUST retain the routes received from the peer" and "MUST NOT differentiate between stale and other routing information during forwarding"; withdrawing retained routes from clients defeats the retention the RR's own RIB performs, and bgp-rs already behaves this way. This changes what RR clients see during a source restart: they keep the source's routes until the purge, instead of losing them at once | **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-13: "yes" (bgp-rr honours GR retention like bgp-rs).** The design is written under it |
| Q-6 | Round 6 (I-2), precedent Q-6 of `plan/immediate/spec-bgp-graceful-restart-rfc-defects.md`. When a receive-publication failure ends a session and the Cease never reached the wire, the down reason becomes the new declared constant `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid`, in state events, `show` FSM history and the session-closed log line, in place of "session closed" / "connection lost". Accept the spelling "adj-rib-in invalid"? | Yes. Reason: it names the fact the operator acts on (the session's received routes are known-invalid and are not retained), it keeps the space-separated style of "session closed" and "connection lost", and it does not claim a NOTIFICATION that was never delivered. When the Cease was delivered the reason stays "notification" (D1) | **Answered by the owner, 2026-10-13: "yes" (close reason "adj-rib-in invalid", `rpc.ReasonAdjRIBInInvalid`; GR does not retain).** The design is written under it |

## Known Limitations
<!-- Deliberate scope boundaries. Anything here that is actually outstanding work
     is not a limitation: write it as its own spec, in the bucket that item
     belongs to, and name that spec here (ai/rules/planning.md). -->
- The "newly installed routes" half of the 9.2-7 sentence is exercised by the x-5 positive carrier and claimed under x-5 only (TDD plan).
- A non-ADD-PATH client may hold a route one revision old for one round when its source re-announces the owed prefix between a round's snapshot and its admission (Data Flow 7, ANNOUNCE mover); this is the price of never letting a re-announcing source postpone the decision (R-9), and AC-26 (b) proves the next round corrects it.
- bgp-rr is in scope (Q-2); its `handleStateDown` starts one goroutine per down event (`go func()` in `routeReflector.handleStateDown`), as bgp-rs does (`go rs.sendPeerDown`, tracked by `routeServer.downWG`). Decided now (round-2 N-8): this is not a permitted shape under `ai/rules/goroutine-lifecycle.md` (it is per event, not a one-time lifecycle step), it predates this spec, and this spec's goal holds without changing it, because the goroutine only sends the departure command and the command's wait is what this spec changes. So it is a defect walked into: Phase 4 writes one row in a new `plan/journal/goroutine-started-per-event.md` (no existing class names it) covering both plugins, and this spec does not change the goroutine.

One sub-claim of B1 not reproduced: "fwdPool.Dispatch blocks bgp-rs" (no non-test caller of `Dispatch`; the forward rails use `TryDispatch` then the unbounded `dispatchOverflow`); the finding stands on its other two sub-claims. ## RFC Documentation (Scope: protocol)

Add `// RFC NNNN Section X.Y: "<quoted requirement>"` above enforcing code.
MUST document: validation rules, error conditions, state transitions, timer
constraints, message ordering, and every MUST/MUST NOT.

## Checklist

### Pre-Spec Verification (before the design is presented)
- [ ] Metadata table present, with a valid Status, Depends, Phase and Updated
- [ ] `ai/INDEX.md` keyword table checked
- [ ] An `rfc/short/` summary exists for every RFC referenced
- [ ] Template format followed: the 🧪 emoji, tables rather than prose, `[ ]` never `[x]`
- [ ] No code snippets
- [ ] Files to Modify names feature code, not only tests
- [ ] Current Behavior and Data Flow sections completed
- [ ] AC-N rows carry testable assertions
- [ ] Every assumption has a Basis and a validation method; every failure mode is a risk row
- [ ] Required Reading carries `→ Decision:` / `→ Constraint:` checkpoints
- [ ] Integration Checklist marks "CLI grammar" when a command is added, "Doctor check" when a runtime dependency is

### Goal Gates (MUST pass)
- [ ] AC-1..AC-N all demonstrated
- [ ] Every user story has a working path and a passing test (N-A when Scope is tooling or docs, which delete that section)
- [ ] Wiring Test table complete: every row a concrete test name, none deferred
- [ ] `./le verify worktree` passes. It runs every stage against a COMMIT in a throwaway worktree, which is the pre-commit gate (`ai/rules/git-safety.md`). An in-place `./le verify current` is void the moment the tree moves under it
- [ ] Feature code integrated (`internal/*`, `cmd/*`), not library-only
- [ ] Integration and Documentation checklists answered Yes/No/N-A with evidence
- [ ] Architectural Verification table filled, including registration over hardcoding
- [ ] Critical Review passes, and `ai/rules/quality.md` is satisfied: lint fixed rather than disabled, the focused check for the changed behavior run once with its OUTPUT PASTED, and any red named with the one-line reason it is scaffolding
- [ ] Every A-N confirmed or broken, none `unvalidated`
- [ ] Every item this spec did not do is a spec of its own, named here, in its own bucket

### TDD
- [ ] Tests written
- [ ] Tests FAIL (paste output)
- [ ] Tests PASS (paste output)
- [ ] Boundary tests for all numeric inputs (or N-A when the feature takes none)
- [ ] Functional `.ci` tests for end-to-end behavior
- [ ] Interop tests for protocol features (or N-A with a reason)

### Closure
- [ ] Append `plan/TEMPLATE-CLOSURE.md` and complete every section in it
- [ ] `/ze-review` gate clean, recorded via `internal/le/spec/review.go`
- [ ] Any lesson routed to its governing surface under `ai/rules/planning.md`; no lesson artifact created merely for closure
- [ ] **Commit A:** code + tests + docs + edited spec + any journal rows owed by the work
- [ ] **Commit B:** `remove <the spec's path in its bucket>` only, in the same `./le commit create` script (commit A preserves the spec in history)
